\hypertarget{Editor-HardKeys-2.4}{%
\subsection{Editor HardKeys}\label{Editor-HardKeys-2.4}}

Editor Controls are used to program and edit parameter values for all fixtures as well as to create and edit objects such as cues and libraries.

\textbf{Editor}

• 1 Dedicated dimmer wheel

• 4 Main encoder push wheels

• 4 Extra encoder push wheels

• 1 Trackball

• \textbf{{[}Next{]} / {[}Prev{]}} Keys

• XYZ lock keys

• \textbf{{[}Res{]}} Wheel resolution key

• Numeric Key Pad

• \textbf{{[}Vibe{]}} Key (Shift button)

• Editor Command keys

• Object Select keys

\hypertarget{Master-Controls-2.5}{%
\subsection{Master Controls}\label{Master-Controls-2.5}}

Master Controls are used to trigger the Controller assigned as the Master and to limit the intensity of all the fixtures.

\textbf{Grand Master and BlackOut}

• \textbf{{[}Grand Master{]}} Slider.

• \textbf{{[}BO{]}} Blackout key.

\textbf{A/B and Master Keys}

• \textbf{{[}GO/BACK/HOLD{]}} keys for executing cues on the Master Controller.

• \textbf{A/B CrossFader} non-motorized pair of sliders for executing Qlists and Cues manually.

\hypertarget{Playback-Controls-2.6}{%
\subsection{Playback Controls}\label{Playback-Controls-2.6}}

Playback Controls are used to trigger the Objects assigned to the controller.

\textbf{Playback}

• Individually paging \textbf{Slider Set Controllers} with motorized fader and three buttons for for the execution and control of Qlists, Cues, Scenes, Group Masters (Submasters), Rate Masters, Flash Masters.

• Individually paging \textbf{Qkey Controllers} with pressable knob or single button for the execution of Qlists, Cues, Scenes, Groups selection, Libraries, Snaps, Macros, Rate.

• Individually paging \textbf{Auxiliary Key Controllers} with single button for the execution of Qlists, Cues, Scenes, Groups selection, Libraries, Snaps, Macros.

• Non-paging \textbf{Global Slider Controllers} with non-motorized fader and single button for the execution and control of Qlists, Cues, Scenes, Group Masters (Submasters), Rate Masters, Flash Masters.

\hypertarget{Controller-Keys-2.7}{%
\subsection{Controller Keys}\label{Controller-Keys-2.7}}

Controller Keys are used to select and trigger the controllers.
These keys are divided into: Operation Keys. Command Keys, Mode Keys.

\textbf{{[}Operation Keys{]}} manage the assignments to the controllers.

\textbf{Operation Keys}

• \textbf{{[}SELECT{]}} key to assign the Master Controller.

• \textbf{{[}LOAD{]}} key to pre-load a controller for execution.

• \textbf{{[}RELEASE{]}} Key to release a target controller.

• \textbf{{[}FREE{]}} Key to empty a target controller.

• \textbf{{[}SNAP GO{]}} Key to trigger a Snap.

• \textbf{{[}PROTECT{]}} Key to lock the desk with a password.

\textbf{{[}Command Keys{]}} provide additional functions to the controllers.

\textbf{Command Keys}

• \textbf{{[}FLASH{]}} makes the target controller flash.

• \textbf{{[}SOLO{]}} releases the other controllers, allowing only one controller to be triggered at once.

• \textbf{{[}GO RELEASE{]}} makes a Go and Release command to the target controller.

• \textbf{{[}ON{]}} activates the target controller.

• \textbf{{[}OFF{]}} deactivates the target controller.

• \textbf{{[}LATCH{]}} (not implemented yet).

• \textbf{{[}GO{]}} makes a Go command to the target controller.

• \textbf{{[}HOLD BACK{]}} makes a Hold and Back command to the target controller.

• \textbf{{[}\textless---\textgreater{]}} reverses the order of the Cues on the target controller.

• \textbf{{[}\textgreater\textgreater\textgreater{]}} makes a Step forward to the target controller (Go in Cut time).

• \textbf{{[}\textless\textless\textless{]}} makes a Step backward to the target controller (Back in Cut time).

• \textbf{{[}ASSERT{]}} makes a Go command to the current Cue on the target controller.

• \textbf{{[}RATE{]}} for overall or individual rate override of the controllers.

• \textbf{{[}TEACH{]}} for assigning the BPM tap time of the controllers.

\textbf{{[}Mode Keys{]}} manage the function operated by the controllers.

\textbf{Mode Keys}

• \textbf{{[}FADERS{]}} Controllers will trigger the assigned objects.

• \textbf{{[}RATE{]}} Controllers will trigger the Rate of the running chasers or effects, if present.

• \textbf{{[}SIZE{]}} Controllers will trigger the Size of the running effects, if present.

• \textbf{{[}CHANNEL{]}} Controllers will trigger DMX addresses, patched 1 to 1. (not implemented yet)

• \textbf{{[}PROTECT{]}} Key to lock the desk with a password.

\hypertarget{Page-Keys-2.8}{%
\subsection{Page Keys}\label{Page-Keys-2.8}}

Page Keys are used to quickly move between the pages.

\textbf{Page Keys}

• \textbf{{[}VIEW{]}} Accesses the Pages View.

• \textbf{{[}PATCH{]}} switches between Patch Pages.

• \textbf{{[}PROGRAM{]}} switches between Program Pages.

• \textbf{{[}PLAYBACK{]}} switches between Playback Pages.

Vibe Pages can be also switched from the screen's Toolbar by tapping the Home Page buttons, or through the keyboard by pressing F1, F2, F3, F4 function buttons.

\hypertarget{layout}{%
\section{Layout}\label{layout}}

👉 Customize the Vibe \textbf{Layout} and get the best interface to fit any operator's needs and preferences.

This chapter provides an overview of Vibe's layout and GUI (Graphical User Interface).

\textbf{Chapter index:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Interface-3.1}{3.1 Interface}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Vibe-Menu-3.2}{3.2 Vibe Menu}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Pages-3.3}{3.3 Pages}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Views-3.4}{3.4 Views}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Layouts-3.5}{3.5 Layouts}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Interface-3.1}{%
\subsection{Interface}\label{Interface-3.1}}

Vibe has a multi-touch based interface, using simple gestures for selection and navigation.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Supported \textbf{Gestures}:

  ◾ One finger or left mouse click to select.

  ◾ Two fingers or right mouse click to swipe and scroll.

  ◾ Long finger press or left mouse click for Drag\&Drop.

  ◾ Long finger press or left mouse click on objects for Quick Menu Actions.

  ◾ Multi-selection via ️️⤴ ⤵ (right-\textgreater up /right-\textgreater down) shaped gestures.
\item
  Vibe directly supports up to 4 monitors, two embedded and two external. (Additional monitors may be added by using Display Port 1.2 or higher and MST splitter for a total of 4 external monitors at 1920 x 1080 resolution).
\item
  The small embedded screen called \textbf{Smart Screen} is dedicated to context sensitive fixture information and interactive parameter pickers. It is used for Editor programming.
\item
  The large embedded screen called \textbf{Vibe Main Screen} is general purpose, as well as the external monitors that may be connected. It is used for most of the patch, program and playback operations, collecting all the views on fully customizable pages that can be saved into user layout files.
\item
  Each screen, excluding the Smart Screen, can have one \textbf{Window} or more, with an independent set of \textbf{Pages}.
\item
  Each window can contain up to 12 Pages divided in 3 different Page Types:

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    up to 4 Pages for \textbf{PATCH}
  \item
    up to 4 Pages for \textbf{PROGRAM}
  \item
    up to 4 Pages for \textbf{PLAYBACK}
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{smart-screen}{%
\subsubsection{Smart Screen}\label{smart-screen}}

The 11.6'' multi-touch monitor embedded on the right Editor section of the Vibe 2 screen console models is referred to as the \textbf{Smart Screen}.

\textbf{Smart Screen}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  On Vibe multi-screen console models the Smart Screen position is fixed on the dedicated monitor. The Smart Screen View cannot be moved on the other screens and the configuration of this screen cannot be customized.
\item
  On Vibe single-screen console models and on Vibe PC the Smart Screen is a View, so it can be positioned on any layout Page.
\end{itemize}

Vibe Smart Screen has different options that allow o use it both for the fixtures' parameters manipulation and for the controllers' playback.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  The Bank keys (intensity, Position, color, etc\ldots) give access to the selected fixtures's parameter controls.
\item
  The line on the bottom of the Smart Screen, called \textbf{Echoline}, shows the current fixture selection or the syntax.
\item
  On the right side of the echoline there are the Smart Screen's options and settings.
\item
  The lower part and the right side of the screen show the parameters that may be controlled, depending on the selected fixtures and banks and according to the encoder wheels and extra wheels assignment.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{toolbars}{%
\subsubsection{Toolbars}\label{toolbars}}

Vibe Toolbar is a function line that may be placed either on the top or the bottom of the Vibe main screens.

The left side of the Toolbar contains the \textbf{Vibe Menu} button, the \textbf{Home Pages} buttons, and the \textbf{Command Line} that shows the current syntax.

The right side of the Toolbar, also referred to as the \textbf{Editor Toolbar}, contains a set of interactive buttons that are used to access additional editor commands.

The \textbf{Editor Toolbar} shows a different set of buttons according to the programming syntax.

\textbf{Patch Toolbar}

\textbf{Program Toolbar}

The functions of the Toolbar buttons are described in the following chapters.

\hypertarget{Vibe-Menu-3.2}{%
\subsection{Vibe Menu}\label{Vibe-Menu-3.2}}

Vibe Menu can be opened at any time by tapping the \textbf{\{VIBE\}} key on the Toolbar.

The Vibe Menu is a hierarchical menu containing logically grouped submenus.

It is used to access common system functions such as \textbf{Save Show} and \textbf{Exit}, as well as to configure the \textbf{Layout} of the Pages.

It contains all the Views listed in dedicated submenus.

The upper part of the Menu shows the currently loaded show file.

The search field may be used to filter and find the needed view by writing the view's name in it.

\hypertarget{show-menu}{%
\subsubsection{Show Menu}\label{show-menu}}

The Show Menu is used to manage Vibe show files.

Open the Show Menu to access the following options:

\textbf{\{New Show\}} - Start a new show.

\textbf{\{Save Show\}} - Save the current show in the last defined directory.

\textbf{\{Save Show As\ldots\}} - Open a popup to name and save the current show (on Vibe DB, on an external device, or on both).

\textbf{\{Load Show\}} - Open a popup to load a saved show file (from Vibe DB or from an external device).

\textbf{\{Default Show\}} - Open the default Vibe show. { (not implemented yet) }

\textbf{\{Import Partial Show\}} - Open a popup to load a part of a saved show file. { (not implemented yet) }

\textbf{\{Load Vector Show\}} - Open a popup to load and convert a show from Compulite Vector (.cvs show files).

\textbf{\{Clean Up Show\}} - Open a popup where it is possible to check and delete the data that are not being used in the show.

\textbf{\{Show Report\}} - Open a popup where it is possible to create excel files with the data of the show such as Groups, Libraries, Qlists and Scenes content.

\hypertarget{load-vector-show}{%
\paragraph{Load Vector Show}\label{load-vector-show}}

The Load Vector Show popup, if opened from a PC that has Vector software installed, will allow to load a show directly from Vector's show folder.

After selecting the Vector show file it will be analyzed by the Vibe and then a popup will guide through the process of device matching and show conversion.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Set the matching objects on each tab, by changing the number assigned to the Vibe object.
\item
  Press the Next or Back keys to navigate through the tabs.
\item
  Press the Reset key to discard the matches edited on the current tab.
\item
  Press the Generate Report (enabled on the last tab) to create a grid file that lists all the matches that have been set between the Vector and the Vibe show files.
\end{itemize}

Vector Shows will be loaded and converted as follows:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Devices}: If a matching device exists, it will be used and modes will be matched. If a similar device exists, it will be used and modes will try to be matched. If no match or close match is found, a generic device will be created.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Patch}: Vector device parameters will be mapped to Vibe device parameters.
\item
  \textbf{Groups}: will be matched.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Libraries}: will be matched per Bank, parameter values will be matched.
\item
  \textbf{General Libraries}: will be matched to Custom Libraries.
\item
  \textbf{Effects}: will be matched.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Qlists}: will be matched.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Cues}: Vector cue numbers will be matched to Vibe cue numbers with no possibility of renumbering before the conversion process.
\item
  \textbf{Cue Values}: Vector cue parameter values will be matched to Vibe cue parameter values.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Temp Cues}: will be converted into Vibe Scenes.
\item
  \textbf{Submasters}: will be converted into Vibe Submasters.
\item
  \textbf{Snaps}: will be matched and mapped to Vibe virtual controllers, until the number of Vector Playbacks exceeds the amount of Vibe Controllers.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Playbacks}: Vector Playbacks will be matched to Vibe Controllers, paging will be matched, all button functions common to Vector and Vibe will be matched.
\item
  \textbf{Macros}: { Not supported yet. }
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{load-mvr-show}{%
\paragraph{Load MVR Show}\label{load-mvr-show}}

The Load MVR Show popup is used to load a show in MVR format and convert it into a Vibe show file.

After selecting the MVR show file it will be analyzed by the Vibe and then a popup will guide through the process of device matching and show conversion.

MVR Shows will be loaded and converted as follows:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Devices}: If a matching device exists, it will be used and modes will be matched. If a similar device exists, it will be used and modes will try to be matched. If no match or close match is found, a generic device will be created.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Fixture}: MVR fixtures IDs will be mapped to Vibe fixtures IDs.
\item
  \textbf{Patch}: MVR fixture addresses will be mapped to Vibe fixture addresses.
\item
  \textbf{Calibration}: MVR fixtures calibration data will be mapped to Vibe calibration data.
\end{itemize}

After matching the MVR devices to the Vibe devices, press Apply and wait for the loading bar to end the show conversion process.

\hypertarget{cleanup-show}{%
\paragraph{CleanUp Show}\label{cleanup-show}}

The Clean Up Show popup shows all the objects that are not used on the show file, such as unused Fixtures and Devices, unused Libraries, unassigned Qlists and Scenes, divided by tabs.

Use this feature to find and delete the unused objects, to reorder the show file and to make it lighter by deleting all the unnecessary contents.

\hypertarget{show-report}{%
\paragraph{Show Report}\label{show-report}}

The Show Report popup collects all the objects stored into the show file, such as Groups, Libraries, Qlists and Scenes, divided by tabs.

Report files can be generated for all the data or just for the selected objects.
Tap ``Generate For Selected'' to export only the selected objects or tap ``Generate For All'' to export all the data, independently from the current selection.

The Format keys allow to choose how the parameters should be written on the document.

👉 Enable the \{Patch Data\} key to store a sheet containing all the patch information, such as fixtures' list, universes, addresses, devices and personalities.

Reports are saved in .cvs file format into the Reports Folder, inside the WorkDir.

Use this feature to save a document containing the show information that may be needed.

\hypertarget{layout-menu}{%
\subsubsection{Layout Menu}\label{layout-menu}}

The Layout Menu is used to manage the Vibe layout files.

Open the Layout Menu to access the following options:

\textbf{\{New Layout\}} - Start a new blank layout.

\textbf{\{Save Layout\}} - Save the current layout in the last defined directory.

\textbf{\{Save Layout As\ldots\}} - Open a popup to name and save the current layout (on Vibe DB or an external device).

\textbf{\{Load Layout\}} - Open a popup to load a saved layout file (from Vibe DB or an external device).

\textbf{\{Default Layout\}} - Open the default Vibe layout, depending on the console model.

\textbf{\{Import Partial Layout\}} - Open a popup to load a part of a saved layout file. { (not implemented yet) }

\textbf{\{Windows\}} - Open a sub-menu to add an External window, toggle the window's Title Bar, enter or exit the Full Screen mode.

\hypertarget{views-menu}{%
\subsubsection{Views Menu}\label{views-menu}}

The Views Menus contain a list of all the views that can be added to any page of the Vibe layout.

Vibe Views are divided in logical order in 3 sub-menus:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Patch Views}
\item
  \textbf{Program Views}
\item
  \textbf{Playback Views}
\end{itemize}

Open Patch / Program / Playback Views menus to access all the views that can be added to any page to configure the Vibe layout.

Vibe's \textbf{Layout} is totally flexible, it can be configured and customized to match the user preferences.

All Vibe Views listed in the Patch, Prog and Play Views menus may be added, positioned, resized and tabbed on any Page of the layout.

The \textbf{Views} chapter will provide a short explanation of each view's features.

\hypertarget{settings-menu}{%
\subsubsection{Settings Menu}\label{settings-menu}}

The Settings Menu is used to manage the Vibe system settings.

Open the Settings Menu to access the following options:

\textbf{\{System Settings\}} - Open a popup where it is possible to configure all the general system settings.

\textbf{\{Network Settings\}} - Open a popup to configure the Console name and IP Address, find other Vibe systems on the same Network, and create a Client based connection such as the Master / Slave.

\textbf{\{DMX Settings\}} - Open a popup to enable, disable and configure the DMX outputs and inputs ports, the network protocols such as VC's, ArtNet and ACN, and the DMX rate.

\textbf{\{I/O Settings\}} - Open a popup to enable, disable and configure the MIDI notes, OSC commands, the Midi Show Control, and the timecode protocols such as MTC and SMPTE.

\textbf{\{CITP Settings\}} - Open a popup to enable, disable and configure the CITP settings.
Learn more about configuring CITP in the dedicated chapter.

\textbf{\{Offline Tool\}} - Open the Offline Tool to manage software and hardware settings.
Learn more about the Offline Tool in the dedicated chapter. { (under development) }

\textbf{\{Fixture Builder\}} - Open the Fixture Builder to build, import or edit devices.
Learn more about the Offline Tool in the dedicated chapter. { (under development) }

👉 System settings popup can be quickly opened by tapping on the ``System Status'' View.

👉 All settings popups can be directly accessed by adding the virtual ``Settings Buttons'' view to the layout.

\hypertarget{help-menu}{%
\subsubsection{Help Menu}\label{help-menu}}

\textbf{\{Version\}} - Open a popup listing the installed version of Vibe software and components.

\textbf{\{Manual\}} - Open a popup with the Vibe Manual that can be also found online at \url{https://vibemanual.compulite.com/}.

👉 Vibe Manual is embedded in the Vibe Software.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  If the system is online, the web manual will be opened in the browser.
\item
  If the system is offline, a local version of the Manual will be opened.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{\{Tutorial\}} - Open the tutorial popup that is shown at Vibe's software start up.

\textbf{\{Error Report\}} - Create an Error Report file that can be shared with Compulite Support in case of software problems.

\hypertarget{exit}{%
\subsubsection{Exit}\label{exit}}

The Exit and Close buttons are located in the Vibe Menu.
- Main window shows the Exit button to exit the software
- External windows show the Close button to close the current window.

If Exit is pressed, a popup asks to save the show file.

If Close is pressed, a popup asks to confirm.

\hypertarget{Pages-3.3}{%
\subsection{Pages}\label{Pages-3.3}}

Vibe has 3 types of Pages:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Patch Page}
\item
  \textbf{Program Page}
\item
  \textbf{Playback Page}
\item
  The page buttons are used to switch between the different pages and they are specific to the monitor that has focus (the last touched or mouse selected screen).
\end{itemize}

\textbf{{[}View{]}} opens the Page View and the button color coding shows the currently focused monitor.

👉 Paging is independent for each window, but it can be synchronized by pressing \textbf{{[}Shift{]}+{[}View{]}}

\textbf{{[}Patch Page{]}} or the F2 keyboard button switches between the existing Patch Pages.

\textbf{{[}Program Page{]}} or the F3 keyboard button switches between the existing Program Pages.

\textbf{{[}Playback Page{]}} or the F4 keyboard button switches between the existing Playback Pages.

All the Pages are organized in the \textbf{Page View} that can be accesses either by pressing the \textbf{{[}VIEW{]}} button or by tapping the \textbf{\{VIBE\}} Menu and then the \textbf{\{PAGES\}} key on the Toolbar.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  All Pages can be completely customized by adding, sizing and positioning the \textbf{Views} listed inside the Vibe Menu, independently from where the view is located inside the Vibe Menu.
\item
  Pages can also be moved from one type to another, as soon as one of the 4 spaces per type is available.
\item
  The Pages View can be unlocked through the Selection button in order to assign Home Pages, reorder the pages with drag and drop, Clone or Delete existent Pages.
\end{itemize}

👉 On the Console it is recommended to set views such as the System Status and the Playback Status on the bottom of the Program and Playback pages, to have them in correspondence to the physical controllers.

\hypertarget{home-pages}{%
\subsubsection{Home Pages}\label{home-pages}}

Home page buttons, located on the Toolbar, are a fast way to recall up to three main pages.

F1 keyboard button switches between the three Home Pages.

The currently active Home Page is shown in Green.

Any page can be stored as a Home Page on the Page View.

The Home Pages may be assigned when the screen is in Page View and it is unlocked by tapping the Selection key.

\hypertarget{clone-pages}{%
\subsubsection{Clone Pages}\label{clone-pages}}

Clone pages for a faster building of a similar layout page.

Any page can be cloned as long as there is room available.

The Clone Pages key is displayed on the Toolbar, near the menu, when the screen is in Page View and it is unlocked by tapping the Selection key.

\hypertarget{external-windows}{%
\subsubsection{External Windows}\label{external-windows}}

Any additional window that is added to the Vibe layout, maybe to be placed on an external monitor, will have exactly the same possibilities of the Main window.

Each external window also has:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  3 page types (Patch, Prog, Play-B) with up to 4 pages each,
\item
  3 Home Pages directly accessible throght the Home keys,
\item
  A Toolbar showing the Vibe Manu and Command Line.
\end{itemize}

The Page buttons operate on the currently active window, the last one selected with a touch or mouse input.

The Vibe Menu of the external windows shows the \textbf{Close} button instead of the \textbf{Exit} button. Tapping on it will close the window without exiting the software.

Vibe Layout files save all the windows that are currently shown in the Vibe workspace.

\hypertarget{Views-3.4}{%
\subsection{Views}\label{Views-3.4}}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Vibe Views are listed inside the Vibe Menu, into the Patch, Program, Playback Views submenus.
\item
  Any View can be added to any Page and stored in the layout.
\item
  Views can be moved between different pages. While editing the layout and dragging a view, press a Page button to switch the current page and the view will follow, making it possible to add it to the visualized page.
\item
  Views can be resized and tabbed together, but each view has its own dimension limits.
\item
  To edit the placement of views on the layout, the \textbf{\{Lock\}} icon near the \textbf{\{VIBE\}} menu key must be toggled to unlock the layout editing. After changes are made, it must be toggled again to lock the layout and allow normal usage.
\item
  To configure the layout, any object in the Patch, Program and Playback Views hierarchical sub-menus can be tapped to open the view on a blank portion of the current page, or can be dragged and dropped into the page surface. Once on the page it may be moved or sized freely, space permitting. This operation automatically unlocks the layout editing.
\item
  If an object is dragged on top of another, both will turn green showing it is possible to tab them and create a \textbf{tabbed} view. Keep a tabbed view pressed to extract an object from the tab an move it separately.
\end{itemize}

👉 Use the \textbf{Quick Layout Actions} menu to directly access the View's lists in Vibe Menu, Lock and Unlock the Layout, open the Page View or the Layout Settings popup. Quick Layout Actions may be opened through a long press gesture either on the Toolbar or on any empty space of a page.

Here is the complete list of all the views that can be added to the layout pages to configure and customize the Vibe workspace.

\hypertarget{patch-views}{%
\subsubsection{Patch Views}\label{patch-views}}

\textbf{Fixtures} - The list of all the fixtures created in the show.

\textbf{Sets} - The list of all the sets, including the default sets (Fixture, Channel, Spot, Matrix, Media Server) and the custom sets.

\textbf{Devices} - The list of all the devices imported in the show.

\textbf{Universes} - The list of all the DMX universes.

\textbf{Curves} - The list of all the curve profiles, including the default curves and the custom ones.

\textbf{Fixture View} - The table of the created fixtures, with ID, patch and device information.

\textbf{Address View} - The table of the DMX addresses, showing the fixtures and parameters patch and properties.

\textbf{Universe View} - The table of the DMX universes that allows to patch by dragging the cells.

\textbf{DMX In/Out View} - The table that shows the current values of the DMX output and input universes.

\textbf{OSC View} - The list and trigger of the OSC commands.

\textbf{External Devices} - The space to check and configure connected devices, such as ePort nodes and RDM fixtures.

\textbf{Selection} - The box used to grab the fixture selection and drag it on the universe view.

\hypertarget{program-views}{%
\subsubsection{Program Views}\label{program-views}}

\textbf{Smart Screen} - The view that contains the graphical editor screen.

\textbf{Encoders} - The view that contains the encoders in the lower part of the Smart Screen.

\textbf{Hard Keys} - The virtual version of the hard keys panel.

\textbf{Single Buttons} - This sub-menu collects all the virtual buttons.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Single Button} - The list of all the virtual button.
\item
  \textbf{Layout Page Buttons}
  - The virtual buttons used for page navigation: View, Patch, Program, Playback.
\item
  \textbf{Controller Page Buttons}
  - The virtual buttons used for controller navigation: Slider and Qkey Page Up/Down.
\item
  \textbf{Settings Button}
  - The virtual buttons used to access the settings popups.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Softkeys}
- This sub-menu collects the softkey views of the objects.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Group}
  - The softkey view of the Groups.
\item
  \textbf{Qlist}
  - The softkey view of the Qlists.
\item
  \textbf{Scene}
  - The softkey view of the Scenes.
\item
  \textbf{Snap}
  - The softkey view of the Snaps.
\item
  \textbf{Filter}
  - The softkey view of the Filters.
\item
  \textbf{Macro}
  - The softkey view of the Macros.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Library} - This sub-menu collects the softkey views of the libraries.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Intensity} - The softkey view of the Intensity Bank libraries.
\item
  \textbf{Position} - The softkey view of the Position Bank libraries.
\item
  \textbf{Color} - The softkey view of the Color Bank libraries.
\item
  \textbf{Beam} - The softkey view of the Beam Bank libraries.
\item
  \textbf{Image} - The softkey view of the Image Bank libraries.
\item
  \textbf{Shape} - The softkey view of the Shape Bank libraries.
\item
  \textbf{Custom} - The softkey view of the Custom libraries.
\item
  \textbf{Effect} - The softkey view of the Effect libraries.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Context} - The dynamic softkey view showing contents based on the current syntax.

\textbf{Live} - The table view that shows the live status of all the fixtures' parameters.

👉 Live view have 4 different visualization modes, allowing to have or not have fixtures divided by \textbf{Headers per Device} and to show or not show \textbf{SubDevices}. These modes may also be switched later in the Live View Settings popup.

\textbf{Live Parameters} - The icon view that shows the live status of the fixtures' parameters.

\textbf{Qlist Tracksheet} - The table used to check, edit and update the Qlists and Cues stored in the show.

\textbf{Exam} - The worktree used to check all the objects stored in the show.

\textbf{Topo} - The space to create interactive topographical views used to select the fixtures and trigger the objects.

\textbf{Timeline} - The space to record and trigger time-code based timelines.

\hypertarget{playback-views}{%
\subsubsection{Playback Views}\label{playback-views}}

\textbf{A/B Crossfader} - The virtual version of the A/B crossfader.

\textbf{Slider} - The virtual version of the Slider controllers: Set, Fader, 3 Buttons, Button, 5 Sets.

\textbf{QKey} - The virtual version of the Qkey controllers: Set, Fader, Button, 5 Sets.

\textbf{AuxKey} - The virtual version of the AuxKey controllers: Set, Fader, Button, 5 Sets.

\textbf{Global Slider} - The virtual version of the Global Slider controllers: Set, Fader, Button.

\textbf{Master Keys} - The virtual Master Keys panel.

\textbf{Operation Keys} - The virtual version of the Operation Keys panel.

\textbf{Command Keys} - The virtual version of the Command Keys panel.

\textbf{Live Controller} - The tabbed view that shows the live status of a target controller and its content.

\textbf{System Status} - The box that shows the current status of the system, also used to access the system setting.

\textbf{Playback Status} - The box that shows the current status of a single controller, also used to access the controller's setting.

\textbf{Playback Status Set} - The box that shows the current status of a set of 5 controllers, also used to access the controller's setting.

\hypertarget{view-settings}{%
\subsubsection{View Settings}\label{view-settings}}

Some views have specific settings that can be set from the view settings popup, opened from the toolbar when the layout is unlocked.

View settings can be accessed when layout is \textbf{Unlocked}, by selecting the view and tapping the buttons on the toolbar. These settings are applied to the selected view.

\textbf{Toggle Scrollbar}: add or remove a scrollbar to/from the view, if available.

\textbf{Toggle Frame}: add or remove the frame to/from the view.

\textbf{Frame Color}: customize the view's frame for a better graphical separation between the views.

\textbf{Softkey Settings}: any softkey view can be graphically customized by selecting it in layout edit mode and opening the settings popup with the \{Settings\} button on the toolbar.

Softkey settings are used to change the size, font and color of the softkeys' text and background.

The settings can be saved as Pre-Built and applied to the other views of the same tab, page, or to the softkey views in all the pages.

The settings can be saved as default, and it is always possible to come back to the default or to reset the changes.

Some special color options are available to show color library softkeys with the colors that are stored in the library.

The auto text color option in Softkey Settings popup automatically sets the softkey's text color in black or white in contrast with the softkey's background color, to make it always visible.

\textbf{Context Options} only apply to the Context view. This view can show or hide the visualized objects depending on the user's settings or on the smart option.

The Context Normal Mode filters the visualized objects according to the enabled buttons.

The Context Smart Mode filters the visualized objects automatically avoiding the softkeys that are already visible in the page.

\textbf{Single Button Settings}: any single button view can be graphically customized by selecting it in layout edit mode and opening the settings popup with the \{Settings\} button on the toolbar.

Specific settings, such as background color and text color, size, or autotext option, either in Idle State or On Press can be assigned to each single button.

The settings can be saved as Pre-Built and applied to the other single buttons of the same tab, page, or to the buttons in all the pages.

The settings can be saved as default, and it is always possible to come back to the default or to reset the changes.

\textbf{Views Menu} is availeble on some views, such as Live, Topo and Timeline.

The View's menu can be closed when the layout is Locked by tapping the Close Menu button.

The Open Menu button can be placed wherever on the view with a long click gesture and drag.

\hypertarget{Layouts-3.5}{%
\subsection{Layouts}\label{Layouts-3.5}}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  After all pages and views are configured, the layout configuration can be saved into a \textbf{Layout} file by tapping the \textbf{\{VIBE\}} Menu and then the \textbf{\{LAYOUT\}} key.
\item
  After configuring new windows on the external monitors, a layout file should be saved to preserve the external monitor layout on reboot. Layout files store the configuration of external monitors and if the system is booted without the expected external monitor a warning message will appear.
\item
  Layout files, containing all the graphical settings of pages and views, are loaded independently from the Show files, containing all the stored data.
\end{itemize}

👉 Layout Files are particularly useful when the same desk is managed by different operators; each user can load his own customized layout without losing any show data programmed by the previous user.

\hypertarget{layout-settings}{%
\subsubsection{Layout Settings}\label{layout-settings}}

Layout Settings can be accessed when layout is \textbf{Locked}, by tapping the Vibe Menu and then the \{Setting\} button on the toolbar, near the Lock and Pages buttons. These settings are applied as defaults to all the Pages and Views.

It is possible to change the Vibe Theme, to move the Toolbar on top or bottom position on all the pages, and to set Default View Settings for the Headers, the Frames and the Scrollbars.

\hypertarget{settings}{%
\section{Settings}\label{settings}}

👉 Configure the Vibe \textbf{Settings} to manage the system default behaviors and the protocols inputs and outputs.

This chapter provides a description of Vibe's software settings that can be customized.

\textbf{Chapter index:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{System-Settings-4.1}{4.1 System Settings}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Network-Settings-4.2}{4.2 Network Settings}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{DMX-Settings-4.3}{4.3 DMX Settings}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{I-O-Settings-4.4}{4.4 I/O Settings}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{CITP-Settings-4.5}{4.5 CITP Settings}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{System-Settings-4.1}{%
\subsection{System Settings}\label{System-Settings-4.1}}

System Settings popup is accessed by selecting \{System Settings\} from the Vibe Menu, by tapping the {[}System Settings{]} single button key, or by tapping the \{System Status\} area in the system status view.

\hypertarget{editing}{%
\subsubsection{Editing}\label{editing}}

\textbf{Editing Properties}

\textbf{Default Stage Value:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Home Value: when released fixtures parameters return to their home values as specified in the device's definition or user \textbf{Home Scene}.
\item
  Maintain Last Value: when released fixture parameters remain at their current value, except for the dimmer.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Auto Param Grouping:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  When enabled, parameters are grouped as follows:
\end{itemize}

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}ccc@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\textbf{Bank} & \textbf{Parameters} & \textbf{Grouping} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Intensity & & Not Grouped \\
Position & Pan, Tilt & Grouped \\
Position & Virtual X/Y/Z & Grouped \\
Color & All Color Mix & Grouped \\
Color & CMY Color Mix & Grouped \\
Color & Color Wheels, Color Correction and misc & Not Grouped \\
Beam & Any Param & Not Grouped \\
Image & Any Param & Not Grouped \\
Shape & Blade Depth, Angle, Rotation - Per Blade & Grouped \\
\end{longtable}

\textbf{Clear Editor After Store:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  After storing a cue, values remain in the editor until {[}RESET{]} or {[}VIBE{]} + {[}RESET{]} is pressed.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Automatically Assign Cues to PB:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  After storing a cue, it is automatically assigned to the Master Controller.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Editor Flash:}

Sets default values for toolbar \{FLASH\} feature.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{Base\} = Start point (low value) \{Time\} = Flash duration \{Size\} = Amplitude (high values)
\item
  Defaults are \{Base\} 0.2 (20\%) \{Time\} 2 seconds \{Size\} 0.6 (80\%)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Qlist Behaviour:}

Tracking / Non-Tracking:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Tracking:

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Values track forward. The stage look is made up of a combination of hard and tracked values.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Non-Tracking:

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Only hard values are recorded and zero dimmer values is treated as a hard zero.
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\begin{quote}
{In Non-Tracking mode, parameter values that are not given values will still output to the stage where they were last left. This can accidentally lead to mistakes where when played back, the cue does not look the same as it did when it was recorded. It is suggested that the \textbf{Store Options} \{All Parameters if Active\} or \{All Parameters if Selected\} are used whenever possible to avoid errors.}
\end{quote}

\textbf{Clear Editor on Master GO:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The editor will be reset when \textbf{GO} on the \textbf{Master Controller} is pressed.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Store Scene by Default:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  By default {[}STORE{]} {[}HERE{]} (press any controller button) will create and assign the first available Scene to the controller instead of a Qlist and Cue.
\item
  Qlists may then be stored by using the syntax {[}STORE{]} {[}CUE{]} {[}HERE{]} (press any controller button).
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Next Cue Increment:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  When {[}Store{]} {[}Here{]}, {[}STORE{]} {[}STORE{]}, or {[}STORE{]} {[}ENTER{]} are used to append a new cue to the end of a Qlist, the specified spacing will be used.
\end{itemize}

\begin{quote}
If the increment is 1 and the last cue is 10, the new cue would be 11. If the increment is 10, then the new cue would be 20.
\end{quote}

\textbf{Apply Compulite Mode:}

Sets a number of default settings to emulate the traditional non-tracking Compulite Mode.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Default Stage Values = \{Maintain Last Value\}
\item
  \{Clear Editor After Store\} = Off
\item
  \{Automatically Assign Cues to PB\} = Off
\item
  Qlist Behavior = \{Non-Tracking\}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{ON Value:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Virtual wheel sets the default editor value for the dimmer parameter when the {[}ON{]} key is pressed.
\item
  {\{First ON Recalls Last\} - Pressing {[}ON{]} the first time will copy the last dimmer value set for a fixture into the next selected fixture. Pressing again will set the dimmer value to the ON default.}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Restore Defaults:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{Restore Popup Positions\} - Restores the position of the popups that have been moved.
\item
  \{Restore Defaults\} - Restores settings changes to the last time \{Apply to Defaults\} was set.
\item
  \{Restore Factory\} - Restores settings changes to factory defaults.
\item
  \{Apply to Defaults\} -Updates system setting changes to \textbf{show specific defaults}.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{timing}{%
\subsubsection{Timing}\label{timing}}

\textbf{Timing Properties}

\textbf{Default Back Time:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Default Time used when the {[}Back{]} or {[}Hold Back{]} Buttons are pressed.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Default Assert:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Default Time used when a controller's parameters are reasserted with the {[}ASSERT{]} {[}HERE{]} command.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Default GOTO Time:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Default Time used whith the GOTO command. GOTO time can be overridden using command {[}GOTO{]} {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}TIME{]} {[}Value{]} {[}ENTER{]}.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Default Release Time:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Default Fade out time for parameters being released from the editor using the {[}RESET{]} or {[}RELEASE{]} keys, or Controllers being released from the stage using the controller {[}RELEASE{]} button.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Use Cue Time:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The Back, Asset and GOTO times may be seet to use the Cue Time as default by enabling the {[}Use Cue Time{]} button.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Look Ahead:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Set All Controllers On will enable the Look Ahead function for all controllers.
\item
  Set All Controllers Off will disable the Look Ahead function for all controllers.
\end{itemize}

\begin{quote}
Once all is set ON individual controllers may disable Look Ahead at any time.
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
Once all is set OFF individual controllers may enable Look Ahead at any time.
\end{quote}

\textbf{System Time:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  System Time (Editor Time) is applied to values being introduced into the editor and other miscellaneous features that do not otherwise have their own timing settings.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{hardware-1}{%
\subsubsection{Hardware}\label{hardware-1}}

\textbf{Hardware Properties}

\textbf{Panel Backlight:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Inactive keys:} Adjusts the LED backlight level of keys that are not valid in the current operational state and the LED backlight level of controllers that are unassigned.
\item
  \textbf{Active Keys:} Adjusts the LED backlight level of keys that may be used in the current operational state and the LED backlight level of controllers that have objects assigned.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Operating System:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Opens relevant Windows setting screen to adjust the OS settings.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{defaults}{%
\subsubsection{Defaults}\label{defaults}}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Defaults} are the customizable initial settings for most system objects.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{controller-settings}{%
\paragraph{Controller Settings}\label{controller-settings}}

\textbf{Default Controller Settings}

The popup contains all the settings referred to the controller, independently from the object loaded.

Controller Settings are divided into:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Flash Behavior
\item
  Slider Behavior
\item
  Properties
\item
  Priority
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Flash behavior:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Release On Release} - While a Flash button is depressed, the controller will assert its values, when released the controller will be released from affecting the stage.
\item
  \textbf{Pure LTP} - Pressing a Flash key reasserts overridden LTP values from another controller but does not release them when the flash key is released.
\item
  \textbf{Go On Flash} - If a slider is down it will flash the intensity to full and advance the Qlist in cue time with every press of the Flash key.
\item
  \textbf{Latch} - Flash key toggles ON/OFF.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Slider behavior:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Intensity Only} - Slider only controls the intensity.
\item
  \textbf{Go + Fade Parameters} - Moving the slider off zero will initiate a GO command. All parameters, except intensity, will fade to their recorded values using cue time. Intensity will remain under the control of the slider.
\item
  \textbf{Go + Jump Parameters} - Parameters Jump to their values as soon as the slider moves off zero. Intensity will remain under the control of the slider.
\item
  \textbf{Release At Bottom} - The controller will be released from affecting the stage when the slider reaches zero. Default System Release Time or Qlist Release Time will be used.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Properties}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Exclude from Snap} - The controller will not be recorded in Snapshots.
\item
  \textbf{Exclude from (Snap) Override} - Snap commands will be ignored even if the controller was initially recorded in a snap.
\item
  \textbf{Exclude from Free All} - The controller will not be unloaded with a global {[}VIBE{]} + {[}FREE{]} command. It may only be unloaded with {[}FREE{]} {[}HERE{]}.
\item
  \textbf{Exclude from Release All} - The controller will not be released with a global {[}VIBE{]} + {[}RELEASE{]} command. It may only be unloaded with {[}RELEASE{]} {[}HERE{]}.
\item
  \textbf{Prevent from Paging} - Locks a Qlist or Scene to the current page. Paging ⤴ or ⤵ will not affect the Qlist or Scene on the controller.
\item
  \textbf{Prevent from Grab} - The Grab command will not capture the controller's active parameters.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Priority}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Virtual Priority Wheel} - Sets the controller's LTP group number. Controllers assigned higher numbers cannot be overridden by lower numbers. Controllers assigned the same number will be LTP among themselves. Values may be entered using the virtual wheel or directly by tapping the middle of the center area of the wheel and typing a value from the keypad.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Restore defaults}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Resets Controller settings to their last stored configuration.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{controller-actions}{%
\paragraph{Controller Actions}\label{controller-actions}}

\textbf{Default Controller Actions}

The popup contains all the actions that can be assigned to the controller, depending on the object type (Qlist, Chaser, Scene, Submaster).

Controller Actions are divided into:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Slider Controller: 1 fader + 3 buttons
\item
  A/B Crossfader: dual fader + 2 buttons
\item
  Other Controllers: 1 fader + 1 button
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  When a Scene is configured as a \textbf{Group Submaster} it has a unique set of functions and button options.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{snap-settings}{%
\paragraph{Snap Settings}\label{snap-settings}}

\textbf{Load Pages:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Snap will change the current page to the page the Snap was stored in.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Load Master Controller:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Snap will change the {[}Select{]} to the controller that was Master Controller at the time of the Snap.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Snap Time:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Default fade time for all controllers being initiated by the Snap.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Unit Button:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Toggles the virtual time wheel units between Milliseconds, Seconds, Minutes, Hours
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{qlist-settings}{%
\paragraph{Qlist Settings}\label{qlist-settings}}

\textbf{Qlist Mode:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Normal} - Cues do not auto loop and all normal time functions are calculated.
\item
  \textbf{Chaser} - Cues auto-loop from one to another. Only In-Time, and Delay-In Time are calculated. Parameter time is still valid.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Qlist Direction:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Backward} - Chasers and Qlists transition highest to lowest.
\item
  \textbf{Forward} - Chases and Qlists transition lowest to highest.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Tracking:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{ON} - Normal tracking logic will be used.
\item
  \textbf{OFF} - Tracking is not calculated and dimmer at zero is treated as a hard zero (Compulite Mode).
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Preferences}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Back to top on release} - After a controller is released, it will reset to the first cue in the Qlist.
\item
  \textbf{Sequence} - If toggled on, pressing GO will advance the Qlist.
\item
  \textbf{Mark cue at top of the list.} - The Qlist will start with a Mark Cue to preposition the parameters.
\item
  \textbf{Loop back to first} - The Qlist will loop from the end to the beginning of the list when GO is pressed.
\item
  \textbf{Trigger} - Toggles on/off the execution of Macros and Snaps that are attached to cues.
\item
  \textbf{Look Ahead} - Enable/Disable the Look Ahead function.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Loop GO Behavior:}

\textbf{Release time:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Wheel at Default - System Default Release time is referenced.
\item
  Rotate the wheel to set a Qlist specific time that a controller references to fade to off.
\item
  Tap the inside of the virtual wheel until it turns red and set a release value from the keypad.
\item
  \textbf{Unit Button} Toggles the virtual time wheel units between Milliseconds, Seconds, Minutes, Hours.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Restore default}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Resets Qlist properties to their last stored configuration.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{chaser-settings}{%
\paragraph{Chaser Settings}\label{chaser-settings}}

\textbf{Chase pattern:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Normal} - Cues loop from one to another in order.
\item
  \textbf{Build} - Cues are added on to each other until the end of the Qlist and then the build is repeated. (Normal tracking behavior).
\item
  \textbf{Bounce} - Cues transition from lowest cue number to highest, then reverse and transition from highest to lowest.
\item
  \textbf{Bounce Build} - Cues are added on to each other lowest to highest are added to each other highest to lowest.
\item
  \textbf{Random} - Cue Order is randomized.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Time:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Chase Time} Independent in-time for dimmer when a chase is started.
\item
  \textbf{Unit Button} Sets the units for time settings.
\item
  \textbf{Loop count} Repeats the chase loop the specified count and stops on the last cue.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Properties:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Int Limit} Proportionately limits the intensity output of the cues assigned to a controller.
\item
  \textbf{X-fade} Proportionately adjusts the crossfade curve of a chase from full crossfade to jump.
\item
  \textbf{Restores default} - Resets Chase properties to their last stored configuration.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{cue-settings}{%
\paragraph{Cue Settings}\label{cue-settings}}

\textbf{Time:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Behavior selects the target time between Wait, Follow, Look Ahead, Time In/Out, Delay In/Out.
\item
  Virtual time Wheel assigns the time value, using the Unit button to set the unit for time settings.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Properties:}

\begin{verbatim}
- Tracking options for the stored cues. Default is Track.

- Force Black button adds a Force Black Cue before the stored Cue.
\end{verbatim}

\hypertarget{cue-store-options}{%
\paragraph{Cue Store Options}\label{cue-store-options}}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{\{Red + Grey\}} Editor Values in 100\% 90\% will be stored.
\item
  \textbf{\{Red Only\}} Only Editor values in Red will be stored. This can be a useful way to filter parameters.
\item
  \textbf{\{All Param If Active\}} All parameters of all fixtures in the editor will be stored if the dimmer parameter is greater than zero.
\item
  \textbf{\{Store Stage\}} All fixtures outputting ANY DMX value from the console will be stored. This includes tracked values, home values and dimmer values at zero.
\item
  \textbf{\{All Param For Selected\}} All parameters from the fixture selection will be turned red and stored in the cue.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{scene-settings}{%
\paragraph{Scene Settings}\label{scene-settings}}

\textbf{Time In}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Sets the default in-time for Scenes. The default value may be changed using:
\item
  Virtual time Wheel assigns the time value, using the Unit button to set the unit for time settings.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{scene-store-options}{%
\paragraph{Scene Store Options}\label{scene-store-options}}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{\{Red + Grey\}} Editor Values in 100\% 90\% will be stored.
\item
  \textbf{\{Red Only\}} Only Editor values in Red will be stored. This can be a useful way to filter parameters.
\item
  \textbf{\{All Param If Active\}} All parameters of all fixtures in the editor will be stored if the dimmer parameter is greater than zero.
\item
  \textbf{\{Store Stage\}} All fixtures outputting ANY DMX value from the console will be stored. This includes tracked values, home values and dimmer values at zero.
\item
  \textbf{\{All Param For Selected\}} All parameters from the fixture selection will be turned red and stored in the cue.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{library-settings}{%
\paragraph{Library Settings}\label{library-settings}}

\textbf{Library settings are the same for all the following library types:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Intensity
\item
  Position
\item
  Color
\item
  Beam
\item
  Image
\item
  Shape
\item
  Custom
\end{itemize}

*\textbf{Default Library Settings:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Library Type - Set the library behavior between Fixture Specific, Parameter Specific, Pattern, Device+Fixture, Device+Parameter, Device+Pattern.
\item
  Reference - Set the library to be referenced in Cues, Scenes and Effects.
\item
  Timing - Set the library to include time values or not.
\item
  Filters - Open the Library Filters popup.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Effect Library settings also include two additional options:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Include Base - If toggled on, the start points of parameters will be stored with the Effect making them ``Absolute Effects''.
\item
  Randomize - If toggled on, every time the library is assigned in the editor the order of selected fixtures will be randomized. When storing a randomized effect into a Scene or a Cue, the order will be kept as it was at the moment of storing.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{library-filters}{%
\paragraph{Library Filters}\label{library-filters}}

\textbf{Library Defaults:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The popup window is used to edit, for each bank, which parameters will be filtered by the library.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Select the library bank from the Library column, then tap the parameters in the \textbf{Available} box to add them to the \textbf{Filtered} box, so that the library will store them.
\item
  Select the library bank from the Library column, then tap the parameters in the \textbf{Filtered} box to add them to the \textbf{Available} box, so that the library will NOT store them.
\item
  \textbf{Restore Defaults} button will recover the default parameters assignment.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{General:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The popup window is used to create and edit the \textbf{Filters} that can be used on-the-fly when storing libraries, cues and scenes.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  The keys on the left side are used to create, delete or renumber Filters and to assign them a name and a specific softkey color.
\item
  The Available and Filtered parameters boxes are used to defin the parameters that the filter is going to be applied to.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{system}{%
\subsubsection{System}\label{system}}

\textbf{Auto Save:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  The show file can be automatically saved at predefined time intervals.
\item
  Auto save function can be enabled or disabled in the system settings tab, with the option to assign saving time intervals.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{System Units:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Vibe can be set to use either \textbf{metric} or \textbf{imperial} units for stage measurement.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Auto Recover:}

Vibe is made up of four modules:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{GUI - Graphic User Interface} - The windows software with the Vibe graphics.
\item
  \textbf{LPU - Lighting Processing Unit} - The engine that processes and outputs the show data.
\item
  \textbf{HAL - Hardware Access Layer} - The system that connects the panels and devices to the Vibe.
\item
  \textbf{SQL} - The Data management DataBase.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{LPU and HAL Status} LEDs are on the status box:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  In the case of an unexpected halt, the toggled on modules will attempt a restart.
\item
  In the case of an unexpected halt with modules untoggled, the user can attempt a restart manually by tapping the Recover keys.
\item
  There is no option to Auto Recover the database as it is always up to date.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Lock Screen Image}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  The Lock Screen Image can be customized by loading a different image.
\item
  Lock screen is shown when system is locked or when Vibe is waiting for the Fixture Builder to be exit.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Network-Settings-4.2}{%
\subsection{Network Settings}\label{Network-Settings-4.2}}

Network Settings popup is accessed by selecting \{Network Settings\} from the Vibe Menu or by tapping the {[}Network Settings{]} single button key.

Network Settings popup has the following tabs:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Main Network: manages the network settings of the data output.
\item
  User Networks: manages the network settings of the additional ports.
\item
  Users: manages the multiuser settings.
\end{itemize}

For Network management, please refer to the \textbf{Network Chapter}.

\hypertarget{main-network}{%
\subsubsection{Main Network}\label{main-network}}

\hypertarget{user-networks}{%
\subsubsection{User Networks}\label{user-networks}}

👉 This tab does not exist on the VibePC software version, it may be found only on Vibe consoles.

\hypertarget{users}{%
\subsubsection{Users}\label{users}}

{ \textbf{(Not implemented yet)} }

\subsection{DMX Settings}\label{DMX-Settings-4.3}}

DMX Settings popup is accessed by selecting \{DMX Settings\} from the Vibe Menu or by tapping the {[}DMX Settings{]} single button key.

DMX Settings popup has 4 tabs:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Local Output
\item
  VCs
\item
  ArtNet
\item
  sACN
\end{itemize}

For DMX output management, physical or over network protocols, please refer to the \textbf{Patch Chapter}.

\hypertarget{I-O-Settings-4.4}{%
\subsection{I/O Settings}\label{I-O-Settings-4.4}}

I/O Settings popup is accessed by selecting \{I/O Settings\} from the Vibe Menu or by tapping the {[}I/O Settings{]} single button key.

I/O Settings popup has 4 tabs:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  MIDI
\item
  Notes/Commands
\item
  OSC
\item
  SMPTE
\end{itemize}

For MIDI management and MIDI Notes configuration, please refer to the \textbf{Timeline Chapter}.

For OSC protocol management, please refer to the \textbf{Network Chapter}.

For SMPTE protocol management, please refer to the \textbf{Timeline Chapter}.

\hypertarget{CITP-Settings-4.5}{%
\subsection{CITP Settings}\label{CITP-Settings-4.5}}

CITP Settings popup is accessed by selecting \{CITP Settings\} from the Vibe Menu or by tapping the {[}CITP Settings{]} single button key.

CITP Settings popup has 4 tabs:

\begin{itemize}

  General
\item
  Media Servers
\item
  Visualizers
\item
  Consoles
