For CITP protocol management, please refer to the \textbf{Network Chapter}.

\hypertarget{patch}{%
\section{Patch}\label{patch}}

👉 \textbf{Patch} the Vibe in a few short passages: import devices, create fixtures, assign addresses and output the DMX signal.

This chapter deals with creating and patching fixtures.

\textbf{Chapter index:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Sets-5.1}{5.1 Sets}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Devices-5.2}{5.2 Devices}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Create-and-Patch-5.3}{5.3 Create and Patch}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{DMX-Properties-5.4}{5.4 DMX Properties}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Fixture-View-5.5}{5.5 Fixture View}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Address-Properties-5.6}{5.6 Address Properties}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Park-5.7}{5.7 Park}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Clone-5.8}{5.8 Clone}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Replace-5.9}{5.9 Replace}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Calibration-5.10}{5.10 Calibration}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Auto-Patch-5.11}{5.11 Auto-Patch}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Sets-5.1}{%
\subsection{Sets}\label{Sets-5.1}}

Vibe contains an extensive \textbf{Device Library} with DMX parameter mapping for fixtures, media servers, and common miscellaneous devices like smoke machines.
Before a device can be programmed it must be imported and patched to DMX addresses.

Vibe has an advanced numbering system that allows devices to be grouped into five familiar categories called \textbf{SETS}.

The Default Sets are Fixture, Channel, Spot, Matrix, Media Server. Additional custom \textbf{SETS} may also be created. Fixture numbering in all sets can be freely changed if there are no overlapping numbers. Each Set has a unique ID.

\textbf{Set ID numbers:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  0 = \textbf{{[}Fixture{]}} is a special global numbering set that must also reference one of the other sets. All devices must be assigned a Fixture number and one of the other set numbers.
\item
  1 = \textbf{{[}Channel{]}} is a numbering set that would normally be used for conventional single parameter devices.
\item
  2 = \textbf{{[}SPOT{]}} is a numbering set that would mainly be used for multi-parameter devices like moving lights.
\item
  3 = \textbf{{[}MATRIX{]}} is a numbering set that would mainly be used for RGB or other non-moving LED fixtures, also useful for pixel mapping devices.
\item
  4 = \textbf{{[}SERVER{]}} is a numbering system used for media servers.
\end{itemize}

Each \textbf{SET} may have Fixtures numbered from 1 ⟶ 999999

To patch a device, you must first move focus to the main monitor and press the {[}PATCH{]} Workspace Template key. By default, the first page will contain all the displays needed for patching.

\hypertarget{add-sets}{%
\subsubsection{Add Sets}\label{add-sets}}

The option to add a user-created set is done via the Sets view.

\textbf{To Add a Set}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Add a Sets view:
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Open \emph{Vibe} menu
\item
  Tap Patch
\item
  Tap Sets
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{1}
\item
  On the Sets view tap the Add icon on the header
\item
  On the popup define the set's name and number
\item
  Tap apply
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{edit-sets}{%
\subsubsection{Edit Sets}\label{edit-sets}}

There is an option to edit a user-created set. This is done via the Sets view.

\textbf{To Edit a Set}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Add a Sets view:
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Open \emph{Vibe} menu
\item
  Tap Patch
\item
  Tap Sets
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{1}
\item
  On the Sets view select a user-created set
\item
  Tap the Edit icon on the header
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{Devices-5.2}{%
\subsection{Devices}\label{Devices-5.2}}

\textbf{Once the Patch Page is open:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Use the Devices view.
\item
  Tap \{Import\}, the Manufactures picker list will appear.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{2}
\item
  Flick or drag along the list to browse to the desired manufacturer then tap the manufacturer's name.
\item
  A new list and search window will open. If the exact device name is known, type it in the search window, if not flick or drag to browse the list until the desired device is found.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{4}
\tightlist
\item
  The \textless{} key may be pressed at any time to return to the imported Devices list.
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{advanced-search}{%
\subsubsection{Advanced Search}\label{advanced-search}}

Fixtures may be searched directly by name using the \textbf{Advanced Device Search}.

Searches may be filtered by parameter order and DMX Channel Count to limit the search.

👉 The filters are very helpful in finding close matches for fixtures that are not currently in the device library.

\textbf{To access Advanced Search:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Tap \{Import\} on the Devices view header - The tab will show \{Advanced Search\}.
\item
  Tap \{Advanced Search\} - The Advanced Search Pop-up will appear.
\item
  Type the fixture's name in the Fixture Name box, set the number of channels, or select the parameters to filters the devices database.
\item
  Select the fixture from the Matching Devices list.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{4}
\item
  The DMX Modes pop-up will open.
\item
  Review the DMX chart and select the desired mode.
\item
  Tap Apply or Import or press {[}ENTER{]} to close the popup and add the fixture to the Imported Devices list.
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{Create-and-Patch-5.3}{%
\subsection{Create and Patch}\label{Create-and-Patch-5.3}}

\textbf{To open the Create and Patch popup:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Import the desired device.
\item
  The device will now be in the list below the Imported Devices header.
\item
  Tap the device you wish to patch and the Create and Patch Fixtures popup will appear.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{To Create and Patch fixtures:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  In the \textbf{Create and Patch} pop-up, type the quantity in the red highlighted field via the keypad or flick/drag to browse the list until the desired quantity number is found then tap the number.
\item
  If the \textbf{Quantity} number is not highlighted in {Red}, tap the field to make it active.
\item
  Press the ⟶ key on the keypad to tab to the next field, or tap in the \{SET\} field. The appropriate SET for the selected fixture should automatically be selected, but as some fixtures don't always cleanly fit into standard categories, check that the correct Set is selected. If not, flick/drag to browse to the correct SET then tap it. You may also type the Set number (0-5).
\item
  Press the ⟶ key on the keypad to tab to the \{Set Number\} field.
\item
  Set the Channel, Spot, Matrix, or Server number for the fixture in the red highlighted field using the keypad or scroll to browse the list until the desired start number is found, then tap the number.
  👉 By default, the first available number for the selected SET is pre-assigned.
\item
  Press the ⟶ key on the keypad to tab to the next field, or tap any box in the Fixture Number field to select it.
\item
  By default, the same number as the SET Number will appear as the Fixture Number. Many users like to group Fixtures by 10ʼs or 100ʼs so it is common to type 101 for the start of one type of fixture, 201 for a second type and so on. The SET number and Fixture number do not have to be the same.
\item
  Next to the Fixture Number field is a data entry box that displays the fixture's default short name. This may be edited at any time while in this pop-up. The name will be updated when the fixtures are created upon closing the popup.
\item
  In the second section of the pop-up, fixtures that were selected for creation can be patched via the DMX patch wizard. Press the ⟶ key on the keypad to tab to the next field, or tap a number in the UNIVERSE field. If the desired universe is not visible, flick/drag to browse to the correct universe number then tap it.
\item
  Press the ⟶ key on the keypad to tab to the next field, or tap any number in the \textbf{ADDRESS} field.
\end{enumerate}

👉 By default, the interval between fixtures of the same type is fixed.
Using the \textbf{INTERVAL} field, a custom interval may be set.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{10}
\tightlist
\item
  To complete \textbf{CREATE and PATCH} tap the \textbf{\{APPLY \& CONTINUE\}} key in the bottom \textbf{right corner of the popup}, Press \textbf{{[}ENTER{]}}, or tap Apply to close the popup and return to the patch workspace.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Theatrical Multi-Patching can also be done manually using keypad syntax:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Toggle off the \textbf{\{MAIN PATCH\}} key at the top of the DMX patch wizard before closing the pop-up. The fixtures will be created but will remain unpatched.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{To Patch fixtures using traditional keypad syntax:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}ADDRESS{]} {[}\#{]}.{[}\#{]}
\end{itemize}

👉 By default, the first available DMX address of the selected Universe will be shown in the \textbf{ADDRESS} field. If the default start address is not appropriate, type or scroll the list to the desired start address and tap it.Universe.Address, {[}STORE{]}. \textbf{Or}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}ADDRESS{]} {[}\#{]}.{[}\#{]} +/ - ⟶ {[}\#{]}.{[}\#{]} {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}STORE{]}.
\end{itemize}

👉 Addresses may also be typed in absolute format: 1 ⟶ 32,768 (64 Universes standard). The system will automatically assign the correct universe.

👉 When starting from a \{New Show\} only \{Physical DMX outputs\} are enabled. Network universes must be enabled in \textbf{PATCH} \{DMX Settings\}

\hypertarget{drag-and-drop-patch}{%
\subsubsection{Drag and Drop Patch}\label{drag-and-drop-patch}}

If \{Main Patch\} is toggled off in the Create and Patch pop-up, fixtures can be created but not patched. Created fixtures may then be dragged and dropped onto the Universe View.

\textbf{Drag and Drop from the Fixtures tab:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Create fixtures.
\item
  Select the Fixture \textbf{Set} you wish to patch from the Set list.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{2}
\tightlist
\item
  Select a destination \textbf{Universe} from the Universes list. The universe grid will be shown in the Universes view.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{3}
\tightlist
\item
  Select the \textbf{Fixtures} you wish to patch from the Fixtures list.
  👉 Multiple fixtures may be selected by swiping ⤴ or ⤵ in the Fixtures list.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{4}
\item
  \textbf{Press and Hold} the fixtures, they will turn into floating icons.
\item
  Drag the fixture icons from the Fixtures view onto the Universe View grid. All associated DMX addresses will turn green. Drop the fixture icon on the desired DMX start address
\item
  The fixtures are now patched.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Make multiple selections of fixtures using the keypad:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Type a range of fixtures using the keypad - the fixture selection will be displayed numerically in the \textbf{Selection} box.
\item
  \textbf{Press and Hold} anywhere in the Selection box - a fixture icon will appear.
\item
  Drag the icon to the Universe View grid and drop it at the destination start address.
\item
  The range of fixtures is now patched.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Multi-Patch using Drag and Drop:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Drag and drop the same fixture to multiple DMX offsets.
\end{enumerate}

👉 All types of fixtures may be multi-patched but caution should be used when multi-patching multi-parameter fixtures.

\textbf{Unpatch fixtures using drag and drop:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{Press and Hold} any patched DMX offset - The DMX addresses for the fixtures patched to it will turn green and a fixture icon will be shown.
\item
  Drag the selected block of fixtures towards the bottom of the screen - a waste bin icon will appear.
\item
  Drag the block until the icon is over the waste bin and drop the fixtures.
\item
  The fixtures are now unpatched.
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{modify-patch}{%
\subsubsection{Modify Patch}\label{modify-patch}}

Once patched fixtures can be modified using keypad syntax or using the \textbf{Patch Toolbar}.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{CLEAR PATCH\} - Opens the Clear Patch popup.
\item
  \{DELETE FIXTURE\} - Opens the Delete Fixtures popup.
\item
  \{CLONE\} - Opens the Fixture Cloning popup.
\item
  \{REPLACE\} - Opens the Device Replacement popup.
\item
  \{ADD FIXTURE\} - Opens the Create And Patch popup.
\item
  \{PATCH\} - Opens the Patch Fixture popup.
\item
  \{RENUMBER\} - Opens the Renumber Fixture popup.
\item
  \{AUTO GENERATE\} - Opens the Auto Generate popup.
\item
  \{FIXTURE CALIBRATION\} - Opens the Fixture Calibration popup.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Modify the patch using keypad syntax:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]} \{CLEAR PATCH\} - From the Editor toolbar.
\item
  {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}DELETE{]} - Only works in the Patch Page.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{address-test}{%
\subsubsection{Address Test}\label{address-test}}

Sometimes, especially in theatrical environments, it is useful to check if a DMX channel is empty or not. This can be done from the console by enabling the Address Test function on the header of the Universe View.

In Address Test mode each selected channel is visualized with a yellow background and it is forced to jump at full intensity.

Many channels can be selected by dragging or through multiple touches.

{[}RESET{]} releases the channels and disables the Address Test function.

Once the function is disabled all the channels come back to their normal state.

\hypertarget{DMX-Properties-5.4}{%
\subsection{DMX Properties}\label{DMX-Properties-5.4}}

The DMX Properties is divided in 4 sections corresponding to the 4 output types supported:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Local Output: the physical DMX port embedded in the console.
\item
  VC's: Compulite proprietary protocol for DMX over Ethernet.
\item
  ArtNet: The most diffused protocol for DMX over Ethernet used for lighting fixture control.
\item
  sACN: The most diffused protocol for DMX over Ethernet used for lighting fixture control and communication with other equipment related to show lighting (such as Tracking systems).
\end{itemize}

Most of the options and configuration properties are common to all the protocols, but a few of them are specific to the selected protocol and will be grayed out or not available.

Vibe is capable of transmitting more than one protocol at a time on the same Ethernet port.

To save bandwidth it is also possible to enable only the universes for each protocol that are required.

Vibe has separate universe patches for Local, VCs, Art-Net, and sACN.

By default, on each protocol universes are patched on a 1:1 basis.

It is possible to reroute universe output destinations.

DMX can be remapped as needed to anyone of the supported 256 DMX universes.

The Vibe console is licensed to output from 64 up to 256 source universes, but these universes may be freely rerouted to any destination universe from 0 ⟶ 256 as long as the total amount of universes does not exceed the purchased license.

\textbf{Available Universes}

In the bottom left of the popup, a red framed box informs about the amount of universes that the system can output on each protocol.

This number is based on the connected devices, summing each device universes to reach the system's total amount:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Vibe PC software by default enables up to 64 input and output universes on VC's protocol and up to 1 universe on ArtNet and ACN protocols.
\end{itemize}

👉 If no PC Wing is connected to the Vibe PC, then the VibePC works in Demo Mode and the ArtNet and ACN protocols will get random 10 seconds blackouts, advised by a Red box on the toolbar. VC's protocol is not affected by the random blackouts.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Vibe consoles by default enable up to 64 input and output universes.
\end{itemize}

👉 { Vibe consoles support up to 256 universes output; to expand the 64 default universes the console needs to install a \textbf{Universe Licence}. Not implemented yet.}.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Vibe consoles Local Universes depend on the quantity of embedded DMX ports.
\item
  Vibe (and Vector) PC Wings add up to 16 input and output universes on ArtNet and ACN protocols.
\item
  ePort8 Nodes add up to 8 input and output universes on ArtNet and ACN protocols.
\item
  ePort41 Nodes add up to 5 input and output universes on ArtNet and ACN protocols.
\item
  ePort2 Nodes add up to 2 input and output universes on ArtNet and ACN protocols.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{local-output}{%
\subsubsection{Local Output}\label{local-output}}

\textbf{Enable Local output}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Open the \{DMX SETTINGS\} from Vibe Menu \textgreater{} Settings or tap the DMX Settings button - The DMX Properties popup will open.
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Under the Protocol heading, tap \{Local Output\} - The selection will turn red.
\item
  Under Broadcast Options tap \{Out\} - The status indicator light will turn green. The console will now be outputting the selected protocol on the EtherCON Data ports.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Reroute Console Universes:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Under the \textbf{Source Universe} header, scroll the list to the source console universe number you wish to route. Once tapped, the selection will turn red. A universe number may also be typed from the keypad.
\item
  Under the \textbf{Destination Universe} header, tap one of the cells in the destination universe grid. The source universe number will now be routed through the destination universe number.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Additional functions:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Clear a destination universe - Tap \{Universe \#\} in the destination grid. Tap \{Clear\}.
\item
  Clear all destination universes - Select the protocol to clear. Tap \{Clear All\}.
\item
  Reset default route for a single destination universe - Tap \{Universe \#\} Tap \{Config\}.
\item
  Reset all defaults for the selected protocol - Tap \{Config All\}.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Enable Input / Output}

Use the Broadcast Option keys to enable / disable the Input and the Output, to either receive or send the protocol.

When the green dot is shown on the key, the protocol is set as enabled. Tap apply to activate it.

\textbf{Remap Input / Output}

Toggle between In (Receive) and Out (Send) on the top of the view to either show the input or the output universes configuration in the destination universe box.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Open the \{DMX SETTINGS\} from Vibe Menu \textgreater{} Settings or tap the DMX Settings button - The DMX Properties popup will open.
\item
  In the Protocol column, Tap \{Local Output\}.
\item
  Under the Source Universe heading, tap the desired source universe number or once red type the universe number you wish to be the source.
\item
  Under the Local Output Destination Universe heading, tap one of the displayed local outputs - the source Universe will now be patched to the output.
\end{enumerate}

👉 Use the Clear All and the Config All keys to remap the protocol on a 1:1 basis.

\textbf{Change output to input}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Currently Vibe supports 2 inputs but multiple connectors may be assigned to the same input.
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap a patched output - Two additional options will light up near the Output Destination Universe box.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{Make Input 1\} - Makes the selected outputs into Input 1. The Physical connector display will turn white to indicate it is now an input.
\item
  \{Make Input 2\} - Makes the selected outputs into Input 2. The Physical connector display will turn white to indicate it is now an input.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Change input to output}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Tap a patched input - an additional Make Output option will light up near the Output Destination Universe box.
\item
  Tap \{Make Output\} - The Connector will now output DMX.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{DMX refresh rate}

Sometimes older equipment cannot handle the higher refresh rates, so it may be reduced with the DMX Max Rate wheel. It is not recommended to go below 28Hz as some flickering may be observed.

👉 If VibePC is used to control an external software, such as a lighting visualizer, running on the same computer, the Local Outputs are used to communicate the VC's protocol to the other software using the loopback IP address 127.0.0.1.

\subsubsection{VC}\label{vc}}

\end{itemize}

\textbf{Enable Input / Output}

Use the Broadcast Option keys to enable / disable the Input and the Output, to either receive or send the protocol.

When the green dot is shown on the key, the protocol is set as enabled. Tap apply to activate it.

\textbf{Remap Input / Output}

Toggle between In (Receive) and Out (Send) on the top of the view to either show the input or the output universes configuration in the destination universe box.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Open the \{DMX SETTINGS\} from Vibe Menu \textgreater{} Settings or tap the DMX Settings button - The DMX Properties popup will open.
\item
  In the Protocol column, Tap \{VC's\}.
\item
  Under the Source Universe heading, tap the desired source universe number or once red type the universe number you wish to be the source.
\item
  Under the Local Output Destination Universe heading, tap one of the displayed local outputs - the source Universe will now be patched to the output.
\end{enumerate}

👉 Use the Clear All and the Config All keys to remap the protocol on a 1:1 basis.

\textbf{Change output to input}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Currently Vibe supports 2 inputs but multiple connectors may be assigned to the same input.
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap a patched output - Two additional options will light up near the Output Destination Universe box.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{Make Input 1\} - Makes the selected outputs into Input 1. The Physical connector display will turn white to indicate it is now an input.
\item
  \{Make Input 2\} - Makes the selected outputs into Input 2. The Physical connector display will turn white to indicate it is now an input.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Change input to output}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Tap a patched input - an additional Make Output option will light up near the Output Destination Universe box.
\item
  Tap \{Make Output\} - The Connector will now output DMX.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{DMX refresh rate}

Sometimes older equipment cannot handle the higher refresh rates, so it may be reduced with the DMX Max Rate wheel. It is not recommended to go below 28Hz as some flickering may be observed.

\textbf{Unicast}

By default the protocol transmission is Broadcast, if a Unicast or Multicast transmission needs to be set, tap on the Unicast Settings toggle key. It is possible to set specific IPs to send the output to. If there is a port configured to a specific IP it will not be sent broadcast.

The Unicast Settings allow to Add or Delete the IP addresses that will be used for the Unicast or Multicast transmission.

Any IP address may receive the whole protocol with all its universes, or only a part of it as defined in the ``From Port'' and ``To Port'' fields.

Ports that are not defined as unicast will be still sent as broadcast.

Each port can be configured to multiple IPs, more ports can be sent to the same IP.

\textbf{Configure Unicast:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Tap on the protocol in DMX Settings popup.
\item
  Toggle to \emph{Unicast Settings} to open the unicast popup.
\item
  Tap Add to add a new IP line or Delete to remove an existing IP
\end{enumerate}

\begin{quote}
On the first line -- ports 1 ⟶ 5 will be sent to IP 172.16.10.10
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
On the second line -- port 5 will be sent also to IP 172.16.10.11
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
On the third line -- ports 6 ⟶ 10 will be sent to IP 172.16.10.12
\end{quote}

\textbf{SubNet view}

ArtNet is designed in blocks of 16 universes.

The blocks starts at subnet 0 and increments by 1 every 16 universes.

The subnet number and fixture number in the subnet can be viewed by toggling \{Subnet View\}.

Enable the Subnet view through the dedicated key on the right of the destination universe box to visualize the subnet number instead of the universe number on the ArtNet universes.

\textbf{Unicast}

By default the protocol transmission is Broadcast, if a Unicast or Multicast transmission needs to be set, tap on the Unicast Settings toggle key. It is possible to set specific IPs to send the output to. If there is a port configured to a specific IP it will not be sent broadcast.

Any IP address may receive the whole protocol with all its universes, or only a part of it as defined in the ``From Port'' and ``To Port'' fields.

Ports that are not defined as unicast will be still sent as broadcast.

Each port can be configured to multiple IPs, more ports can be sent to the same IP.

\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item

\item

\item
  Tap Add to add a new IP line or Delete to remove an existing IP
\end{enumerate}

\begin{quote}
On the first line -- ports 1 ⟶ 5 will be sent to IP 172.16.10.10
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}

\end{quote}

\hypertarget{dmx-input}{%
\subsubsection{DMX Input}\label{dmx-input}}

\textbf{The following objects may be mapped to DMX inputs}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  DMX input mapped directly to the console's DMX outputs
\item
  DMX input mapped to Fixtures
\item
  DMX input mapped to Controllers
\item
  DMX input mapped to Macros
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Map a DMX output to a DMX input}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Press {[}ADDRESS{]} {[}\#{]}
\item
  Tap \{INPUT PATCH TO\} on the toolbar - Command line will say INPUT PATCH TO
\item
  Press {[}ADDRESS{]} {[}\#{]}
\item
  Press {[}STORE{]}
\end{enumerate}

\begin{quote}
{[}ADDRESS{]} {[}1{]} \{INPUT PATCH TO\} {[}ADDRESS{]} {[}1{]} {[}STORE{]} - When DMX 1 is received from an external source like a console, DMX 1 will directly output to the Vibe DMX output. No values will be shown in the live display as no actual parameters are involved.
\end{quote}

\textbf{Map a range of DMX outputs to a range of DMX inputs}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Press {[}ADDRESS{]} {[}\#{]} ⟶ {[}\#{]}
\item
  Tap \{INPUT PATCH TO\} on the toolbar - Command line will say INPUT PATCH TO.
\item
  Press {[}ADDRESS{]} {[}\#{]} ⟶ {[}\#{]}
\item
  Press {[}STORE{]}
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Map a Fixture to a DMX input}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Press {[}ADDRESS{]} {[}\#{]}
\item
  Tap \{INPUT PATCH TO\} on the toolbar - command line will say INPUT PATCH TO
\item
  Press {[}Fixture{]} {[}\#{]}
\item
  Press {[}STORE{]}
\end{enumerate}

\begin{quote}
DMX inputs will automatically be mapped to all the fixtures parameters starting at the specified address. {[}ADDRESS{]} {[}1{]} {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}1{]} (A fixture with 42 parameters) {[}STORE{]} will automatically match the 42 parameters to input addresses 1 → 42. The next fixture would have to be mapped starting at address 43.
\end{quote}

\textbf{Map a controller to a DMX input}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Press {[}ADDRESS{]} {[}\#{]}
\item
  Tap \{INPUT PATCH TO\} on the toolbar - Command line will say INPUT PATCH TO.
\item
  Press {[}PLAY-B{]} (Controller) - The Controller Selection popup will appear.
\item
  Choose the Controller Type, Page Number, and Controller number from the popup.
\item
  Press ENTER, or tap Apply to close the popup and complete the patch.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Map a Macro to a DMX input} - Macro will trigger at 50\%

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Press {[}ADDRESS{]} {[}\#{]}.{[}\#{]}
\item
  Tap \{INPUT PATCH TO\} on the toolbar - Command line will say INPUT PATCH TO.
\item
  Press {[}MACRO{]} {[}\#{]}
\item
  Press {[}STORE{]}
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Delete a DMX Input patch assignment}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Press {[}ADDRESS{]} {[}\#{]}
\item
  Tap \{INPUT PATCH TO\} on the toolbar - Command line will say INPUT PATCH TO
\item
  Press {[}DELETE{]}
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{dmx-inout-view}{%
\subsubsection{DMX In/Out View}\label{dmx-inout-view}}

DMX Output and Input view is meant to show in real time the output and input values of each DMX channel of the selected universe.

Use the view's menu to select the target output or input universe.

DMX values can be shown in numeric or in graphic visualization.

\hypertarget{Fixture-View-5.5}{%
\subsection{Fixture View}\label{Fixture-View-5.5}}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The fixture view lists all the created fixtures and their patch information.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Select a Set on the \textbf{Sets View} to filter the fixtures listed.
\item
  Use the Fixture\# column to select the fixtures and the {[}DESELECT{]} button to release the selection. Selected fixtures will be highlighted in yellow.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Renumber Fixtures}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap on the Set or on the Set number column of a fixture selection, or tap the \{RENUMBER\} key on the patch toolbar, to change the fixture Set and ID.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Rename Fixtures}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Tap on the name column of a fixture selection to change the fixture Name.
\item
  Renaming multiple fixtures at once is possible by using the \# at the end of the text.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Re-patch Fixtures}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap on the DMX Address column of a fixture selection, or tap the \{PATCH\} key on the patch toolbar, to change the fixture address.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Change Fixtures' Mode}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Tap on the Mode column of a fixture selection to change the fixture mode.
\item
  Different personalities of the same device need to be imported in the show.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Invert Pan/Tilt}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap on the Pan Invert, Tilt Invert, Pan/Tilt Swap columns of a fixture selection to change the fixture position parameters' behavior.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Address-Properties-5.6}{%
\subsection{Address Properties}\label{Address-Properties-5.6}}

The address view lists all the DMX addresses of the selected universe and their properties.

Each cell contains the properties of a DMX address and the icon of the parameter patched on it.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  To edit address properties, tap on the addresses to select them and then ``Address Properties'' on the header. The Address Properties popup will open.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  The following address properties can be edited:

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    \textbf{Proportional Level}: limits the highest value that the address can reach.
  \item
    \textbf{Base}: limits the lowest value that the address can reach.
  \item
    \textbf{Curve}: assigns to the address a specific curve. Default is a Fade In curve.
  \item
    \textbf{Grand Master}: defines if the address is under the Grand Master's control. Default is ON for the dimmer parameters and OFF for the other parameters.
  \item
    \textbf{Restore Defaults}: restores the default address properties.
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Park-5.7}{%
\subsection{Park}\label{Park-5.7}}

\textbf{Park an address:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  {[}ADDRESS{]} {[}\#{]} \{PARK\} - The Park popup will appear.
\item
  Set a DMX level from 0 ⟶ 255 using the virtual slider.
\item
  Tap Apply or press {[}ENTER{]} to close the popup and park the address.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Unpark an address:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  {[}ADDRESS{]} {[}\#{]} \{PARK\} - The Park popup will appear.
\item
  Tap \{Clear Park\}.
\item
  Tap Apply or press {[}ENTER{]} to close the popup and unpark the address.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Park a fixture:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]} \{PARK\} {[}STORE{]}
\end{enumerate}

Fixture will now be parked at the current values.

\textbf{Unpark a fixture:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]} \{PARK\} {[}DELETE{]}
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Park a parameter:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]} \{PARAMETER\} \{PARK\} {[}STORE{]}
\end{enumerate}

The Parameter will now be parked at the editor value.

\textbf{View parked fixtures and address:}

In the Exam view tap Patch and then Park to check all the parked addresses and fixtures.

\begin{quote}
{\textgreater{} The Grandmaster \textbf{does not} affect parked addresses and fixtures. }
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
{\textgreater{} The {[}B.O.{]} key \textbf{does} blackout parked addresses and fixtures. }
\end{quote}

\hypertarget{Clone-5.8}{%
\subsection{Clone}\label{Clone-5.8}}

Vibe has smart and advanced clone features.

The Fixture Cloning feature allows to copy all the stored data from a selection of fixtures to another one.

To open the fixture cloning popup, press the dedicated button in Patch page's Editor Toolbar.

Choose the order of source fixtures on the left column and the order of destination fixtures on the right column, then press Done.

Vibe will provide a smart fixture cloning keeping the fixtures' data as similar as possible.

Selection Rules:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  All source fixtures must be of the same type.
\item
  All destination fixtures must be of the same type.
\item
  Many fixtures can be cloned to many fixtures, but the amount must be the same.
\item
  It is possible to select 1 source and many destinations.
\end{itemize}

The fixture cloning operation is not reversible, so there is no undo once hitting the DONE button.

\textbf{Exchange Clone}

The Cloning option on the top can be set as a one sided or a two-sided clone.

A one-sided clone copies the stored data from the source to the destination fixtures only.

A two-sided clone, called Exchange Clone, makes a smart replacement of data from the source fixtures to the destination fixtures and vice versa.

\textbf{Advanced Clone}

The Advanced fixture cloning option on the top enables access to a pair of Next popup windows where the matching parameters can be filtered, forced, and customized by the user.

The next step shows the Parameter Selection window.

The cloning can be based on raw values, preserving the parameters' levels, or on smart values, preserving the fixture's functions. Raw values means that Vibe will match between parameter types and copy the raw value. Smart means that Vibe will try to find the real data information of the parameter and convert the values to match the destination fixture to look the same. Default is Smart.

On the right side there is a match table between the source and destination fixtures parameters. This table is dynamic and shows the data set from the left side. User can also change matching parameters manually, but this is only to very special cases and anyway it is not recommended. SMART will anyway overrule the table in case there is a parameter that is included in the smart.

The next step shows the Data window.

The Data window permits to filter the Groups, Libraries, Qlists, Scenes and Submasters, to apply the cloning only to the selected contents. Default is that all are selected.

NOTE: on the exchange option, these lists will show objects from both source and destination fixtures.

There is an option to clone the calibration data, so the destination fixture will get the same location coordinates as the source fixture. Default is Disabled, because in real world 2 fixtures cannot have the same physical position, but can be enabled, for example in case of fixture switches or if working on a 3D visualizer trying different lighting setups on the same rig.

There is an option to clone the look ahead mask so if the source fixture is in the mask, then the destination fixtures will also be in the mask. Default is Enabled.

There is an option to choose what to do with the parameters of the destination fixtures that are not included in the clone because they were not matched or any other reason. Merge means to keep these values, overwrite will delete the values. Default is Overwrite.

\hypertarget{Replace-5.9}{%
\subsection{Replace}\label{Replace-5.9}}

Vibe has smart and advanced device replacement.

The Device replacement feature allows to replace a device with another one, keeping all the matching data stored.

To open the device replacement popup, press the dedicated button in Patch page's Editor Toolbar.

Choose the Source device on the left column and the new device from the Imported Devices on the right column, then press Done.

Vibe will provide a smart device replacement keeping the fixtures' data as similar as possible.

The device replacement operation is not reversible, so there is no undo once hitting the DONE button.

\textbf{Advanced Replacement}

The Advanced replacement option on the top enables access to a Next popup window where the matching parameters can be filtered, forced and customized by the user.

The next step shows the Parameter Selection window.

Each bank has its option to enable/disable. Enabled means it will be included in the Device replacement and disabled means it will not be included. Default is enabled.

The replacement can be based on raw values, preserving the parameters' levels, or on smart values, preserving the fixture's functions. Raw values means that Vibe will match between parameter types and copy the raw value. Smart means that Vibe will try to find the real data information of the parameter and convert the values to match the destination fixture to look the same. Default is Smart.

On the right side there is a match table between the source and destination fixtures parameters. This table is dynamic and shows the data set from the left side. User can also change matching parameters manually, but this is only to very special cases and anyway it is not recommended. SMART will anyway overrule the table in case there is a parameter that is included in the smart.

\hypertarget{Calibration-5.10}{%
\subsection{Calibration}\label{Calibration-5.10}}

Calibration Data give to the Vibe all the information regarding the fixture positioning on the stage, defined by X, Y, Z coordinates and Rotations around the axes, based on the point identified as stage center.

\{Fixture Calibration\} key on the Patch Toolbar opens a popup used to calibrate the fixtures through the XYZ coordinates, that may be either taken from a 3D Visualizer or from a real measurement.

When fixtures are properly calibrated it is possible to specify a stage point on Vibe xyz position picker to automatically point all the fixtures on it.

Another advantage of the fixture calibration is to speed up the creation of topographical views (Topo).

There are a few passages to get the calibration data of the fixtures from third party software, that depend on which visualizer is used.

\hypertarget{import-csv}{%
\subsubsection{Import CSV}\label{import-csv}}

Most of the 3D visualizers on the market feature a process to export a table of the fixture coordinates into a .csv file.

After exporting the CSV containing the fixtures' positioning coordinates from the visualizer, the .csv file table should be prepared in order to match the column's order to Vibe and import the Calibration Data.

\textbf{Instructions for Loading an External Fixture Calibration}

Before beginning, the user must prepare the calibration data in CSV format.

The file should include the following columns in the specified order:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  A - \# -- The fixture ID number in the Visualizer, or the Fixture Name, or leave empty (this field is ignored by the CSV Import process. It may contain anything that helps the user to match the source fixture list to the Vibe fixtures)
\item
  B - ID -- The fixture number in Vibe
\item
  C - X -- The X coordinate of the fixture's position in meters
\item
  D - Y -- The Y coordinate of the fixture's position in meters
\item
  E - Z -- The Z coordinate of the fixture's position in meters
\item
  F - RotX -- The X value of the fixture's rotation, in degrees
\item
  G - RotY -- The Y value of the fixture's rotation, in degrees
\item
  H - RotZ -- The Z value of the fixture's rotation, in degrees
\end{itemize}

Notes:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  The 1st line with column headers is not important, but the order of the columns must be exactly as listed above.
\item
  Empty fields are considered as 0 (zero) values.
\item
  Eventual additional columns must be deleted.
\end{itemize}

Once the CSV file is ready, move it to the following directory in the Vibe installation:

vibe/compulite/userdata/

To import the calibration data in Vibe:

Open a Patch page.

Click the ``Fixture Calibration'' button on the taskbar.

Click the ``Import Calibration Data'' button.

Use the file explorer to select your CSV file and click OK.

A popup will appear displaying the data read from the CSV file.

Rows where Vibe could not match the fixture number to an existing fixture will not be shown.

The data may be edited within the popup before applying it.

Click OK to apply the calibration.

\hypertarget{wysiwyg}{%
\subsubsection{Wysiwyg}\label{wysiwyg}}

As an alternative to the CSV import, calibration data may be manually copied from Wysiwyg.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  On Vibe, go to {[}PATCH{]} page and tap on \{FIXTURE CALIBRATION\}.
\item
  Tap on \{Stage\} button and set the stage dimensions.
\item
  On Wysiwyg, specify the drawing origin point at the center of the stage. For this, it will be necessary to work in the CAD tab using the TOP view. Press on the top left corner (that connects the vertical ruler and the horizontal ruler -- (see {Blue Frame}) and select the option of 'Mover Ruler (Set View Origin)ʼ.
\item
  Select the center of your stage (see {Red Arrow} in the Picture)
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{4}
\tightlist
\item
  Go to `Data tab', press Option on the toolbar ⟶ Document Options ⟶ Draw Defaults - and change the units to metric.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{5}
\tightlist
\item
  Copy these values to the Fixture calibration table in Vibe:
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  the column X to the column X,
\item
  the column Y to the column Y,
\item
  the column Z to the column Z.
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{6}
\item
  After all values are in the Vibe fixture calibration table, select the X column (by tapping the header) and press {[}-{]}.
\item
  Fixtures are now calibrated.
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{capture}{%
\subsubsection{Capture}\label{capture}}

As an alternative to the CSV import, calibration data may be manually copied from Capture.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  On Vibe, go to {[}PATCH{]} page and tap on \{FIXTURE CALIBRATION\}.
\item
  Tap on \{Stage\} button and set the stage dimensions.
\item
  On Capture, place the center of the stage in the intersect point of the 2 Bold Axis, when the CAD displays a view from the Top
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{3}
\item
  Go to Tools ⟶ Options and change the measurements to metric.
\item
  Select a fixture in Capture to see the XYZ coordinates when the table is showing 'Selected Itemsʼ.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{5}
\tightlist
\item
  Copy these values to the Fixture calibration table in Vibe:
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  the column X to the column X,
\item
  the column Y to the column Z,
\item
  the column Z to the column Y.
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{6}
\item
  After all values are in the Vibe fixture calibration table, select the X column (by tapping the header) and press {[}-{]}, and select the Y column and press {[}-{]}.
\item
  Fixtures are now calibrated.
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{l8}{%
\subsubsection{L8}\label{l8}}

As an alternative to the CSV import, calibration data may be manually copied from L8.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  On Vibe, go to {[}PATCH{]} page and tap on \{FIXTURE CALIBRATION\}.
\item
  Tap on \{Stage\} button and set the stage dimensions.
\item
  On L8, go to ROOM environment and set the `World Center' to define the center of the stage. Eventually an object with zero-sized dimensions can be created and set as world center. Ensure that the measurement is set on metric.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{3}
\item
  Go to DMX environment. All the fixtures' coordinates are now based on the world center.
\item
  Select a fixture in L8 from the list on the left side of the screen to see the XYZ coordinates.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{5}
\tightlist
\item
  Copy these values to the Fixture calibration table in Vibe:
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  the column X to the column X,
\item
  the column Y to the column Z,
\item
  the column Z to the column Y.
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{6}
\tightlist
\item
  Fixtures are now calibrated.
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{vectorworks}{%
\subsubsection{VectorWorks}\label{vectorworks}}

As an alternative to the CSV import, calibration data may be manually copied from VectorWorks.

{Coming Soon.}

\hypertarget{depence}{%
\subsubsection{Depence}\label{depence}}

As an alternative to the CSV import, calibration data may be manually copied from Depence.

{Coming Soon.}

\hypertarget{Auto-Patch-5.11}{%
\subsection{Auto-Patch}\label{Auto-Patch-5.11}}

Vibe can automatically get the device list, fixture patch and calibration data, by loading and converting an MVR show file.

As an alternative to the MVR show import, Vibe integration with some visualizers allows to automatically get the fixtures' information by establishing a specific network connection with the third party software, that depends on which visualizer is used.

\hypertarget{wysiwyg-1}{%
\subsubsection{Wysiwyg}\label{wysiwyg-1}}

\textbf{Auto Patch from Wysiwyg} may be done by installing the Compulite - Wysiwyg driver.

First download the \textbf{Compulite driver for Wysiwyg} from: \url{http://www.compulite.com} and then install the driver:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Run the file CompuliteWYSIWYG\_DriverSetup.exe.
\item
  Read the licence terms and click I Accept to continue.
\item
  Choose the Compulite consoles for which the driver will install.
\item
  After reading the information displayed, tap INSTALL.
\item
  Tap FINISH.
\end{enumerate}

The computer running the Wysiwyg software must be configured to the same subnet as the Compulite console.

Use the Data port to connect to the PC running Wysiwyg (On Vibe PC use any Ethernet port).

Enable VC transmission on Vibe.

After completing the patch procedures in Wysiwyg, \textbf{establish the connection} between the console and the Wysiwyg application.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  In the Wysiwyg application, click the Live tab.
\item
  Open the Live menu.
\item
  Choose Device Manger.
\item
  Click New.
\item
  Search for your Console by using the search bar or by expanding the Consoles -\textgreater{} Manufacturer nodes and choosing Compulite.
\item
  Click Insert.
\item
  Click on the console and choose Properties. In the protocol field make sure ``Compulite VC'' is selected.
\item
  In the Address field, enter the console's IP address.
\item
  Bind Wysiwyg Universes to Vibe output ports. Under Universe, click to open drop selection and select a Wysiwyg universe. Do so for every port you wish to bind. Alternatively select multiple rows and click ``Assign'', then select a Wysiwyg universe to bind all selected ports.
\item
  Close the properties window.
\item
  Click Connect.
\item
  Verify that the status now shows Connected.
\item
  Click Close.
\end{enumerate}

A popup will show that a Wysiwyg client is now connected.

Use \textbf{Wysiwyg Auto Patch feature}:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  In the Wysiwyg application, click the Live Wysiwyg tab.
\item
  Open the Live menu.
\item
  Choose Auto Patch.
\item
  Click Send Patch.
\item
  Click Close, to close the dialog box.
\end{enumerate}

A popup on the Vibe screen will ask to accept the autopatch from Wysiwyg. Tap Apply.

A popup on the Vibe screen will ask to save the current show file.

A popup on the Vibe screen will show the matched devices and allows to match them in a different way or to built the unmatched ones as new devices.

When all the devices are matched, tap Apply and the convert process will start.

\hypertarget{capture-1}{%
\subsubsection{Capture}\label{capture-1}}

\textbf{Auto Patch from Capture} may be done via the CITP protocol.

It is possible to sync the show from Capture. In order to do that, set the Capture CITP settings to use Standard Multicast and select the relevant IP address.

In addition, make sure Vibe and Capture are on the same subnet.

After finishing preparing the show on Capture, go to Vibe, open the CITP popup, enable CITP and connect to Capture from the Visualizers tab.

Then \emph{Get Data} from Capture and Vibe will be ready to go.

\hypertarget{editor}{%
\section{Editor}\label{editor}}

👉 Understand the Vibe \textbf{Editor}, a simple concept and a direct syntax to manage a versatile fixture selection.

This chapter introduces to the editor, explaining the concept and syntax, then describing the ways of making fixture selections.

\textbf{Chapter index:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Concept-6.1}{6.1 Concept}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Syntax-6.2}{6.2 Syntax}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Selection-6.3}{6.3 Selection}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Groups-6.4}{6.4 Groups}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Concept-6.1}{%
\subsection{Concept}\label{Concept-6.1}}

The Vibe is an advanced lighting control system designed to simplify programming in all styles of Entertainment lighting.

\textbf{Maintain Selection}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  To avoid reselecting fixtures after a STORE command, Vibe maintains its selection on reset of the editor.
\item
  To clear the editor and the fixture selection {[}VIBE{]} (Shift) + {[}RESET{]} must be pressed.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Fixture Control}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  To simplify programming, Vibe uses an abstract layer that allows fixtures of different hardware types to be presented to the user in a universal way.
\item
  Fixtures are programmed on the \textbf{Editor} either by using the programming syntax with the HardKeys or the Smart Screen pickers and features.
\item
  The dedicated graphical ``Smart Screen'' presents global pickers for most parameters including:
\item
  Intensity picker for dimmer values and strobe effects
\item
  Position picker with P/T and XYZ Trackpad
\item
  HSV color picker (Hue, Saturation, Intensity)
\item
  Wheel picker for Gobo, Prism, Animation and Color wheels
\item
  Beam picker for Iris, Zoom, Focus and Frost effects
\item
  Blade picker for framing system
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Real World Units}

Where possible picker values are displayed in ``Real World'' units

\begin{itemize}
\item
  XYZ P/T in degrees
\item
  Strobe in HZ
\item
  Color in HSV 0-360 degrees
\item
  Zoom in degrees
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Virtual Parameters}

To make it easier to work with fixtures using different color systems and XYZ tracking systems, Vibe creates virtual parameters that may not exist in the physical fixture.

Some examples are:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Virtual Intensity for controlling LED fixtures that do not have a dimmer parameter.
\item
  Virtual HSV as a common denominator for all color mixing systems of Multi-color fixtures, such as CMY, RGB, RGBA, RGBW, RGBACL, \ldots{}
\item
  Virtual XYZ coordinates for calibration of fixtures that only have physical Pan/Tilt parameters.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Color spaces}

Vibe works in two synchronized color spaces:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  HSV: Color for all types of fixtures can be set using the Color picker or the HSV parameter wheels. HSV virtual parameters are stored in Cues and Libraries just like physical parameters. Automatically CMY or RGB values (or any color mixing parameter of the fixture) will be displayed, but not directly referenced in cues using virtual HSV.
\item
  Color Mix (RGB, CMY, others): If color values are set by using the real color mixing parameters, physical values are stored in the cue and HSV values will be displayed but not directly referenced.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Position spaces}

Vibe works in two synchronized position spaces:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  XYV: Position for all types of fixtures can be set using the XYZ Position picker or the XYZ parameter wheels. XYZ virtual parameters are stored in Cues and Libraries just like physical parameters. Automatically Pan and Tilt values will be displayed, but not directly referenced in cues using virtual XYZ.
\item
  Pan/Tilt: If position values are set by using the real focusing parameters, physical values are stored in the cue and XYZ values will be displayed but not directly referenced.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Virtual crossfading}

By default the color and position crossfading is done by using the physical color parameters, but any Cue can be assigned to crossfade the virtual parameters instead.

\hypertarget{Editor-6.4}{%
\subsection{Editor}\label{Editor-6.4}}

All fixture selections and parameter modifications occur in the \textbf{Editor} (also called Programmer in other consoles).

The HardKeys panel, both virtual and physical, and the Smart Screen, are the most important components used to program the Editor.

The \textbf{Live View} shows all values from both the fixtures in the Editor and the output from Controllers (also called Playbacks or Executors on other consoles).

Color coding is used to distinguish between values coming from the controllers and values in the Editor.

The Editor priority is always higher than the Playback priority, so any parameter moved in the Editor will overcome the values coming from any controller.
Release the parameter or the fixture from the Editor to get back to the controller's output values.

\hypertarget{hardkeys}{%
\subsubsection{HardKeys}\label{hardkeys}}

The Editor Hardkeys are the buttons on the Vibe Panel use to program.

Vibe is a touch-based system, allowing efficient fixture manipulation through its smart on-screen features, but it is also possible to program it in a traditional syntax based way by pressing the hardkeys in the proper sequence.

As all the other panel's physical components (buttons and controllers), also the Hardkeys may be added to the layout by using the \textbf{Virtual HardKeys} View.

\hypertarget{tracking-qonly}{%
\subsubsection{Tracking \& Qonly}\label{tracking-qonly}}

\textbf{Tracking Mode}

Vibe by default is a tracking console and follows the conventions of modern show lighting.

Vibe also has a special track feature called \textbf{SKIP}: if cues are modified using \textbf{SKIP}, instead of \textbf{Qonly}, the relationship with the original hard value is maintained and changes to the original value skip over the modification and preserve the track.

\textbf{Qonly Mode}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Modifications, insertions and deletions will be done Qonly by default.
\item
  Move fades will still be valid.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Non-Tracking or Compulite Mode}

Vibe can also program without tracking, by applying the Compulite Mode.

Using Compulite Mode the defaults are changed to:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Tracking turned off.
\item
  Editor is not cleared after a Store.
\item
  After Reset, Editor values mantain their last position instead of returning to home values.
\item
  Controllers do not advance to the Cue after a Store.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{blind-editor}{%
\subsubsection{Blind Editor}\label{blind-editor}}

The Blind Editor is a parallel Editor used to create or modify objects without outputting values to the stage.

When using Blind Editor the {[}Blind{]} key flashes \textbf{{yellow}} and the Command Line turns \textbf{yellow}.

In Blind Editor the syntax and operations on the Objects, such as Store and Update, can be done exactly like in the Live Editor, but with no output on stage.
If a change is stored on content that is currently in Playback, then the output will be affected.

Vibe has two Blind Editor behaviors:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Blind acts as an independent editor, separate from the live editor.
\item
  Blind grabs the values from the live editor and viceversa. This is used to emulate the behavior of single editor consoles, where the main editor output is disabled while using the Blind Editor.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{To open the blind editor:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Press {[}BLIND{]} to open the Blind Editor empty, or as leaved at the last access.
\item
  Press {[}VIBE{]} + {[}BLIND{]} to open the Blind Editor in single editor mode. The Live Editor contents are copied to the Blind Editor.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{To exit the blind editor:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Press {[}BLIND{]} to close the Blind Editor and get back to the Live Editor. The contents in the Blind Editor are kept.
\item
  Press {[}VIBE{]} + {[}BLIND{]} to exit the Blind Editor in single editor mode. The Blind Editor contents are copied and merged to the Live Editor. The contents in the Blind Editor are also kept.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Syntax-6.2}{%
\subsection{Syntax}\label{Syntax-6.2}}

Vibe uses a hybrid command syntax, called action syntax, that does not need an ``enter'' command to complete the sequences

For keypad command line driven operations the command completes the sequence, the logic is:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Source Object, Filter, Options, Command (Action Completes)
\end{itemize}

{[}Group 1{]} {[}Full{]} {[}Library 2 (Position) 1{]} {[}Cue 1{]} {[}Store Options{]} \{Red + White\} {[}Store{]}

For softkeys and controllers driven operations touching the object completes the sequence, the logic is:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Source Object, Filter, Command, Options, Destination (Object Completes)
\end{itemize}

\{GROUP\} {[}1{]} {[}Full{]} {[}LIBRARY{]} \{Position\} {[}3{]} {[}Store{]} {[}Store Options{]} \{Red Only\} press any button on {[}Controller 1{]}

The \textbf{Command Line} is the area where commands appear as they are entered.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  It is located on the Toolbar on the side of the {[}VIBE{]} menu.
\item
  When in BLIND Mode the command line is bounded in yellow.
\end{itemize}

The \textbf{Selection Line} is the area where the active fixture selection appears.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  It is located on the Smart Screen.
\item
  It shows the current fixture selection in the selection order.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Selection-6.3}{%
\subsection{Selection}\label{Selection-6.3}}

In this guide FIXTURE will be the default SET, but any SET (Channel, Spot, Matrix, Server, User Sets) could also be applicable.

Default SET is changed by double pressing the appropriate SET key

Dimmer is implied, but parameter would normally be specified after {[}+ ⟶ -{]} and before value.

\textbf{{[}CE{]}} will backspace the command line one character at a time.

\textbf{{[}Shift CE{]}} will clear the command line to the beginning and put the system in an idle state.

Fixtures are selected using the \textbf{{[}FIXTURE{]}} key on the keypad.

Fixtures on different Sets may be selected using the syntax \textbf{{[}SET{]} {[}\#{]} {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]}}.

Fixtures may be stored in Groups and recalled using the \textbf{{[}GROUP{]}} key.

Fixtures may also be interactively selected via the Live, the Live Parameter and the Topo views.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{{[}+{]} {[}-{]}} keys are valid with Fixture selections and Groups.
\item
  \textbf{{[}FIXTURE{]}} . (dot) reselects the last selection.
\item
  \textbf{{[}FIXTURE{]}} reselects all fixtures in the Editor.
\item
  \textbf{{[}CE{]}} backspaces the command line one character at a time, but currently will not go back one command at a time.
\item
  \textbf{{[}VIBE{]} + {[}CE/CLEAR{]}} clears the command line.
\item
  \textbf{{[}RELEASE{]}} removes the parameters from the Editor.
\item
  First press releases the active parameters (last adjusted) for selected fixtures.
\item
  Second press releases all remaining parameters for selected fixtures.
\item
  \textbf{{[}DESEL{]}} deselects all fixtures but maintains the values in the Editor.
\end{itemize}

\begin{quote}
This function is usually used to protect the operator from accidentally adjusting parameters in the Editor when they are temporarily captured. It may also be used to ``idle'' the command line to allow {[}TEXT{]} \{SK\} function while fixtures are still in the Editor.
\end{quote}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{{[}RESET{]}} releases all parameters from the Editor.
\item
  \textbf{{[}VIBE{]} + {[}RESET{]}} releases all parameters from the Editor and deselects all the fixtures.
\item
  \textbf{{[}NEXT{]} / {[}PREV{]}} increments or decrements fixtures in a selection. When used with a Group, will be confined to the fixtures in the Group.
\item
  \textbf{{[}VIBE{]} + {[}NEXT{]} / {[}PREV{]}} increments or decrements fixtures in a selection, keeping the fixtures' amount and distance.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Command logic and shortcuts}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  {[}Fixture{]} \# {[}+ ⟶ -{]} {[}Full{]} / {[}Zero{]} (@100 and @00).
\item
  {[}Fixture{]} \# {[}+ ⟶ -{]} {[}@{]} enter value. Two digits are required to complete value entry.
\item
  {[}Fixture{]} \# {[}+ ⟶ -{]} {[}@{]} {[}@{]} will set values to Full.
\item
  {[}Fixture{]} \# {[}+ ⟶ -{]} {[}@{]} single value, {[}ENTER{]}.
  \textgreater{} {[}Fixture{]} {[}1⟶ 6{]} {[}@{]} {[}5{]} {[}ENTER{]} will set a value of 50\%.
  \textgreater{} {[}Fixture{]} {[}1⟶ 6{]} {[}@{]} {[}7{]} {[}ENTER{]} will set a value of 70\%.
\item
  Pressing {[}ENTER{]} at the end of value input is optional and does not affect the sequence. In most cases, it will simply terminate the command line.
\item
  {[}Fixture{]} \# will jump the \textbf{live display} screen to the specified fixture if the fixture is not visible on the screen. (Fixture Jumping must be enabled in the \textbf{Live Display, \{Tools\}} (Wrench icon) popup under the \textbf{Behavior} Tab
\item
  {[}Fixture{]} \# {[}+ ⟶ -{]} {[}@{]} {[}+{]} {[}value{]} or {[}@{]} {[}-{]} {[}value{]} will make a \textbf{relative} change to the stage value.
  \textgreater{} \{If the stage value is 50, @ + 10 will set the value to 60, @ - 30 would set the value to 20\}.
\end{itemize}

The rules for single digit plus Enter will also apply.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  {[}Fixture{]} \# {[}⟶ {]} \#, Parameter, {[}@{]} \{value{]} {[}⟶{]} {[}value{]} will \textbf{fan} the range. (if no parameter is specified, dimmer/intensity will be fanned) 0, and 100\% are also valid as well as {[}Zero{]} and {[}Full{]}.
\item
  {[}Fixture{]} \# {[}⟶ {]} \# Parameter, {[}@{]} {[}value{]} {[}⟶{]} {[}⟶{]} {[}value{]} will \textbf{mirror} fan the range. \{1➡⟶ 100 would produce 0, 10, 20, 30, 40, 50, 40, 30, 20, 10, 0\}
\item
  Fixture \textbf{Dot} = Restore last. (There's a toolbar equivalent)
\item
  Fixture ⟶ = Reselect Editor. (There's a toolbar equivalent)
\item
  Fixtures and Parameters may be deselected using {[}RELEASE / DESEL{]} Key. This leaves values in the editor but not under the control of the wheels.
\item
  {[}GROUP{]} \# selects all fixtures that are recorded into the group. *The typed fixture order is preserved in a recorded group.
\item
  With groups, parameters may be fanned via syntax as above, but their recorded order will be considered.
\item
  Control without having to press Shift, Reset, which clears the values in the Editor.
\item
  \{LIBRARY\} \{LIBRARY\}, will select and enter values from a library into the Editor. This is especially useful when using position libraries as groups with positions.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{selection-tools}{%
\subsubsection{Selection Tools}\label{selection-tools}}

\textbf{Selection} button allows to split the fixtures' selection.

Press {[}SELECTION{]} on Prog and Play pages' toolbar to access the features.

Press {[}RESTORE{]} to get back to the original selection.

Press {[}LOCK{]} to make the current selection as the new base selection.

Press {[}CE/CLEAR{]} to return to the upper toolbar's menu.

Selection toolbar has the following Selection Tools.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{ODDS\} - Absolute odd number in the range
\item
  \{EVENS\} - Absolute even numbers in the range
\item
  \{2/1\} - Grouping of 2 starting at the first fixture of the range (relative odd numbers)
\item
  \{2/2\} - Grouping of 2 starting at the second fixture of the range (relative even numbers)
\item
  \{3/1\} - Grouping of 3 starting at the first fixture of the range
\item
  \{4/1\} - Grouping of 4 starting at the first fixture of the range
\item
  \{ / \} - Grouping of \#/\# starting at the \# fixture of the range
\end{itemize}

\begin{quote}
{[}Fixture{]} {[}1{]} {[}⟶{]} {[}14{]} \{Selection Tools\} {[}3{]} \{/\} {[}1{]} - would select fixtures 1,4,7,10,13
as soon as the \{/\} is pressed EVERY 3/1 will be displayed on the selection line.
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
{[}Fixture{]} {[}1{]} {[}⟶{]} {[}14{]} \{Section Tools\} {[}3{]} \{/\} {[}2{]} - would select fixtures 2,5,8,11,14
as soon as the \{/\} is pressed EVERY 3/2 will be displayed on the selection line.
\end{quote}

\hypertarget{order-tools}{%
\subsubsection{Order Tools}\label{order-tools}}

\textbf{Order} button allows to make changes to the fixtures' selection order.

Press {[}ORDER{]} on Program and Playback pages' toolbar to access the features.

Press {[}RESTORE{]} to get back to the original selection.

Press {[}CE/CLEAR{]} to return to the upper toolbar's menu.

Order toolbar has the following Ordering Tools.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{INVERT\} - Inverts the selection order linearly
\item
  \{MIRROR IN\} - Mirrors the selection order from the extremes to the center
\item
  \{MIRROR OUT\} - Mirrors the selection order from the center to the extremes
\item
  \{TRANSPOSE\} - Transposes the raws and columns of the selection order (useful for matrixes)
\item
  \{PATTERN\} - Opens the Pattern popup with more advanced reordering tools
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Transpose}

Transpose feature is used to reorder fixture selection based on a specified offset.
This is particularly useful for creating interesting effects and fanning.

\begin{quote}
Fixture 1 ⟶ 12 \{Transpose\} 2 {[}ENTER{]} = 1+3+5+7+9+11+2+4+6+8+10+12
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
Fixture 1 ⟶ 12 \{Transpose\} 3 {[}ENTER{]} = 1+4+7+10+2+5+8+11+3+6+9+12
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
Fixture 1 ⟶ 12 \{Transpose\} 4 {[}ENTER{]} = 1+5+9+2+6+10+3+7+11+4+8+12
\end{quote}

\textbf{Pattern}

Pattern feature is used to edit the selection order by using smart functions on a graphical interface.
The Pattern popup also includes all the other Order Tools.
It can be used prior to entering the Advanced Effects editor to create more complex block patterns that are available using the basic \{Block Of\} and \{Sub-Block Of\} key in the effects editor.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Make a fixture selection
\item
  Tap \{Pattern\} on the Editor Toolbar - The Pattern popup will open.
\end{enumerate}

👉 {This is not an effect even though it is a dynamic display.}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{2}
\item
  Tap the indicator at the top of the displayed fixtures. - it turns white.
\item
  Select a number under the Block Size header.
\item
  Tap the \{Spread\} icon. - the columns will expand to show the new fixture order.
\item
  If desired make further order changes under the Offset Header and tap \{offset\} icon.
\item
  Make further modifications or reset the selection with the \{Reset\} \{Invert\} \{Transpose\} and \{Invert Columns\} keys.
\item
  Once finished, exit the popup by pressing Apply or {[}ENTER{]}.
\item
  Open the Advanced Effects editor and program the desired effect.
\end{enumerate}

👉 Simple Block and Sub Block groupings may be created in both Effects Editors with no need of using order tools, but the pattern feature allows more advanced blocks and groupings.

\hypertarget{Groups-6.4}{%
\subsection{Groups}\label{Groups-6.4}}

Groups are objects used to speed up the fixture selections, stored into the Groups Library.

A Group allows to recall all the fixtures included in it by tapping on the softkey or pressing {[}GROUP{]} {[}\#{]}.

👉 {The order that fixtures are selected is stored in the group.}.

\textbf{Store Groups to SKs:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}+ ⟶ -{]} {[}STORE{]} tap desired \{Group Softkey\} - it does not matter if levels are set, they will be ignored.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Label SK Groups:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Directly after storing a group, start typing from the keyboard and a Text Entry popup will open.
\item
  At any time, pressing the {[}TEXT{]} key followed by tapping the SK will open the Text Entry popup.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Store Groups via keypad syntax:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}+ ⟶ -{]} {[}\#{]} {[}GROUP{]} {[}\#{]} {[}STORE{]}.
\item
  Pressing the {[}TEXT{]} key after any Group store will open the Text Entry popup.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Modify Groups using Group Settings popup:}

Range text labeling may be done via this method.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Using keypad, press {[}GROUP{]} {[}\#{]} {[}Settings{]} - the Group Settings pop-up will open.
\item
  Using SKs, tap the source \{GROUP\} SK and then press {[}Settings{]} key.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Store Groups from existent Groups:}

A new Group may be created from existing Groups by selecting multiple Groups using Group SKs or via keypad.

Press {[}STORE{]} then tap destination \{Group\} SK or Press {[}Group{]} {[}\#{]} {[}STORE{]} to create the new Group.

The new Group will not be linked nor referenced to the original Groups.

\textbf{Groups may be used to release fixtures:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  With keypad, {[}GROUP{]} {[}\#{]} {[}RELEASE{]}
\item
  With SKs, \{Group \#\} \protect\hyperlink{release}{Release}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Release a fixture from a Group Method 1:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  With keypad, Fixture \# {[}+ ⟶ -{]}, {[}GROUP{]}, \#, {[}STORE{]}, Group popup appears, select \{Release\}
\item
  With SKs, Fixture \# {[}+ ⟶ -{]}, \protect\hyperlink{store}{STORE}, \{Group SK\}, Group popup appears, select \{Release\}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Release a fixture from a Group Method 2:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  {[}GROUP{]} {[}\#{]} {[}EDIT{]}, {[}UPDATE{]} key flashes red ({[}EDIT{]} tap \{GROUP SK\} is also valid)
\item
  {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}RELEASE{]}
\item
  Press {[}UPDATE{]} to complete the sequence
\end{itemize}

\textbf{To Delete a Group:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  With keypad, {[}GROUP{]} \# {[}DELETE{]}
\item
  With SKs, \{GROUP\} {[}\#{]} {[}DELETE{]}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{program}{%
\section{Program}\label{program}}

👉 \textbf{Program} the Vibe: all the parameters are easily controllable with the smart pickers and efficient powerful features.

This chapter deals with using the editor and the smart screen for fixture manipulation and parameter control.

\textbf{Chapter index:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Parameters-7.1}{7.1 Parameters}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Smart-Screen-7.2}{7.2 Smart Screen}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Editor-Features-7.3}{7.3 Features}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Live-7.4}{7.4 Live}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Parameters-7.1}{%
\subsection{Parameters}\label{Parameters-7.1}}

Parameters are any feature of a device that may be manipulated and stored into an object.

Examples of parameters are:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Dimmer
\item
  Pan/Tilt
\item
  Cyan/Yellow/Magenta
\item
  Zoom/Focus
\item
  Gobo Wheel
\item
  Blade Rotation
\end{itemize}

To control properly any fixture, the Vibe device needs to be built according to the DMX chart of the fixture's parameters, that is usually reported in the manufacturer's user manual.

\hypertarget{param-banks}{%
\subsubsection{Param Banks}\label{param-banks}}

Vibe organizes the fixtures' parameters under 6 standard \textbf{Banks} and 2 special Banks:

Standard Banks are:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Intensity

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    for params related to the power and rate of the light output
  \item
    typical parameters are Dimmer, Shutter, Strobe
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Position

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    for params related to the movement and focusing
  \item
    typical parameters are Pan, Tilt, Move speed
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Color

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    for params related to the light's colors and tints
  \item
    typical parameters are Red, Green, Blue, White, Cyan Magenta, Yellow, CTC
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Beam

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    for params related to the angle and size of the light beam
  \item
    typical parameters are Zoom, Focus, Iris, Prism
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Image

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    for params related to the pictures that may be applied to the light beam
  \item
    typical parameters are Gobo wheel, Gobo rotation, Animation wheel
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Shape

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    for params related to the framing system that cuts the light output
  \item
    typical parameters are Blade depth, Blade angle, Blade rotation
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

Special Banks are:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Control

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    a param that sends ``real time'' messages to fixtures control functions
  \item
    typical functions are Lamp On, Lamp Off, Reset
  \item
    it is a special bank because its parameters are not stored in cues, scenes and libraries
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Media

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    a bank that collects specific features to control media servers
  \item
    it is a special bank because its parameters are actually included in the standard banks
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{virtual-params}{%
\subsubsection{Virtual Params}\label{virtual-params}}

Vibe automatically adds to the fixtures the relevant virtual parameters, that allow controlling the corresponding real parameters in a smart and effective way.

Virtual controls are also mapped to the 4 physical wheels.

Virtual parameters are the following:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Virtual Dimmer}: assigned to fixtures that don't have a specific dimmer parameter, usually RGB LEDs, it controls the light output intensity by adjusting the color values proportionally.
\item
  \textbf{Virtual HSI}: assigned to fixtures with any kind of color mixing system, it's a set of 3 params (Hue, Saturation, Intensity) that controls the color output by adjusting the real color values.
\end{itemize}

👉 It comes particularly useful when a fixture needs to be switched with another having a completely different color mixing system, for example an CMY to an RGBA.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Virtual XYZ}: assigned to fixtures with Pan/Tilt parameters, it's a set of 3 params (VX, VY, VZ) that controls the focusing by adjusting the position values. If based on a correct fixture \textbf{calibration} it allows to focus the fixtures on any stage point by simply tapping on the screen.
\end{itemize}

👉 It comes particularly useful when a fixture needs to be switched with another having a different Pan and Tilt range.

\hypertarget{fixture-manipulation}{%
\subsubsection{Fixture Manipulation}\label{fixture-manipulation}}

The manipulation of any fixture's parameter values may be set by using either Vibe's intuitive \textbf{Smart Screen} picker system or the conventional encoder wheels and the keypad method.

The switch between banks can be done either from the Smart Screen or through the combination SHIFT + \# where the \# represents the bank number.

\textbf{Standard programming:}

Programming may be done traditionally by using \{Bank\} keys and the 4 large push wheels.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Select the fixtures to program: Fixture \# {[}+ ⟶ -{]}
\item
  Select the \textbf{\{BANK\}} of the parameter you wish to adjust.
\item
  Banks may contain more than 4 wheels of parameters. Tap the \textbf{\{BANK\}} key or push the encoder wheel again to page to the next set of 4 parameter wheels.
\item
  Select the Param to adjust either on the touch screen or by pushing the correspondent encoder wheel. The wheel display turns red.
\item
  Rotate the encoder wheel of the parameter you wish to adjust, or directly add values with the keypad:
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Push a parameter wheel to select the parameter
\item
  \textbf{AT} will appear on the command line
\item
  Type a value from Zero ⟶ Full.
\end{itemize}

\begin{quote}
{\textbf{@} is only used for the dimmer, parameters other than dimmer do not use the @ key.}
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
{A Parameter value must be entered in 2 digits or one digit and the {[}Enter{]} key for whole numbers: 05, 50, 5 {[}ENTER{]} = 50.}
\end{quote}

\textbf{Interactive programming:}

Programming may be done interactively by using \{Bank\} keys and the 4 large push wheels.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Select the fixtures to program: Fixture \# {[}+ ⟶ -{]}
\item
  Select the \textbf{\{BANK\}} of the parameter you wish to adjust OR Drag the \textbf{Wheel Picker} on the right side of the \textbf{Smart Screen} and browse to the wheel and slot you want to program.
\item
  Tap the tab of the feature OR Tap the wheel slot that you wish to program. The context sensitive area on the \textbf{Smart Screen} will open with the appropriate picker.
\item
  Adjust the virtual controls through the options shown in the picker, that will be grayed out if not available in the current fixture selection.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Select multiple parameters:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
