  After making a fixture selection, toggle multiple parameters ON/OFF by tapping \{Parameter Keys\}, pressing encoder wheels, or toggling desired live display headers.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Manipulate all parameters in a bank:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  After making a fixture selection, hold {[}SHIFT{]} and tap a \{Parameter Bank Key\}.
\item
  Pressing and holding a \{Parameter Bank\} will popup three options:

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    \{HOME\} - Sets the bank's parameters to Home values.
  \item
    \{ALL PARAMS\} - Selects all parameters in the parameter bank.
  \item
    \{RELEASE\} - Releases the bank's parameter from the Editor.
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{editor-controls}{%
\subsubsection{Editor Controls}\label{editor-controls}}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Dimmer Wheel} is always controlling the selected fixtures' Dimmer params, independently from the currently selected bank and parameters.
\item
  \textbf{Trackball} is always controlling the selected fixtures' Pan and Tilt params, independently from the currently selected bank and parameters.
\item
  \textbf{{[}PREV{]} / {[}NEXT{]}} keys have the same functions of the Next and Prev hardkeys, but are positioned near the trackball and the dimmer wheel to make the adjustment of these params fast and comfortable.
\item
  \textbf{{[}X LOCK{]} / {[}Y LOCK{]}} keys lock the X or Y axis of the trackball, allowing to move individually Pan or Tilt instead of both.
\item
  \textbf{{[}RES{]}} - Sets global encoder resolution.
\end{itemize}

{Blue} = Low 8 bit,

{Green} = Medium 10 bit (Default)

{Red} = Higher 12 bit.

{Yellow} = High 16 bit.

{Magenta} = Highest 24 bit.

👉 Holding the {[}VIBE{]} key down while turning an encoder temporarily sets the encoder to high resolution 16 bit.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Encoder Wheels} the 4 interactive encoder wheels control the 4 params currently shown on the Smart Screen's wheels display and allow to select or deselect the parameters by pushing the wheels.
\end{itemize}

👉 Vibe uses a precision algorithm for encoders. Based on the starting resolution, the slower an encoder is turned, the more resolution it has.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Extra Wheels} the 4 additional interactive encoder wheels on the Smart Screen's right side control 4 additional params, that are dependent on the bank and selected fixture type.
\item
  Position, Image and Shape assign Focus and Zoom to the interactive wheels.
\item
  Color assigns Color Wheels and CTC to the interactive wheels.
\item
  Beam assigns Prism and Prism Rotate to the interactive wheels.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Smart-Screen-7.2}{%
\subsection{Smart Screen}\label{Smart-Screen-7.2}}

The 11.6'' multi-touch monitor directly above the 4 encoder wheels is referred to as the \textbf{Smart Screen.}

The \textbf{Smart Screen} is the ``brain'' of the Vibe, designed for an easy and intuitive lighting control experience.

Smart Screen is dedicated to displaying:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Context sensitive interactive bank, and parameter information.
\item
  Encoder wheel displays.
\item
  Playback information for the controllers that are directly above it.
\item
  Special popups for Libraries, Store options and Grab functions.
\item
  A simplified Effects editor.
\item
  Displays for Fan feature.
\end{itemize}

The Context sensitive area changes depending on the bank and fixture type.

Where possible, similar parameters from different fixture types will be presented to the user using Vibe's universal fixture model.

It is not necessary to memorize the manufacturer's specification of each fixture, as all fixtures are programmed in a similar consistent way.

\textbf{Smart Screen is divided into sections corresponding to the Parameter Banks.}

The Bank buttons on the Smart Screen, used to access the different parameters of the selected fixtures, have the following behavior:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Bank buttons 1-6 are always enabled. (i.e., to access libraries in the Context view)
\item
  If the Bank is enabled and active (there are parameters for the current selection in this bank), it has White text.
\item
  If the Bank is enabled and inactive (there are no parameters for the current selection in this bank), it has Black text.
\item
  If the Bank is disabled (not functional in the current selection), it is grayed out and not accessible.
\item
  If the Bank is currently selected, it has a colored background.
\end{itemize}

Parameters can be selected on the Smart screen by tapping on their box or by pressing the corresponding encoder wheel.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Currently selected params get a Red background
\item
  Editor activated params get a white background
\item
  Editor released params get no background (their value may be in idle state or come from an active controlled)
\item
  Synchronized params (virtual with physical and viceversa) get an orange text
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Quick Actions}

The Bank boxes and the Encoder param boxes on the bottom of the Smart screen, as well as the Extra wheels param boxes on the right side (when the ``Smart'' option is set) have some quick actions that can be activated with the long-click gesture.

\textbf{Banks} Quick Actions, referred to all the parameters of the bank, are:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Home (assign home value)
\item
  All Params (selection in editor)
\item
  Release (release from editor)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Params} Quick Actions, referred to the specific parameter on the encoder or extra wheel, are:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Home (assign home value)
\item
  Release (release from editor)
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{intensity}{%
\subsubsection{Intensity}\label{intensity}}

Intensity bank has parameters like Dimmer, Shutter and Strobe.

The dimmer level can be set either with the editor keys and wheels, and through the smart screen by using the virtual sliders and the predefined incremental steps.

The strobe and shutter level, mode and frequency can be set in the smart screen with the macros showing the selected fixtures' properties.

\textbf{Smart Screen - Intensity Bank}

If the selected shutter / strobe parameter's step has the possibility of a linear control of the rate, the virtual slider that controls the transition timing in Hz will be enabled.

\hypertarget{position}{%
\subsubsection{Position}\label{position}}

Position bank has parameters like Pan and Tilt.

The fixtures' position can be set either with the editor keys, wheels, and trackball, and through the smart screen by using the virtual sliders and the position picker.

\textbf{Smart Screen - Position Bank}

The position is calculated at the same time on Pan/Tilt values and on virtual XYZ values, based on the fixture's calibration on the stage.

Independently from the fixture's pan and tilt range, the virtual XYZ coordinates allow easy interchangeable operations between different kinds of fixtures.

In case of exchange with a fixture using a different pan and tilt range, the XYZ values will automatically recover the positions.

\textbf{Position Lock} functions allow to temporarily lock a movement direction to gain a more precise control of the unlocked ones.

This may be done on the panel by using the X-Lock, Y-Lock and Z-Lock buttons, which blink while enabled (when using the physical parameters, they will respectively lock Pan and Tilt).

This may also be done on the Smart Screen position pickers.

The Physical picker shows the Pan and Tilt Lock keys, while the Virtual picker shows the X,Y,Z Lock keys.

\hypertarget{color}{%
\subsubsection{Color}\label{color}}

Color bank has parameters like Color Mix (RGB, CMY, RGBW, \ldots) and Color Wheels.

The color level can be set either with the editor keys and wheels, and through the smart screen by using the virtual sliders in ``Params'' screen, the color picker in ``Virtual'' screen, or the color presets.

\textbf{Smart Screen - Color Bank}

The color mix is calculated at the same time on the color values and on virtual HSI values, based on the fixture's color mixing system.

Independently from the fixture's color mixing system, the color picker based on HSI system allows easy interchangeable operations between different kinds of fixtures.

In case of exchange with a fixture using a different color mixing system, the virtual HSI values will automatically recover the fixture's colors.

The color wheels are visualized in the interactive wheel picker on the right and can be selected with a touch. The selection opens the image picker to get complete control on the wheels' features.

The color mix can also be set by using the predefined preset combinations and Vibe's smart features.

\textbf{Filters Picker}

Color bank has a Lee and an Other filters section to assign colors based on the most used brands of gel filters.

Use the search field to find a specific filter by its numeric code.

\textbf{Kelvin Picker}

Color bank has a Kelvin picker for a fast adjust of any color temperature between 1000K and 20000K, linearly or by incremental steps.

This feature is recommended for working in television studios and photography environments.

\textbf{Vibe Mood Picker}

Color bank has a Vibe mood picker for a fast reproduction of a color taken from a picture.

This feature is recommended if working with assigned color palettes, for example in cinema or theater shows, to create color libraries directly from the scene's and costumes' pictures.

To get a color from an image:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Load an image file to the Vibe mood picker
\item
  Zoom the section of the target color
\item
  Select the color to assign to the fixture selection
\end{enumerate}

👉 The system provides an inbuilt database of 10 default images that may be used to check the Vibe Mood Picker and create Color Libraries based on the images' tones.

\hypertarget{beam}{%
\subsubsection{Beam}\label{beam}}

Beam bank has parameters like Focus, Zoom, Frost and Prism.

The beam parameters can be set either with the editor keys and wheels, and through the smart screen by using the virtual sliders and the beam picker.

\textbf{Smart Screen - Beam Bank}

The beam picker automatically enables the features available on the selected fixtures, offering an intuitive control system to set the mode, speed and type of transition related to the controlled parameter.

The prisms are visualized in the interactive wheel picker on the right and can be selected with a touch. The selection opens the wheel picker to get complete control on the prism wheels' features.

\hypertarget{image}{%
\subsubsection{Image}\label{image}}

Image bank has parameters like Gobo and Effect wheel.

The image parameters can be set either with the editor keys and wheels, and through the smart screen by using the virtual sliders and the image picker.

\textbf{Smart Screen - Image Bank}

The gobo wheels are visualized in the interactive wheel picker on the right and can be selected with a touch. The selection opens the image picker to get complete control on the wheels' features.

The image picker can be also accessed by pressing the Virtual button on image bank's screen.

On the image picker's right side, it is possible to visualize and select each fixture's wheel, on the left side can be set the indexing and rotation speed of the whole wheel or the single frame.

The interactive wheel picker shows all the graphic frames related to the selected wheel to allow fast interchange operations.

\hypertarget{shape}{%
\subsubsection{Shape}\label{shape}}

Shape bank has parameters like Blade movement and rotation.

The blades' depth and angle can be set either with the editor keys and wheels, and through the smart screen by using the virtual sliders and the blade picker.

\textbf{Smart Screen - Shape Bank}

The blade picker features a smart graphic control of the blade, independently from the fixture's blade system.

Each single blade can be moved, angled, and individually reset, the blade system can be rotated all together, and the reset options permit to reset the whole system or just the rotation parameter.

\hypertarget{wheel-picker}{%
\subsubsection{Wheel Picker}\label{wheel-picker}}

Wheel Picker is used to control any kind of wheel, such as Color, Prism, Gobo and Animation.
The values will be stored into libraries, scenes and cues according to the parameter's bank.

\textbf{Smart Screen - Wheel Picker}

The wheels adjusted with the wheel picker will reflect into values assigned to the wheel's parameters, usually the wheel's positioning and rotation and the wheel's frame selection, index and rotation.

Any wheel of the fixtures can be controlled through the wheel picker exactly in the same way, depending on the available possibilities, by using the Smart Screen's ``Smart'' visualization mode to visualize and select the frames' icons directly on the right side column.

\hypertarget{control}{%
\subsubsection{Control}\label{control}}

Control bank has parameters related to the fixture's functions, like Lamp on/off and Reset.

\textbf{Smart Screen - Control Bank}

The control bank is a special bank as its parameters are not stored in cues. Instead it sends ``real time'' messages to fixtures control functions.

\hypertarget{media}{%
\subsubsection{Media}\label{media}}

Media Bank is the picker designed for an intuitive \textbf{Media Server} programming, to handle media contents in a smart and easy way.

In order to see the content in this bank, users will need to create Media Server fixtures such as Green Hippo's Hippotizer or Arkaos.

All virtual controls are also mapped to the physical wheels for easy use.

The Media Bank has 5 types of pickers:

\textbf{Media}

This picker includes a quick way to trigger media files from the media server. It shows all the folders and files inside the server and lets the user decide which one to play.

By default all the folders and subfolders are visible, and once synchronizing the data with the media server through CITP the file's thumbnails will be shown.

\textbf{FX (Effect)}

This picker includes the effects available on the media server.

There are media servers with more than 1 effect engine (layer) and for each effect there will be a numbered FX Bank button.

The FX Bank includes a list of effects that are possible to trigger on the server and a list of parameters to control the effect.

The parameters will be dynamically changed according to the selected effect.

\textbf{Keystone}

This picker includes a graphic interface to control the keystoning of the server.

There are 4 points available for movement in order to control the position of each corner of the keystone control.

The selected point that is assigned to the physical wheels is marked in red.

\textbf{Generator}

This picker is specifically made for Hippotizer. On this bank, users can control the picture that is sent from the server's layer on the Generator engine.

The bank acts the same as the Media FX bank by selecting the Generator from the Generators list and then moving the Generator's parameters accordingly.

\textbf{Geometry}

This picker includes an interface to control most of the geometric functions of the media server.

There are options to control:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Aspect Mode of the layer
\item
  Zoom
\item
  Rotation, Indexed or Rotating with speed control
\item
  Aspect Ratio
\item
  Positioning, both X and Y
\end{itemize}

In addition there is a graphic preview to show the approximate result of the geometric parameters.

\hypertarget{settings-1}{%
\subsubsection{Settings}\label{settings-1}}

The main Smart screen settings, shown on the first level, refer to the content visualized on the right side columns.

The right side columns may be set in the following modes:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Smart - show the wheel's frames of the selected fixtures
\item
  Param Steps - show the stape of the active parameter
\item
  Extra Wheels - show the parameters assigned to the extra wheels
\end{itemize}

👉 While working on banks like Position, Image and Shape, it is very often needed to adjust the focus and zoom of the fixtures. Therefore it may be used the Extra Wheels option on the Smart screen to get these parameters directly available on the extra wheels, with no need to switch to the Beam bank to control them.

\textbf{Smart Screen's Settings} are accessed by tapping the \{Settings\} key.

The options are divided in 3 menus:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Format
\item
  Resolution
\item
  View
\end{itemize}

The \{Home\} key returns menus to the upper level.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Format} - Sets the view format for wheel displays.

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Percent
  \item
    Decimal
  \item
    Text
  \item
    Text + Percent
  \item
    Source PB
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Resolution} - Sets the wheels' and the virtual pickers' control resolution.

  👉 This may also be set by toggling the {[}Res{]} key over the trackball.

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    8 bit - Blue on {[}Res{]} Key
  \item
    10 bit - Green on {[}Res{]} key
  \item
    12 bit - Red on {[}Res{]} key
  \item
    16 bit - Yellow on {[}Res{]} key
  \item
    24 bit - Magenta on {[}Res{]} key
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{View} - Sets the behavior of the smart screen views, divided in Display Mode and Echoline Mode.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Display Mode}

\begin{verbatim}
-   Auto - Shows programming display when there are fixtures in the editor, and shows the controller displays for Aux Qkeys and Global Sliders when the editor is cleared. 

-   Programming - Maintains the programming display even with the editor released. 

-   Playbacks - Maintains the playback controller display even with fixtures in the editor.
\end{verbatim}

\textbf{Echoline Mode}

\begin{verbatim}
-   Fixture Selection - The line shows the current editor's fixture selection.

-   Syntax - The line shows the current syntax, like the main screen's toolbar.
\end{verbatim}

\textbf{Smart Screen's Preferences} are accessed by tapping the \{Smart\} \{Param Steps\} and \{Extra Wheels\} keys near the \{Settings\} key.

The preferences affect the visualization display in the right side column of the smart screen:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Smart} - All wheels for the selected fixture are shown in the interactive wheel picker area.
\item
  \textbf{Parameter Steps} - Pressing a parameter wheel or tapping the wheel display above it will display a picker list of all feature steps for the selected fixture.
\item
  \textbf{Extra Wheels} - Displays wheel information for the additional context sensitive encoder wheels.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Features-7.3}{%
\subsection{Features}\label{Features-7.3}}

\hypertarget{clear}{%
\subsubsection{Clear}\label{clear}}

To clear the last command or key pressed from the \textbf{Command Line}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Press {[}CE{]} key
\end{itemize}

To clear the \textbf{Command Line} without releasing the fixture selection and the param values assigned on the editor

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Press {[}VIBE{]} + {[}CLEAR{]} keys
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{de-select}{%
\subsubsection{De-Select}\label{de-select}}

To de-select the current \textbf{Fixture Selection} without releasing the param values assigned on the editor

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Press {[}FIXTURE{]} or {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}+ ⟶ -{]} {[}\#{]} {[}VIBE{]} + {[}DESEL{]} keys
\end{itemize}

To re-select the last \textbf{Fixture Selection}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Press {[}.{]} key
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{release}{%
\subsubsection{Release}\label{release}}

To release all the selected fixtures' parameters from the \textbf{Editor}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Press {[}FIXTURE{]} or {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}+ ⟶ -{]} {[}\#{]} {[}RELEASE{]}
\end{itemize}

To release the entire bank of parameters of the selected fixtures from the Editor

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Press {[}FIXTURE{]} or {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}+ ⟶ -{]} {[}\#{]} {[}VIBE{]} + \{Bank\} (or tap on the Bank) {[}RELEASE{]}
\end{itemize}

To release a fixture parameter (or the active parameters) of the selected fixtures from the Editor

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Press {[}FIXTURE{]} or {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}+ ⟶ -{]} {[}\#{]} \{BANK\} \{PARAMETER\} {[}RELEASE{]}
\end{itemize}

If a fixture is selected and active (Red), the first press of {[}RELEASE{]} releases the parameter and then the second press releases the whole fixture.

\hypertarget{reset}{%
\subsubsection{Reset}\label{reset}}

To reset the \textbf{Editor}, releasing all the param values without releasing the fixture selection

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Press {[}RESET{]} key
\end{itemize}

To reset the \textbf{Editor}, releasing both the fixture selection and the param values

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Press {[}VIBE{]} + {[}RESET{]} keys
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{home}{%
\subsubsection{Home}\label{home}}

When a parameter is not in use, meaning it is not in the Editor, neither triggered from any Playback, the values used by Vibe to tell where the fixture parameters should be set can be based on the fixture's home values or on the last value assigned.

Vibe has two options to set uncontrolled parameters' values, that can be set in the System Settings popup in the \{Editing Properties\} tab under the Default Stage Values header:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{Use Home values\} sets the uncontrolled parameters to their \textbf{Home Value}
\item
  \{Maintain Last Value\} leaves the uncontrolled parameters to \textbf{Maintain the Last Value} they were at before being released
\end{itemize}

All the devices have default home values for parameters based on the manufacturer's DMX chart.

Selected fixtures or parameters may be sent to their defaults using the {[}HOME{]} key.

\textbf{Home} - Sets parameter values to home values as defined by the manufacturer or to custom values defined by the user \textbf{Home Scene}.

The {[}HOME{]} button brings the fixture, bank or parameter home value in the Editor.

To \textbf{Home} selected fixtures in the \textbf{Editor}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Press {[}FIXTURE{]} or {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}+ ⟶ -{]} {[}\#{]} {[}HOME{]}
\end{itemize}

To Home the entire bank of parameters of the selected fixtures in the Editor

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Press {[}FIXTURE{]} or {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}+ ⟶ -{]} {[}\#{]} {[}VIBE{]} + \{BANK\} (or tap on the Bank) {[}HOME{]}
\end{itemize}

To Home a fixture parameter (or the active parameters) of the selected fixtures in the Editor

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Press {[}FIXTURE{]} or {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}+ ⟶ -{]} {[}\#{]} \{BANK\} \{PARAMETER\} {[}HOME{]}
\end{itemize}

If a fixture is selected and active (Red), the first press of {[}HOME{]} homes the parameter and then the second press homes the whole fixture.

\hypertarget{highlight}{%
\subsubsection{Highlight}\label{highlight}}

Highlight is used to help identify the positions of fixtures, so they may be edited more easily.

Highlight Mode affects parameters and banks in the following way:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Dimmer - Full
\item
  Other Intensity parameters - home values and masked
\item
  Position - unchanged and editable
\item
  Color - home values and masked
\item
  Beam - home values and masked
\item
  Image - home values and masked
\item
  Shape - home values and masked
\end{itemize}

To Highlight Fixtures or Groups:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Select fixtures using keypad or using Groups.
\item
  Press {[}H.LIGHT{]} - The key will flash in {red} to indicate the mode is active.
\end{enumerate}

All the Fixture selection will be set as specified above.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{2}
\item
  Press {[}NEXT{]} - The first fixture will remain ``highlighted'' and all other fixtures will return to their editor or stage values.
\item
  Adjust Pan/Tilt as required and press {[}NEXT{]} to move to the next fixture.
\item
  Continue through the fixture selection using {[}NEXT{]} {[}PREV{]} until all fixtures are focused.
\item
  Press {[}H.LIGHT{]} again to turn off Highlight Mode. The red light will go out and fixtures will return to their current editor or stage values.
\end{enumerate}

Highlight may be entered before a selection is made.
Press {[}ENTER{]} to Highlight the selection.

To swap new fixtures into Highlight:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Select new fixtures or Groups and press {[}ENTER{]} while still in Highlight Mode - the Highlight selection will be swapped.
\end{itemize}

Until {[}NEXT{]} {[}PREV{]} is pressed Groups will be appended to make up a larger Highlight selection.

Highlight may be customized when the default home values are too generic.

👉 Frequently users tighten the zoom or use a closer iris value as default to better focus the center of the beam.

\hypertarget{lowlight}{%
\subsubsection{Lowlight}\label{lowlight}}

Lowlight is used when many fixtures are lit up and therefore Highlight is not helpful.

Lowlight Mode affects parameters and banks in the following way:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Dimmer - 30\% all fixtures \textbf{except} active fixture which will be at 100\%
\item
  Other Intensity parameters - home values and masked.
\item
  Position - unchanged and editable
\item
  Color - home values and masked
\item
  Beam - home values and masked
\item
  Image - home values and masked
\item
  Shape - home values and masked Unlike Highlight, Lowlight maintains a value of 30\% for dimmers while active.
\end{itemize}

To Lowlight Fixtures or Groups:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Select fixtures using the keypad or using Groups.
\item
  Press {[}L.LIGHT{]} - The key will flash in {red} to indicate the mode is active.
\item
  Press {[}NEXT{]} - The first fixture will go to a dimmer value of 100\% and all other fixtures dimmer values will remain at 30\%. Other parameters will return to their editor or stage values.
\item
  Adjust Pan/Tilt as required and press {[}NEXT{]} to move to the next fixture.
\item
  Continue through the fixture selection using {[}NEXT{]} {[}PREV{]} until all fixtures are focused.
\item
  Press {[}L.LIGHT{]} again to turn off Lowlight Mode. The red light will go out and fixtures will return to their current editor or stage values.
\end{enumerate}

Lowlight may be entered before a selection is made.

Press {[}ENTER{]} to Lowlight the selection.

To swap new fixtures into Lowlight:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Select new fixtures or Groups and press {[}ENTER{]} while still in Lowlight Mode - the Lowlight selection will be swapped.
\end{itemize}

Until {[}NEXT{]} {[}PREV{]} is pressed Groups will be appended to make up a larger Lowlight selection.

Lowlight may be customized when the default home values are are too generic.

👉 Frequently users set the Lowlight dimmer to Zero instead of the default 30\% to create a solo effect.

\hypertarget{rem-dim}{%
\subsubsection{Rem Dim}\label{rem-dim}}

\textbf{Remainder Dim} is an Editor Tool commonly used in theater environments but can be useful in many other situations.

\textbf{\{REM DIM\}} Toggles all dimmer values to 0 except those that are selected.

If no value is given in the editor, the selected fixtures will maintain their current values.

Find \{REM DIM\} key on the toolbar when there's a Fixture Selection active.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  While in \{REM DIM\} is ON, {[}Next{]} / {[}Prev{]} will swap the Rem Dim fixture forwards or backwards.
\item
  If a selection of multiple fixtures is made followed by \{REM DIM\} all unselected fixtures will go to 0\%. The selected fixtures will not be changed or entered into the editor.
\item
  If a selection of multiple fixtures is made, {[}Next{]} / {[}Prev{]} will force all but the first fixture in the selection to 0. The next press of {[}Next{]} / {[}Prev{]} will force the first fixture to 0 and release the next fixture's dimmer. Fixtures in the selection will continue to swap from 0 to release with each press of {[}Next{]} / {[}Prev{]}.
\item
  Pressing REM DIM again toggles the feature off and restores the stage look.
\item
  Cues may be stored with \{REM DIM\} ON and the dimmers forced to 0\% by Rem Dim will not be stored.
\end{itemize}

Once stored, \{REM DIM\} is turned off.

\hypertarget{undo}{%
\subsubsection{Undo}\label{undo}}

Undo button directly rolls back the last actions.

Every parameter programmed in the editor can come back to its previous status by pressing the UNDO button.

Pressing Undo button multiple times will restore step by step the last actions.

Undo function can be used to recover the editor status after a Shift + Reset command, and to restore objects that have been stored, deleted, or updated, such as groups, libraries, qlists, cues and scenes.

Undo button works also with Home and High/Lowlight actions.

\textbf{Undo History}

Settings ⟶ Undo opens the Undo/Redo list to review and restore the last actions.

\textbf{Undo History}

To Undo more operations at a time, access the Undo history with the sequence {[}Vibe{]} + {[}Settings{]}, {[}Undo{]}

In the Undo popup's left column are listed all the undoable actions, and in case an undo command has been done, the list on the right shows the redo able actions.

Each action reports the date and time when it has been done.

Select one or more actions, than press undo or redo to come back to the desired status.

The popup does not close until the apply or restore buttons are pressed.

\hypertarget{fan}{%
\subsubsection{Fan}\label{fan}}

Fan is used to apply a distribution of values to the Fixture Selection.

Fan of the selected fixtures' parameters can be done either through the keypad syntax or by using the {[}Fan{]} key and Smart Screen's interface.

To enter the \textbf{Fan} Mode

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Select the range of fixtures to be fanned.
\item
  Press {[}FAN{]} to enter Fan Mode. - The key will flash in {red} to indicate the mode is active.
\item
  Select a Curve from the Fan Mode picker on the Smart Screen's right side.
\item
  Turn the encoder wheel of the parameter. - The param's values will be set fanned proportionally to the curve applied the order of selection.
\item
  Toggle {[}FAN{]} OR Select a different parameter to turn Fan mode off.
\end{enumerate}

Frequently used \textbf{Fan curves}:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{Fade In\} / = lowest to highest
\item
  \{Fade Out\} ~= highest to lowest
\item
  \{Diagonal\} -/- = Standard PAN Fan
\item
  \{Saw Tooth\} /~= Mirror lowest on ends to highest in middle
\item
  \{Mirror\} / = Highest on ends to lowest in middle
\end{itemize}

Curves can be freely changes while in Fan Mode to check the different results.

Any Curve created in the Curves Editor may be applied to Fan.

The Fixture Selection can be splitted to get fanned in \textbf{Blocks of} and \textbf{Sub Blocks}:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Select the range of fixtures to be fanned.
\item
  Press {[}FAN{]}.
\item
  Select a Curve from the Fan Mode.
\item
  Select \textbf{Blocks of \{\#\}}
\item
  Select \textbf{Sub Blocks \{\#\}}
\item
  Turn the encoder wheel of the parameter requiring fanning.
\item
  Toggle {[}FAN{]} to turn off Fan Mode.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Examples of Blocks and Sub Blocks:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \{Fade In\} - Blocks of 1 and no Sub Block
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \{Fade In\} - Blocks of 4 no Sub Blocks
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \{Fade In\} - Blocks of 6, Sub Blocks of 2
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \{Saw Tooth\} - (Low-High Mirror) Blocks of 12, Sub Blocks 2
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Moving Light Fixture with \{Fade In\} Dimmer and Color Fan, and \{Diagonal\} Pan Fan
\end{itemize}

Fixture \textbf{Groups} may also be used as Blocks.

To Fan Blocks By Groups:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Select the Groups
\item
  Press {[}FAN{]}.
\item
  Tap \{Blocks By Group\} under the Fan Mode picker.
\item
  Use any of the above methods to fan parameters by Group.
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{copy-paste}{%
\subsubsection{Copy / Paste}\label{copy-paste}}

{[}COPY{]} and {[}PASTE{]} are used to make copies of any object, such as Groups, Libraries, Scenes, Cues and Qlists, and store them in the desired positions.

The syntax to copy and paste an object is:

\{Object\} {[}COPY{]} \{Target\} {[}PASTE{]}

👉 {[}COPY{]} and {[}PASTE{]} keyboard syntax can be also used to copy and paste the selected (Red) params of a fixture to another.

When something is in \textbf{Copy} the {[}COPY{]} key will flash in {red} to indicate there is a copied content.

When a \textbf{Paste} command applies the content to the target, the data is kept on the Copy key to allow making other copies.

Toggle {[}COPY{]} or {[}RESET{]} the Editor to remove the content from the copy feature.

\textbf{Copy and Paste param values to Fixtures}

\{Fixture\} \{Param Selection\} or \{GRAB ALL\} {[}COPY{]} \{Fixture\} {[}PASTE{]}

\textbf{Copy and Paste a Bank of param values to Fixtures}

\{Fixture\} \{Bank Selection\} {[}COPY{]} \{Fixture\} \{Param Selection\} {[}PASTE{]}

\textbf{Copy and Paste param values to Groups}

{[}Group{]} \{Param Selection\} or \{GRAB ALL\} {[}COPY{]} {[}Group{]} \{Param Selection\} {[}PASTE{]}

\textbf{Copy and Paste param values from Cues}

{[}Qlist{]} {[}\#{]} {[}Cue{]} {[}\#{]} {[}Fixture{]} {[}\#{]} {[}COPY{]} {[}Fixture{]} {[}\#{]} {[}PASTE{]}

\textbf{Load param values from Cues to Editor}

{[}Qlist{]} {[}\#{]} {[}Cue{]} {[}\#{]} {[}Fixture{]} {[}\#{]} {[}LOAD{]}

\hypertarget{move}{%
\subsubsection{Move}\label{move}}

{[}MOVE{]} is used to change position of any object, such as Groups, Libraries, Scenes, Cues and Qlists.

The syntax to move an object is:

{[}MOVE{]} \{Object\} \{Target\}

{[}MOVE{]} can be also used to re-position Playback controllers without changing their content and settings.

The syntax to \textbf{Move a controller} is:

{[}MOVE{]} \{Controller\} \{Target Controller\}

\hypertarget{grab}{%
\subsubsection{Grab}\label{grab}}

Grab is a powerful tool for selecting fixtures that meet a number of conditions.

By default {[}GRAB{]} selects all fixtures with intensity or virtual intensity values above Zero.

Pressing {[}GRAB{]} without a selection opens a popup on the Smart Screen with the following options.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{\{Filters\}}

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    \{Stage\} - Select anything that is live on the stage and meets the conditions below.
  \item
    \{Editor\} - Select anything that is in the Editor and meets the conditions below.
  \item
    \{Master Controller\} (Playback) - Selects only fixtures on the Master Controller that meet the conditions.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{\{Conditions\}}

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    \{All Fixtures\} - Selects all fixtures that have any changes via the editor.
  \item
    \{Active\} - Fixture has intensity value over zero.
  \item
    \{Invisible\} - Recorded ``hard'' parameter values for fixtures that do NOT have an intensity value over zero.
  \item
    \{Inactive\} - Fixtures that have NO recorded values.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{\{Parameter Selection\}}

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    \{All for Selected\} Grabs all parameters for selected fixtures (shortcut is {[}VIBE{]} + {[}GRAB{]})
  \item
    \{Active (ENTER)\} Select all ``Active'' fixtures and enters them into the Editor. (Shortcut is {[}GRAB{]} + {[}ENTER{]})
  \item
    \{Search \& Replace\}.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  {[}SET{]} {[}GRAB{]} selects all active fixtures filtered by Set.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Grab Fixtures from a Controller:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Press {[}GRAB{]} - Grab Options popup will open.
\item
  Press any Slider/Qkey/Aux Qkey controller button - All fixtures that are in the Controller's active cue and have dimmer levels above Zero (or meet the Grab conditions selected in the popup) will be selected.
\end{enumerate}

👉 Pressing ENTER will put the selection in the EDITOR.

\textbf{Grab Fixtures in a cue based on a \{Library\} SK selection:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Press {[}GRAB{]}, tap a \{Library\} SK - All fixtures with matching libraries will be selected.
\item
  Tap another \{Library\} SK - The Library will be swapped to the new library.
\end{enumerate}

👉 This is especially useful for quickly swapping colors in cues containing libraries

\textbf{Select all parameters for a fixture selection:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Select the fixtures
\item
  Press {[}VIBE{]} + {[}GRAB{]}
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Select all Parameters with hard and tracked values outputting to the stage:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Press {[}GRAB{]} {[}ENTER{]}
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Grab an entire bank of parameters} of the selected fixtures in the Editor:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Press {[}FIXTURE{]} or {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}+ ⟶ -{]} {[}\#{]} {[}VIBE{]} + \{BANK\} (or tap on the Bank) {[}SHIFT{]} + {[}GRAB{]}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Live-7.4}{%
\subsection{Live}\label{Live-7.4}}

Vibe has two ways to display parameter data:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{Live View} - shows editor and playback values as a spreadsheet.
\item
  \textbf{Live Parameter View} - shows editor and playback data in a graphic icon-based format.
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{live-view}{%
\subsubsection{Live View}\label{live-view}}

The Live view is splitted into 4 different modes:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Live Full: shows the list of fixtures
\item
  Live Per Device: shows the list of fixtures, divided per device
\item
  Live Full Sub Device: shows the list of fixtures and subfixtures
\item
  Live Per Device Sub Device: shows the list of fixtures and subfixtures, divided per device
\end{itemize}

The preferred Live View mode may be loaded from the Vibe Menu, Program Views, Live submenu.

\textbf{Live View Options} are on the Live view's menu.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Active} - Filters the fixtures to display.

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    if all the options are toggled off, the view shows all the fixtures.
  \item
    if \textbf{Active On Stage} is on, only parameter columns that are selected in the editor or have values derived from the active stage look are shown. All others are hidden.
  \item
    if \textbf{Active In Editor} is on, only parameter columns that are selected in the editor are shown. All others are hidden.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Sets} - Select which of the fixture sets to display.

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Fixture
  \item
    Channel
  \item
    Spot
  \item
    Matrix
  \item
    Media Server
  \item
    Custom Sets (if created)
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Format} - Controls what data is displayed in the cells.

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Source PB (the source triggering the value, either it is the editor or a controller)
  \item
    Value \textgreater{} \% (values in percentage)
  \item
    Value \textgreater{} Decimal (DMX values 0 ⟶ 255 )
  \item
    Value \textgreater{} Text (Parameter step text)
  \item
    Value \textgreater{} Text + Percentage (Parameter step text and values in percentage)
  \item
    Time (Parameter time, if specified in the editor)
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Fit By} - Opens column width options:

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Window
  \item
    Value
  \item
    Default
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Live View Settings} are on a popup accessible by tapping the Settings icon.

Live View \textbf{Display} tab is to set the cells that are shown and order the columns.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Live View Scheme} - Parameter columns may be freely added, removed, or moved in the grid by selecting and tapping on the move keys.
\end{itemize}

To Move columns:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Tap a parameter column heading or multiple parameter column headings - the green indicator light will turn on.
\item
  Use the \textbf{\{\textbar\textless\}} or \textbf{\{\textgreater\textbar\}} keys to move the parameter column forward or backwards.
\end{enumerate}

To Move columns to the first or last position:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Tap a parameter column heading or multiple parameter column headings - the green indicator light will turn on.
\item
  Use the \textbf{\{\textbar\textless\textless\}} or \textbf{\{\textgreater\textgreater\textbar\}} keys to move the parameter column first position or last position.
\end{enumerate}

To Remove columns:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Tap a parameter column heading or multiple parameter column headings - the green indicator light will turn on.
\item
  Tap the \{Remove\} key.
\end{enumerate}

The \textbf{Dock} option locks the column scroll at the end of the parameter column that is docked. A red line will show on the live display to show the dock position.

The \textbf{Group By Banks} option groups the columns corresponding to the same param bank.

The \textbf{All icons in 1 cell} option is not implemented yet.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Banks} - Columns are added using the icons in the parameter box.
\end{itemize}

To Add or Remove columns:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Select a bank - All parameters for the bank will appear in the box adjacent to the banks.
\item
  Select the parameter or parameters to add to the live display.
\end{enumerate}

👉 Swipe in the the parameter box to browse hidden parameters.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{2}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap a parameter that is not already selected in green and it will be added to the end of the column list.
\end{enumerate}

👉 {Parameter keys are toggles so if the indicator light is on, touching the parameter will remove it. Tapping again will put it back at the end of the columns. }.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{3}
\tightlist
\item
  Use the \textbf{\{\textbar\textless\}} or \textbf{\{\textgreater\textbar\} \{\textbar\textless\textless\}} or \textbf{\{\textgreater\textgreater\textbar\}} to position the column.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Interactive Icons:} - Adds or removes the interactive icons columns.
\end{itemize}

👉 {Interactive Icon keys are toggles so if the indicator light is on, touching an icon key will remove it. Tapping again will put it back at the end of the columns.}.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{General:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Global Fixture Number - Fixture's hidden unique system number.
  \item
    Fixture Layer Number - Adds or removes the Fixture Number column.
  \item
    Fixture Name - Adds or removes the Fixture Name column.
  \item
    Fixture Address - Adds or removes the Fixture Address column.
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

👉 {Fixture \# and Fixture Name are toggles so if the indicator light is on, touching an icon key will remove it. Tapping again will put it back at the end of the columns.}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Presets:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Basic - Live view preset that only shows the param columns
  \item
    Basic + Interactive - Live view preset that shows the param columns, the interactive icons, and the fixture layer name and number
  \item
    Extended - Live view preset that shows the param columns, the interactive icons, and the fixture layer name, number and address
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

The Live View Settings popup has two keys to switch On/Off the ``Headers Per Device'' and the ``Sub Device Mode'' visualization options.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Headers per Device} - Groups the fixtures visualization by devices, showing the device name on each header.
\end{itemize}

👉 Headers can be collapsed by tapping on the arrow in the header.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Sub Device Mode} - Toggles on/off the visualization of sub-fixtures, available when the device contains sub-devices.
\end{itemize}

Live View \textbf{Behavior} tab is to set the view's interactions.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Fixture Jumping}:

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Off - No jumps.
  \item
    Jump if needed - Live display pages the display to show the selected fixtures if they are not shown on the current live page.
  \item
    Lowest Selection to Top - Jumps the lowest fixture number of the selection to the top of the live display.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Parameter Jumping}:

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Jumps the parameter's column and related bank parameters to the first scrollable column (the end of the Dock position).
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Interactive Fixture Selection}:

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Turns on the ability to make fixture selections from the live display grids.
  \item
    {Not implemented yet.}
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Interactive Cell Editing}:

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Turns on the ability to directly edit cells in the live display grid.
  \item
    {Not implemented yet.}
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Interactive Parameter Selection:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Enables parameters to be directly selected from the live display headers.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Submaster Values:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Show Stored Values - Dimmer parameters under the control of Group Submasters will continue to show their stored values and the Submaster icon will also be shown to indicate when the parameter is inhibited.
  \item
    Show Output Values - When the Submaster is pulled down, the actual dimmer output values will be shown in the live display dimmer column.
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{live-parameter-view}{%
\subsubsection{Live Parameter View}\label{live-parameter-view}}

The Live Parameter View shows values in a table format instead of spreadsheet format.

There are three Live Parameter Modes accessible from the view's menu:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Live Parameters
\item
  Live Banks
\item
  Live Fixtures
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Mode} - Select the options of the fixture / parameter icons displayed.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Live Parameter} - Fixtures are graphically displayed by icons per parameter.
\end{itemize}

As an evolution to the theatrical consoles concept, parameters other than dimmer are also shown.

Parameters visible in Live Parameter Mode can be filtered by using the Parameter View Settings popup.
To \textbf{Filter Parameters}:
1. Tap the Settings icon to open the Parameter View Settings popup.
2. Tap parameters on either side to move them from one box to the other.
3. Close the popup with {[}ENTER{]} or the Apply icon.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Live Bank}- Fixtures are graphically displayed by icons per bank.
\end{itemize}

Currently three banks can be graphically viewed:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Intensity} - In the form of an illuminated circle.
\item
  \textbf{Position} - In the form of a cursor that moves in a black rectangle representing the stage.
\item
  \textbf{Color} - In the form of a circular color swatch (Color wheels are not currently supported.)
\item
  \textbf{Live Fixture} - Fixtures are graphically displayed by icons per fixture
\end{itemize}

Intensity, Position, and Color bank icons are merged into a single icon for each fixture.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Active On Stage} - Toggle button.

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    If off, all fixtures are shown.
  \item
    If on, only fixtures that are selected in the editor or have values derived from the active stage look are shown. All others are hidden.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Sets} - Select which of the fixture sets to display.

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Fixture
  \item
    Channel
  \item
    Spot
  \item
    Matrix
  \item
    Media Server
  \item
    Custom Sets (if created)
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Format} - Controls what data is displayed in the cells.

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Value \textgreater{} \% (values in percentage)
  \item
    Value \textgreater{} Decimal (DMX values 0 ⟶ 255 )
  \item
    Value \textgreater{} Text (Parameter step text)
  \item
    Value \textgreater{} Text + Percentage (Parameter step text and values in percentage)
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{library}{%
\section{Library}\label{library}}

👉 Store Vibe \textbf{Libraries} to get presets of values divided by banks of parameters, quickly recallable from the library softkeys.

This chapter deals with filtering Editor parameters when storing objects and creating libraries by banks.

\textbf{Chapter index:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Bank-Libraries-8.1}{8.1 Bank Libraries}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Auto-Generate-8.2}{8.2 Auto Generate}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Library-Defaults-8.3}{8.3 Library Defaults}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Filters-8.4}{8.4 Filters}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Bank-Libraries-8.1}{%
\subsection{Bank Libraries}\label{Bank-Libraries-8.1}}

Libraries are the basic way to store show data on the system.

The use of libraries is not mandatory, as most of the playback operations can be performed without storing any, but is highly recommended in order to make the programming flow better organized and much faster and easier, both during the setup and the editing of the show file.

All kinds of libraries can be scrolled and selected through their specific softkey view or through the Context view.

\textbf{Vibe has two types of Libraries: Object Libraries and Bank Libraries.}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Object Libraries are pools of system object types, such as:
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Groups (used to manage fixture selection)
\item
  Qlists (used to store sequences of Cues)
\item
  Scenes (used to store stage looks)
\item
  Snaps (used to manage controllers' configuration)
\item
  Filters (used to store Bank Libraries and Cues)
\item
  Macros (used to store sequences of commands)
\end{itemize}

An extensive description of each object can be found into its dedicated chapter.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{1}
\tightlist
\item
  Bank Libraries are pools of parameter values divided by banks of parameters, such as:
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Intensity (dimmer, strobe, shutter, \ldots)
\item
  Position (pan, tilt, move speed, \ldots)
\item
  Color (red, cyan, CTC, color wheel, \ldots)
\item
  Beam (focus, zoom, iris, frost, prism, \ldots)
\item
  Image (gobo wheel, animation, pattern, \ldots)
\item
  Shape (blade depth, blade angle, blade rotation\ldots)
\item
  Custom (unfiltered; stores every parameter in the Editor)
\item
  Effect (effects running on parameters in the Editor)
\end{itemize}

Bank Libraries are often called Presets or Palettes on other consoles.

Bank libraries are referenced by stored objects such as Scenes and Cues, so any modification will affect all the objects that have a reference to the edited library.

Video contents, managed on the Media Bank, don't have a specific library because Media Server's parameters are also assigned to the previous Banks. They may be stored on the bank libraries, filtering the parameters contained in that library, either on the Custom libraries.

\hypertarget{operations}{%
\subsubsection{Operations}\label{operations}}

\textbf{Store directly to a Softkey:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  {[}Fixture{]} {[}\#{]} {[}+ ⟶ -{]} Select parameter \{Bank\} on Smart Screen.
\item
  Use keypad, Smart Screen pickers, and/or adjust parameter wheels, to set values.
\item
  Press {[}STORE{]} - The Library \{Store Options\} tab will appear on top right corner of the softkeys, use the Library \{Store Options\} popup at the bottom on the Smart Screen to make changes.
\end{enumerate}

👉 If no option changes are required just directly tap the appropriate Library SK without tapping the Library \{Store Options\}.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{3}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap destination Library softkey.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Store a Library with the keypad syntax:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Press {[}Fixture{]} {[}\#{]} {[}+ ⟶ -{]} - Select parameter \{Bank \} on Smart Screen.
\item
  Use Smart Screen pickers, and/or adjust parameter wheels, or type value to set values.
\item
  Press {[}LIBRARY{]} - Library \{Store Options\} popup will open at the bottom of the Smart Screen and \{Banks\} will appear on the Editor Toolbar.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{3}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap a parameter \{Bank\} key on the Editor Toolbar.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{A shortcut for Bank selection is to specify a number directly after pressing {[}LIBRARY{]} key:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  1 = Intensity
\item
  2 = Position
\item
  3 = Color
\item
  4 = Beam
\item
  5 = Image
\item
  6 = Shape
\item
  7 = Custom
\item
  8 = Effect
\end{itemize}

After typing the first number, the bank name will appear on the command line.

The next number typed will be the library number.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{4}
\item
  Enter the destination library number via the keypad.
\item
  Make \{Library Store Options\} changes, if required.
\item
  Press {[}STORE{]} to complete the sequence.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Edit a Library using {[}UPDATE{]}:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Make a change to parameter value outputting to the stage from a library referenced in an active cue or scene.
\item
  Press {[}UPDATE{]} - The Update popup will open.
\end{enumerate}

Two columns will appear: the left column shows \textbf{Cues} available for updating and the right column shows \textbf{Libraries} available for updating. The target library or libraries should be selected.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{2}
\item
  Selected libraries may be freely deselected in the Update popup. If all Libraries are deselected, updates will become ``hard'' values in the source cue instead of the source libraries being updated.
\item
  Tap Apply or press {[}ENTER{]} to close the popup and complete the update.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Edit a Library using Softkey syntax:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Make a change to parameter value outputting to the stage from a library referenced in an active cue or scene.
\item
  Press {[}STORE{]} and tap source \{Library SK\}.
\item
  The \{Cancel, Update, Overwrite, Release\} popup will appear on the large screen.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{3}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap \{Overwrite\} to replace all fixtures with those in the Editor, \{Update\} to merge the editor into the library, or \{Release\} to remove the selected fixtures from the Library.
\end{enumerate}

👉 Releasing fixtures is only valid if the library is \textbf{Fixture Specific} or \textbf{Device + Fixture} in the case where specific fixtures have modified recorded values.

👉 Alternatively the sequence {[}LIBRARY{]} \{Bank\} from the Editor Toolbar {[}\#{]} {[}STORE{]} to an existing library will work the same as above.

\textbf{Edit Library settings:}

Library Settings may be changed at any time after they are created using the Library Settings popup.

To open the Library Settings popup:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap the Library \{SK\} you wish to modify and press {[}SETTINGS{]}. (This is invalid with Snaps and Macros as it will trigger them)
\end{itemize}

OR

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Press {[}LIBRARY{]} tap \{Bank\} on the Editor Toolbar Press {[}\#{]} {[}SETTINGS{]}
\item
  If Reference is selected, modifying the library will modify the values of ALL cues that reference it.
\item
  If Timing is selected, any parameter cell times in the Editor will be stored with the library.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Rename a Library:}

Text labels may be assigned as library names at any time there is NOT an active selection in the Editor. Any of the following methods are valid:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Directly after storing a Library, start typing on the keyboard. The Text Entry popup will appear.
\item
  Tap a Library \{SK\}, start typing on the keyboard. The Text Entry popup will appear.
\item
  Press {[}TEXT{]} tap a Library \{SK\}. The Text Entry popup will appear.
\item
  Press {[}Library{]} tap \{Bank\} on the Editor Toolbar Press {[}\#{]} {[}TEXT{]}. The Text Entry popup will appear.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Delete a Library:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Method 1: Tap the Libraries' \{SK\} then press {[}DELETE{]}
\item
  Method 2: Keypad sequence for Bank Libraries: {[}LIBRARY{]} tap \{Bank\} on the Editor Toolbar {[}\#{]} {[}DELETE{]}
\item
  Method 3: Keypad sequence for deleting Object Libraries: {[}Object{]} {[}\#{]} {[}DELETE{]}
\end{itemize}

For most Delete functions, pressing {[}DELETE{]} {[}DELETE{]} will do the same as pressing {[}DELETE{]} and {[}ENTER{]} or Apply to complete the operation.

\textbf{Library Softkey Menu:}

The Long Click gesture on any softkey, including the libraries, opens a temporary Quick Actions menu where the user can directly select the most used functions:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Copy: make a copy of the object, that may then be pasted on a different SK
\item
  Move: move the object to a different position, acting like a cut + paste command
\item
  Delete: open the delete object popup
\item
  Settings: open the object settings popup
\item
  Text: open the rename object popup
\item
  Exam: exam the object in the Exam View
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{auto-param-grouping}{%
\subsubsection{Auto Param Grouping}\label{auto-param-grouping}}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  By default Vibe uses the concept of ``Automatic Parameter Grouping'' when storing libraries.
\item
  Auto Parameter Grouping can be disabled by tapping the toggle button in the system settings popup.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  With Auto Param Grouping enabled: when a parameter is moved, all the parameters of the same library bank are grabbed into the editor with their current status, to guarantee that what is viewed on the stage is the same as what is stored in the library.
\item
  With Auto Param Grouping disabled: when a parameter is moved, only that parameter is grabbed into the editor with its current status.
\item
  Even if the Auto Param Grouping is enabled, Filters may be applied to modify what is stored in a Library.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{context}{%
\subsubsection{Context}\label{context}}

The Context view is a softkey view that helps reducing the amount of space on the screen where the libraries are shown.

Context adapts the visualization to the last selected object type or bank, for example by pressing Group it will show the Groups library, by pressing Color the Color library and by pressing Scene the Scenes library.

The Context view's settings popup on the layout editing toolbar allows to filter the contents shown by the context view.

Context views can be filtered manually, by toggling the contents to enable and disable, or automatically, by setting the \textbf{Smart} option. Smart context setting will avoid to show the softkeys that are already shown by other softkey views on the same page.

\hypertarget{Auto-Generate-8.2}{%
\subsection{Auto Generate}\label{Auto-Generate-8.2}}

The option to Auto Generate Groups and Libraries permits a smart and fast creation of the most common fixture groups and parameter libraries.

The Auto Generate button is on the Editor Toolbar in Patch pages.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  By selecting the options in the Auto Generate popup menu, the user can configure the quantity and numbering of the libraries to be automatically created.
\item
  The auto generated libraries can either overwrite or merge the already existing ones.
\item
  Libraries can be named using system names or library numbers, and customized for example using the device's name.
\item
  Libraries can be numbered starting from a desired number or appended to the first available position.
\item
  Libraries of different fixture types can be spaced with the desired interleave.
\item
  Auto generated libraries are created when pressing the GENERATE button on the popup.
\item
  It is possible to check the preferred options on each bank page and then generate all of them in a single click, as well as to generate step by step for each device or for each bank.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{groups}{%
\subsubsection{Groups}\label{groups}}

Auto Generate Groups has the following options:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  All per Set creates groups including the fixtures of the Set.
\item
  All per Type creates groups including the fixtures using each imported Device.
\item
  Odd/Even creates groups with fixtures splitted in halfs between odds and evens.
\item
  Interleave 3 creates groups using a 3 fixtures interleave.
\item
  Interleave 4 creates groups using a 3 fixtures interleave.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{fixture-masks}{%
\subsubsection{Fixture Masks}\label{fixture-masks}}

{Not implemented yet.}.

\hypertarget{intensity-1}{%
\subsubsection{Intensity}\label{intensity-1}}

Auto Generate Intensity Libraries has the following options:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Intensity steps libraries may be splitted: in 3 (0\%, 50\%, 100\%), in increments of 10\%, in increments of 5\%.
\item
  Shutter steps libraries are created according to the fixtures' shutter and strobe parameter.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{position-1}{%
\subsubsection{Position}\label{position-1}}

Auto Generate Position Libraries is not supported, because commonly the position values are fixture specific and they are customized by the operator according to the show and stage setup.

\hypertarget{color-1}{%
\subsubsection{Color}\label{color-1}}

Auto Generate Color Libraries has the following options:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Color Wheels steps libraries are created according to the fixtures' color wheel parameter(s).
\item
  Similar colors may be combined into the same library.
\item
  Half steps libraries may be created between two consecutive colors of the wheel.
\item
  System Colors creates libraries of the most used colors, defined by the system.
\item
  Kelvin creates libraries of the most used color temperatures, defined by the system.
\item
  Color libraries using the fixtures' Color Mixing system may be splitted in steps based on their Hue and Saturation virtual parameters.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{beam-1}{%
\subsubsection{Beam}\label{beam-1}}

Auto Generate Beam Libraries has the following options:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Zoom libraries are created for narrow, half and wide values (usually 0\%, 50\%, 100\%, or viceversa).
\item
  Focus libraries are created for 0\%, 50\%, 100\%.
\item
  Iris libraries are created for narrow, half and wide, and for eventual steps of the Iris parameter.
\item
  Frost libraries are created for 0\%, 50\%, 100\%, and for eventual steps of the Frost parameter.
\item
  Prism steps libraries are created according to the fixtures' prism wheel(s) parameter(s).
\item
  Similar prisms may be combined into the same library.
\item
  Prism Rotation libraries may be created with a clockwise (CW) and counterclockwise (CCW) direction.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{image-1}{%
\subsubsection{Image}\label{image-1}}

Auto Generate Image Libraries has the following options:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Gobo steps libraries are created according to the fixtures' gobo wheel(s) parameter(s).
\item
  Similar gobos may be combined into the same library.
\item
  Gobo Rotation libraries may be created with a clockwise (CW) and counterclockwise (CCW) direction.
\item
  Animation steps libraries are created according to the fixtures' animation or effect wheel(s) parameter(s).
\item
  Similar animations may be combined into the same library.
\item
  Animation Rotation libraries may be created with a clockwise (CW) and counterclockwise (CCW) direction.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{shape-1}{%
\subsubsection{Shape}\label{shape-1}}

Auto Generate Shape Libraries has the following options:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Shape libraries will be based on the fixtures' blading (or shapers) system.
\item
  Shape libraries may be created for different common shapes, such as: horizontal and vertical lines, squares and triangles shapes, blade pack indexing Rotations.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Library-Defaults-8.3}{%
\subsection{Library Defaults}\label{Library-Defaults-8.3}}

When storing Bank Libraries, filters are used to exclude parameters from unrelated banks. The Library filter defaults are set under the \textbf{Filters} popup, \textbf{Library Defaults} tab.

\textbf{Change Defaults:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Press {[}VIBE{]} + {[}FILTERS{]} to open the Filters pop-up.
\item
  Make sure the \textbf{Library Defaults} tab in the lower left corner of the pop-up is selected.
\item
  Tap the \{Library Bank\} to be edited.
\item
  Tap a parameter in the \textbf{Available Parameters} box to move it to the \textbf{Filtered Parameters box}.
\item
  Press the \{Apply\} softkey to apply the filter and stay in the popup or press {[}ENTER{]} or the Apply icon to apply the filter and close the popup.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Restore Defaults:} - Tapping \{Restore Defaults\} will reset Bank Libraries to their system defaults.

\hypertarget{behavior}{%
\subsubsection{Behavior}\label{behavior}}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Library behaviors are used to define which fixtures the library can be applied to.
\item
  Library Bank softkeys have default filters and behaviors based on the way they are commonly used.
\item
  Default library settings and behaviors can be changed in the System settings tab on the \textbf{Defaults} tab.
\item
  Vibe bank libraries have the following behaviors:
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{Fixture Specific} (\textbf{FS}) -- Values will be applied only to the specific stored fixtures.
\item
  \textbf{Parameter Specific} (\textbf{PR}) -- Values will be applied to all matched parameters.
\item
  \textbf{Pattern} (\textbf{PT}) -- Values will be applied to all matched parameters and will keep the pattern of values.
\item
  \textbf{Device + Fixture} (\textbf{DF}) -- Values will be applied to the specific fixtures when possible and the rest will be the same as the first. The first fixture will be used as a reference for all other similar fixture types. If values are changed for specific fixtures they will be stored in the library as if they were fixture specific. If a fixture other than the first fixture is released from the library, its values will again be referenced from the first fixture. If the first fixture is released, the next fixture will become the reference.
\item
  \textbf{Device + Parameter} (\textbf{DR}) -- Values will be applied to fixtures of the same device and having the same parameter type.
\item
  \textbf{Device + Pattern} (\textbf{DT}) -- Values will be applied to fixtures of the same device and will keep the pattern of values.
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{reference}{%
\subsubsection{Reference}\label{reference}}

When a library has the Reference option enabled, using that library will be referenced by the Cues and Scenes stored with the library active in the editor.

Editing a referenced library will affect all the objects that have reference to that library.

Enable or Disable library reference on the library settings or, as system default, in the system settings popup on the \textbf{Defaults} tab.

\hypertarget{time}{%
\subsubsection{Time}\label{time}}

When a library has the Time option enabled it stores not only the parameters' values but also the parameter times assigned in editor.

Enable or Disable library time on the library settings or, as system default, in the system settings popup on the \textbf{Defaults} tab.

\hypertarget{effect-base}{%
\subsubsection{Effect Base}\label{effect-base}}

When an \textbf{Effect library} has the reference ``Include Base'' option enabled, the effect will be stored together with the base values of the parameters used.

Change library reference on the library settings or, as system default, in the system settings popup on the \textbf{Defaults} tab.

\hypertarget{Filters-8.4}{%
\subsection{Filters}\label{Filters-8.4}}

\hypertarget{on-the-fly}{%
\subsubsection{On the Fly}\label{on-the-fly}}

On the Fly Filters are used to filter editor parameters when storing libraries, cues and scenes.

The Green box indicates parameters that will be included in the cue or scene.

\textbf{Store using On The Fly Filters:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Set or load values into the Editor. {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}EDIT{]} is also valid.
\item
  Press {[}FILTERS{]} key.
\item
  The On The Fly Filter popup will open.
\item
  De-select banks and parameters that need to be filtered from the cue.
\item
  Press {[}ENTER{]} or Apply to apply the filter and close the popup.
\item
  Store library, cue or scene. Deselected banks and parameters will not be included.
\end{enumerate}

Options:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{Select All\} - Selects all banks and parameters
\item
  \{Deselect All\} - Deselects all banks and parameters
\item
  \{Save as Default\} - {Not implemented yet.}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{custom-filters}{%
\subsubsection{Custom Filters}\label{custom-filters}}

Custom Filters may be created ``blind'' without a fixture and parameter selection.
Filters are saved in the \textbf{Filters Library} and can be recalled by tapping the filter's softkeys when storing.

Parameters may be included or excluded by the Filter depending on how the filter is created.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Only the parameters in the \textbf{Filtered Parameters} right box will be stored.
\item
  All the parameters in the \textbf{Available Parameters} left box will be ignored.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Create a Filter}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Press {[}VIBE{]} + {[}FILTERS{]} to open the Filters popup.
\item
  Select the \textbf{General} Tab of the popup.
\item
  Press \{New\}. A new filter will be created with the name \{1. Filter 1\}.
\item
  Accept the name or type a new name in the white data entry box at the bottom of the Filter's column.
\item
  Tap in the Available Parameters box the parameters that you want to be included. The parameter will move from the Available Parameters box to the Filtered Parameters box.
\item
  Tap in the Filtered Parameters box the parameters that you want to be excluded. The parameter will move from the Filtered Parameters box to the Available Parameters box.
\item
  Press the \{Apply\} softkey to apply the filter and stay in the popup or press {[}ENTER{]} or the Apply icon to apply the filter and close the popup.
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{effect}{%
\section{Effect}\label{effect}}

👉 Experience Vibe \textbf{Effects}, the most complete FX engine designed to manage dynamic values through any function curve.

This chapter deals with managing the effect engines and curves to create dynamic effects.

\textbf{Chapter index:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Curves-9.1}{9.1 Curves}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Effect-Editor-9.2}{9.2 Effect Editor}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Pre-Built-Effects-9.3}{9.3 Pre-Built Effects}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Smart-Effects-9.4}{9.4 Smart Effects}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Advanced-Effects-9.5}{9.5 Advanced Effects}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Library-Effects-9.6}{9.6 Library Effects}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Effect-Library-9.7}{9.7 Effect Library}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Curves-9.1}{%
\subsection{Curves}\label{Curves-9.1}}

\textbf{Effects} are built from the following components:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Parameters - selected from the created fixtures' available params.
\item
  Patterns - curves that affect the fixture selection's order.
\item
  Primitives - curves that affect the parameter's output values.
\end{itemize}

The same Curves database, containing pre-built curves and user-created custom curves, is used by different features on Vibe:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  the \textbf{Effect Engine} to assign the curve to an effect
\item
  the \textbf{Fan Mode} to set pattern curves to the parameter fanning
\item
  the \textbf{Address View} to set specific curves to DMX addresses, such as dimmer curves
\end{itemize}

All the \textbf{Curves} are listed in the \textbf{Curves View}

The Curves View header has options to manage the Curves:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  New - to create a new curve
\item
  Edit - to modify an existing curve
\item
  Delete - to delete a custom curve; pre-built curves cannot be deleted
\item
  Duplicate - to make a copy of an existing curve
\item
  Favourite - to assign the \textbf{Favourite} tag to the curve, setting it in higher position on the list
\end{itemize}

A Curve creation or editing will open the \textbf{Curve Editor} popup.

Tap the graphic to add anchor points.

Tap the \{Remove Point\} button to remove anchor points.

Tap an anchor point to select and move it on the grid.

Tap the \{Anchor/Control\} button to switch between straight and curved lines between the points.

A curved line can be made on both directions and be spread with the red linked buttons.

\{Snap Horizontally\} and \{Snap Vertically\} buttons fix the anchor points to the grid's rows and columns.

The Name box is to write a name for the curve.

Tap Apply to store the Curve or Cancel to discard it.

\hypertarget{Effects-Editor-9.2}{%
\subsection{Effect Editor}\label{Effects-Editor-9.2}}

\textbf{Effects} are the combination of Parameters, Patterns and Primitives.

Vibe has 2 Effect Engines that provide up to 4 ways of effect building.

A \textbf{Basic} Effect Engine opens on the Smart Screen and allows the creation of:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Pre-built effects}
\item
  \textbf{Smart effects}
\end{itemize}

To access the Basic Engine press the {[}EFFECT{]} button with an active fixture selection and optionally a param selection.

An \textbf{Advanced} Effect Engine opens on the Wide Screen and allows the creation of:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Advanced effects}
\item
  \textbf{Library effects}
\end{itemize}

To access the Advanced Engine press the {[}VIBE{]} + {[}EFFECT{]} buttons with an active fixture selection and optionally a param selection.

Once a Fixture Selection is active, the effect editors can be opened even without a parameter selection, as that can be done afterwards, directly on the effect views.

This is not valid for the Basic Effects, that will be based on the Params selected at the moment that the {[}EFFECT{]} button is pressed.

\hypertarget{attributes}{%
\subsubsection{Attributes}\label{attributes}}

The Effect Editor on the Smart Screen has 2 tabs available:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Attributes} - Used for managing Effect, Pattern and Primitive Settings.
\item
  \textbf{Grouping} - Used for setting \textbf{Blocks Of} and \textbf{Sub Blocks}, to split the fixture selection order.
\end{itemize}

Effect Settings can be applied in two modes:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Relative} - Changes are applied maintaining the offsets between the curves' settings.
\item
  \textbf{Absolute} - Changes are applied with values that are equal for all the selection.
\end{itemize}

The toggle choice between Relative and Absolute can be found both in the Basic and in the Advanced Effect Engine.

\textbf{Effect Settings}

Many different Settings can be used to customize the effect's cycle and the curves' distribution through the fixtures and the parameters involved.

The following Effect Settings affect both the Pattern and the Primitives:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Offset All} - Moves the start time of the Effect Event along the X axis of the Effect Track.
\item
  \textbf{Param Spread} - Is a toggle button that enables the proportional offsetting of the start times of multiple Effect Tracks at the same time.
\end{itemize}

👉 \{Param Spread\} is a quick way to make CMY or RGB effects where colors need to spread apart so as not to mix to black or white.

\textbf{Pattern Settings}

Some Effect Settings apply only to the Pattern, so they are called Pattern Settings.

The \textbf{Pattern} is the curve that sets the order of fixture selection during its transitions across the effect.

The Basic Effect Engine provides a selection of 4 Pattern possibilities:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Fade In = Linear order
\item
  Fade Out = Inverted order
\item
  Saw Tooth = Mirrored order from sides to center
\item
  Mirror = Mirrored order from center to sides
\end{itemize}

The Advanced Effect Engine permits to assign any curve from the Curves database as the Pattern.

The following Settings affect the Pattern:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Spread} - Sets the percentage of offset between each fixture in a selection. 100\% would evenly divide up the selection's transition across the Primitive, while 0\% would have all fixtures transitioning in unison.
\item
  \textbf{UP} - Based on the UP group \#, each UP group will complete a full cycle across the Primitive before the next UP group can start.
\end{itemize}

\begin{quote}
With a selection of 8 fixtures assigned to a sine wave curve and a spread of 100\%:
- if UP is set to 1, fixture 1 will have to complete its full cycle before fixture 2 will begin,
- if UP is set to 2, fixture 1 and 2 will split the cycle before the next two fixtures start.
\end{quote}

\textbf{Primitive Settings}

Some Effect Settings apply only to the Primitive, so they are called Primitive Settings.

The \textbf{Primitive} is the curve applied to the parameter, that makes its values changing accordingly to the curve's shape during the transitions cycle.

The following Settings affect the Primitive:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Time} - The duration of time it takes each fixture to complete one cycle across the primitive.
\item
  \textbf{Size} - The amplitude of the primitive's curve, stating the param range of values.
\item
  \textbf{Base} - The start point of the primitive, stating the starting param values.
\item
  \textbf{Swing} - The direction from the parameters start point (Base).
\item
  \textbf{Swing \{Up\} \{Center\} \{Down\}} - The keys to set the Swing at 0\%, -50\%, -100\%.
\end{itemize}

\begin{quote}
If the Base value of tilt is 50\% (0°) and the \textbf{Swing} is \textbf{Center (-50\%)}, tilt would split center with a sine wave primitive assigned to it. \textbf{Up (0\%)} would send the tilt from strait down to full out into the audience. \textbf{Down (-100\%)} would send the tilt from strait down to full up upstage.
\end{quote}

\hypertarget{grouping}{%
\subsubsection{Grouping}\label{grouping}}

Grouping tab allows to split the fixture selection order.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Tap the \{Grouping\} key - The Grouping tab will show
\item
  If the Offset Pattern Spread is not above 0\%, tap the \{Wave On\} key. The Spread will be changed to 100\%.
\item
  Set the \{Blocks Of\} {[}\#{]} - This interleaves the Pattern into Groupings.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  The Default for \textbf{Blocks} and \textbf{Sub Block} is 1/0 - No Groupings.
\item
  When \{Blocks Of\} is set to 1 or above, the Sub Blocks will move to 1 and Interleave Groups will be possible.
\end{itemize}

\begin{quote}
2/1 would interleave a selection into Odds/Evens.
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
