\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Select the fixtures to be in the Mask.
\item
  Optionally select the parameters to be included in the Mask - If no parameters are selected, all parameters will be included.
\item
  Tap \{LOOK AHEAD MASK\} on the Editor Toolbar.
\item
  Press {[}STORE{]}.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Delete a Look Ahead Mask}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Select the fixtures to be deleted from the Mask.
\item
  Tap \{LOOK AHEAD MASK\} on the Editor Toolbar.
\item
  Press {[}DELETE{]}.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Set Look Ahead Time}

Set the speed at which fixtures move in dark, individually on each Cue.

Cues without a specific Look Ahead Time assigned will use the default Look Ahead Time.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}TIME{]} - The Time popup will open.
\item
  Tap \{Look Ahead\} - The indicator will turn green and Look Ahead will appear over the virtual time wheel.
\item
  Set the time value using the virtual wheel or type the value directly on the keypad.
\item
  Press {[}ENTER{]} or tap Apply to close the popup and store the Look Ahead time.
\end{enumerate}

Look Ahead time can also be changed using the {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}SETTINGS{]} command.

Default Look Ahead time can be set in System Settings/Defaults/Cue Settings.

\textbf{Enable or Disable Look Ahead on individual controllers}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Press {[}SETTINGS{]} {[}HERE{]} to any controller button of the controller you wish to enable or disable Look Ahead on - The Settings popup will open.
\item
  Tap the \{Qlist properties\} tab - The Qlist Properties popup will open.
\item
  Toggle Look Ahead on or off.
\item
  Press {[}ENTER{]} or Apply to close the popup.
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{link}{%
\subsubsection{Link}\label{link}}

\textbf{Link} is a feature that allows to create a link between two not consecutive Cues, so that a Go command to the first Cue will fade to the linked Cue instead of the following Cue.

A common sample of using the Link is when a show is programmed on a main Qlist, and for a temporary cause a part of the scenes has to be ignored without deleting them (maybe an actor is missing and his part of the show will not be performed this time, but it will be performed again on the next dates of the tour, as soon as he comes back).

The Link allows to connect the Cue before the ``missing part'' to the cue that follows it, without losing the data contained in the ``jumped'' cues. When removing the Link the cues in between will be restored.

Cues may be linked using the Link popup.

👉 In cue sheet, cues that are disabled because of a Link are grayed out.

Cues may be linked out of sequence using the following methods:

\textbf{Link with keypad and Toolbar}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Source {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} \{LINK\} on the Editor Toolbar - The Link popup will appear.
\item
  Type link destination {[}\#{]} (Do not press {[}CUE{]}).
\item
  Press Enter or Apply to complete the operation.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Link with Cue Settings}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Source {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}SETTINGS{]} - Cue Settings popup will open.
\item
  Tap \{Link\} - The Link popup will appear.
\item
  Type, scroll or drag to the desired destination cue.
\item
  Press Enter or Apply to complete the operation.
\item
  Press Enter or Apply again to close the Settings popup.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Remove a Link with keypad and Toolbar}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Select the source {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} and tap \{Link\} on the Editor Toolbar, the Link popup will appear.
\item
  Press {[}RELEASE{]} to remove the link.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Remove a Link with Cue Settings}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Select the source cue and open the Link popup by any of the above methods.
\item
  If the proper source cue has been selected, the \{Unlink\} key will appear.
\item
  Tap \{Unlink\} key to remove the Link.
\end{enumerate}

👉 If the source cue is forgotten, check the \textbf{Link \#} field in the Live Master Controller Cue Sheet.

\hypertarget{loop}{%
\subsubsection{Loop}\label{loop}}

In many consoles the only way to create a loop is to create a link from the end of a range of cues back to the beginning. Follow time must then be assigned to all of the cues. In Vibe this is also possible, but a shorter more efficient method is also provided.

\textbf{Loop} function is an evolution of the \textbf{Link} function, and it can be also assigned either with the keypad and Toolbar or with the Cue Settings popup.

Cues may be looped using the Loop popup.

In live playback view, cues that are set as Loop Cues have an orange color.

Cues may be looped using the following methods:

\textbf{Loop with keypad and Toolbar}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Start {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} tap \{LOOP\} on the Editor Toolbar - The Loop popup will appear.
\item
  Type end {[}\#{]} or use \{To Cue No\} field list to select the end cue.
\item
  Press Enter or Apply to complete the operation.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Loop with Cue Setting}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Start {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}SETTINGS{]} - Cue Settings popup will open.
\item
  Tap \{Loop\} - Loop popup will appear.
\item
  Type end Cue {[}\#{]} or scroll the value picker to the desired end cue {[}\#{]}.
\item
  Press Enter or Apply to complete the operation.
\item
  Press Enter or Apply again to close the Settings popup.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Loop Count}

Loop Count define the number of times that a loop is running before stopping at the end of the loop.

The Loop Count may be assigned using any of the above methods to open the Loop popup.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Make sure \{Auto Loop\} is selected.
\item
  Under the \{\# Of Time To Loop\} field, Type loop count number or scroll the value picker to the desired loop count number.
\item
  Press Enter or tap apply to complete the operation.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Auto Follow Loop}

Auto Follow loops advance to the cue following the loop, after completion of the loop count.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Toggle \{Auto Follow\} - On/Off as required.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Set a Loop as Manual Loop}

A Manual Loop is a Loop that does not advance automatically.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Toggle \{Auto Loop\} - Off
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Remove a Loop with keypad and Toolbar}

The Qlist must be released first.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Select the start {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} and tap \{LOOP\} on the Editor Toolbar - The Loop popup will appear.
\item
  Press {[}RELEASE{]} to remove the loop.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Remove a Loop with Cue Settings}

The Qlist must be released first.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Select the start cue and open the Loop popup by any of the above methods.
\item
  If the proper start cue has been selected, the \{Unloop\} key will appear.
\item
  Tap the \{Unloop\} key to remove the Link.
\item
  Press Enter or tap Apply to complete the operation.
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{Chasers-10.6}{%
\subsection{Chasers}\label{Chasers-10.6}}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Chasers are Qlists that playback Cues following the Chaser timing.
\item
  Chasers ignore the Cues' times and run a loop based on the Chaser Time.
\item
  Chasers contain a sequence of Cues in a specified order.
\item
  Each Chaser may have up to 8,000 cues.
\item
  Chasers containing Cues must be assigned to Controllers to output to the stage.
\item
  Every Chaser can be assigned to many different controllers at the same time, and played back individually.
\item
  The playback options of a Chaser are a combination of the general Chase Settings and the Cue Settings of the internal Cues.
\item
  Chasers may be given Text labels.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{chase-settings}{%
\subsubsection{Chase Settings}\label{chase-settings}}

Chase Settings can be stored in two ways: either to the Qlist itself or to the Controller that currently has the Qlist assigned.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}QLIST{]} {[}\#{]} {[}SETTINGS{]}
\end{itemize}

Opens the Qlist Settings popup, to store the settings directly to the Qlist.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap on the Qlist name on the top of the Playback Status box, or {[}SETTINGS{]} {[}CONTROLLER{]}
\end{itemize}

Opens the Controller Settings with the Qlist Settings Tab, to store the settings only to the specific controller. In this case the \textbf{Store to Qlist} button may be enabled to store the settings to the Qlist as well.

In the Qlist Settings popup, set the \textbf{Qlist Mode} to Chaser and tap on the Chase Settings tab to set the settings of the chaser.

The \textbf{Restore default} button rolls back the changed settings to the default ones.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{Chaser Mode\} - Defines if the Chaser Time will be controlled by the \textbf{Rate} or by \textbf{BPM}.

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    \{Rate\} - The multiplier of the chase timing, based on the Qlist whole timing.
  \item
    \{BPM\} - The timing is set by beats per minute, enabling the Time BPM wheel.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \{Chase Pattern\} - Defines the Cues order of the chaser.

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    \{Normal\} - The Cues' are played individually, the sequence is played forwards in the stored order and the last Cue loops to the first Cue.
  \item
    \{Build\} - The Cues' are played one on top of the previous, the sequence is played forwards in the stored order and the last Cue loops to the first Cue.
  \item
    \{Bounce\} - The Cues' are played individually, the sequence is played forwards in the stored order until the last Cue and then backwards until the first Cue.
  \item
    \{Bounce Build\} - The Cues' are played one on top of the previous, the sequence is played forwards in the stored order until the last Cue and then backwards until the first Cue.
  \item
    \{Random\} - The Cues' are played individually, the sequence is played in random order.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \{Time\} - If the Chaser Mode is set on BPM, the Time wheel sets the number of BPM. Default time is 30 bpm.
\item
  \{Loop Count\} - Defines the number of loops of the chaser. If the Loop Count is 0 the number of loops will be infinite.
\item
  \{Properties\}
\item
  \{Int Limit\} - Sets a limit to the intensity parameters.
\item
  \{X-fade\} - Sets the crossfade time between the cues from 0\% to 100\%.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Tracksheet-10.7}{%
\subsection{Tracksheet}\label{Tracksheet-10.7}}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  The Tracksheet view shows the parameter values of the sequence of Cues inside a Qlist.
\item
  Qlist Tracksheet is used to check if the values played back by a Cue are stored as \textbf{hard-values} or are \textbf{tracked} from previous Cues.
\item
  The Tracksheet can be also used to directly adjust and update the Cues.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Each line represents a Cue, each column represents a parameter of a fixture stored in the Qlist.
\item
  Empty cells show that the param has no value on that Cue.
\item
  Colored text cells show the param value, defined by the following color coding:

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    \{Cyan\} - The value is stored in the Cue.
  \item
    \{Blue\} - The value is stored and it is higher than the previous Cue.
  \item
    \{Green\} - The value is stored and it is lower than the previous Cue.
  \item
    \{Red\} - The value is stored and it is the same as the previous Cue.
  \item
    \{Violet\} - The value is not stored in the Cue, but it is tracked from a previous Cue.
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{menu}{%
\subsubsection{Menu}\label{menu}}

The view's menu contains some filters and actions that can be applied to the Tracksheet view.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{Qlist\} - The Qlist button of the Menu is used to navigate and load the Qlist. Only one Qlist can be shown at a time. The loaded Qlist's number and name are shown on the button.
\item
  \{Filters\} - Open a popup where the Fixtures and Parameters shown in the Tracksheet view can be filtered.
\item
  \{Actions\} - Refreshes the view in case changes have been done to the loaded Qlist or to the Filters. If values are changed on the view, shows the update actions and options.
\end{itemize}

👉 The visualized values on the Tracksheet view are not automatically refreshed.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  When the \{Refresh\} key's background is Green the visualized data are up to date.
\item
  When the \{Refresh\} key's background is Red the data or the fixture and param filters may have been modified. Tap on the \{Refresh\} key to update the visualization to the actual stored state.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{filters}{%
\subsubsection{Filters}\label{filters}}

Qlists and Cues may contain a large amount of values, so the Filters may be applied to load on the view only the columns referred to the fixtures and parameters that need to be checked.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{Editor\} - Grabs the Tracksheet Fixture selection from the current Editor selection.
\item
  \{Fixtures\} - Opens the Qlist Tracksheet Filter popup on the Fixtures tab, where the fixtures shown on the view can be selected. Use the \{From Editor\} key to grab the current fixture selection from the Editor.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \{Params\} - Opens the Qlist Tracksheet Filter popup on the Parameters tab, where the parameters shown on the view can be selected.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \{Format\} - Defines the format of data shown on the cell, such as the value in \%, Decimal, Text, Text+\%, or the Cell Time.
\end{itemize}

👉 When a new filter of fixtures and params is configured, the Tracksheet view keeps showing the previous data but the Refresh key becomes Red. Tap on \{Refresh\} to make it Green and apply the filters.

\hypertarget{update-actions}{%
\subsubsection{Update Actions}\label{update-actions}}

\textbf{Update Cues with Tracksheet}

The values stored inside each Cue can be directly edited into the Tracksheet view.

To avoid tracking conflicts, a single Cue can be edited at a time.

To modify any value, tap on the cell and enter the new value with the numeric keyboard, then press Enter or tap outside the selected Cue's line and it will get a gray background (Cue Edit Mode). The edited params will be colored in White.

When a line is in Cue Edit Mode, the Tracksheet Actions keys will appear on the view's Menu:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{UPDATE\} - Updates the Cue with the edited values.
\item
  \{Tracking Options\} - Defines if the update operation will be Track, Skip or Cue Only.
\item
  \{Backward Track\} - Enables the BackTrack, updating the edited values in the origin tracking Cue instead of the currently selected Cue.
\item
  \{Library Track\} - Enables the tracking of the library reference instead of using the hard value without references.
\item
  \{Clear Changes\} - Restores the Cue without updating the edited values.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{playback}{%
\section{Playback}\label{playback}}

👉 \textbf{Playback} the Vibe show through the most flexible sets of physical and virtual controllers, sliders, knobs and buttons.

This chapter deals with the assignment of Qlists and Scenes to the Controllers and the management of playback settings.

\textbf{Chapter index:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Physical-Controllers-11.1}{11.1 Physical Controllers}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Virtual-Controllers-11.2}{11.2 Virtual Controllers}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Controller-Keys-11.3}{11.3 Controller Keys}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Controller-Configuration-11.4}{11.4 Controller Configuration}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Live-Controller-11.5}{11.5 Live Controller}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Teach-Rate-11.6}{11.6 Teach Rate}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Physical-Controllers-11.1}{%
\subsection{Physical Controllers}\label{Physical-Controllers-11.1}}

Playback is done with Controllers, that can be both physical or virtual.

Controllers are divided into different types, characterized by the quantity of buttons, faders and knobs available.

Vibe has five controller types, with the following quantities and numbering:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{A/B Crossfader}: Non-Motorized pair of Sliders with dipless crossfade and Two buttons
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  1 Non-motorized A/B dipless crossfader - Numbered 401
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{1}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Slider}: Motorized Slider Controllers with Three buttons
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  30 pages of 100 Motorized Sliders - Numbered 1.1 ⟶ 30.100
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{2}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Qkey}: Pressable knob or Single button Controllers
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  30 pages of 100 Qkeys - Numbered 1.101 ⟶ 30.200
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{3}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{AuxKey}: Single button Controllers
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  30 pages of 100 Auxkeys - Numbered 1.201 ⟶ 30.300
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{4}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Global Slider}: Non-Motorized Slider Controllers
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  100 Non-motorized Global Sliders - Numbered 301 ⟶ 400
\end{itemize}

👉 Controllers are numbered from right to left to facilitate continuous numbering with physical and virtual controller wings.

Controllers are used to playback or execute a number of stored objects:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Qlists and Cues
\item
  Chasers
\item
  Scenes
\item
  Submasters
\item
  Groupmasters
\item
  Libraries
\item
  Macros
\end{itemize}

The following samples explain how to assign a Qlist to any controller, but the syntax to assign any other stored object is the same.

Assign a Qlist to a Controller:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  {[}QLIST{]} {[}\#{]} OR Object Softkey
\item
  {[}HERE{]} to any button of the target controller.
\end{enumerate}

👉 Blank Qlists may also be assigned to any controller.

👉 If a non existing Qlist is assigned, it will be created.

Assign a range of Qlists to consecutive Controllers:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  {[}QLIST{]} {[}\#{]} → {[}QLIST{]} {[}\#{]}
\item
  {[}HERE{]} to any button of the controller that will be the first of the range.
\end{enumerate}

Assign a Single Cue to a controller:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  {[}QLIST{]} {[}\#{]} {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]}
\item
  {[}HERE{]} to any button of the target controller.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Controller Color Codes}

The objects assigned to any controller are identified by the Controller Color Codes, that are referred to the physical buttons' LEDs and to the virtual controllers' background.

Controller color coding from {[}SELECT{]} Master Controller:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Cyan} = Initial Hard Value
\item
  \textbf{Blue} = Hard Value Changing Upward
\item
  \textbf{Green} = Hard Value Changing Downward
\item
  \textbf{Purple} = Tracked Value
\item
  \textbf{Red} = Redundant Block (may be released with \{\textbf{Unblock}\})
\item
  \textbf{Magenta} = Skip Value
\end{itemize}

Controller color coding from non-{[}SELECT{]} Master Controller:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Yellow} = Output from a Qlist, Cue, Scene
\item
  \textbf{Submaster} or \textbf{Group Master} =
\end{itemize}

Controller Display and Backlit Controller Key's color coding:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Top Button:

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \textbf{Dim White} = Unloaded
  \item
    \textbf{White} = Loaded
  \item
    \textbf{Green} = Qlist, Cue Active
  \item
    \textbf{Red} = Chaser Active
  \item
    \textbf{Yellow} = Scene Active
  \item
    \textbf{Blue} = Sub or Group Master @ FL
  \item
    \textbf{Any of above Flashing} = In Progress
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Middle Button:

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \textbf{Dim White} = Unloaded
  \item
    \textbf{White} = Loaded
  \item
    \textbf{Blue} = Select (Master Controller)
  \item
    \textbf{Flashing Red} = Back
  \item
    \textbf{Flashing Yellow} = Pause
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Bottom Button:

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \textbf{Dim White} = Unloaded
  \item
    \textbf{White} = Loaded
  \item
    \textbf{Blue} = Select (Master Controller)
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{grand-master-blackout}{%
\subsubsection{Grand Master + BlackOut}\label{grand-master-blackout}}

The main controllers of the console are the Grand Master, or GM, and the BlackOut, or B.O.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  The Grand Master proportionally limits the output values of all the DMX addresses that have a Dimmer parameter patched.
\item
  The BlackOut toggles at Full or Zero the output values of all the DMX addresses that have a Dimmer parameter patched.
\item
  The Grand Master slider and the BlackOut button are the only ``special'' controllers that cannot be customized; their functions cannot be changed and they cannot have any object assignment.
\item
  The Grand Master slider and the BlackOut button have priority over the whole system, including all the Playback controllers and also the Editor.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{ab-crossfader}{%
\subsubsection{A/B CrossFader}\label{ab-crossfader}}

The \textbf{Manual Crossfader} set is a controller with 2 non-motorized sliders and 2 buttons.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  The default action of the right button is Go
\item
  The default action of the left button is Hold/Back
\item
  The A/B CrossFader Pair is provided for manual ``Theater Style'' playback of cues.
\item
  By default the fader on the left will control dimmer parameters fading \textbf{in.}
\item
  By default the fader on the right will control all other values, including the dimmer that are fading \textbf{out.}
\item
  Parameter values that match will not dip even if the A and B are at different levels.
\item
  The A/B Controller Actions popup allows to set if the sliders' behaviour should be Splitted (\textbf{Separated}) or Merged (\textbf{In/Out}).
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{slider}{%
\subsubsection{Slider}\label{slider}}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The \textbf{Slider} set is a controller with 1 motorized slider and 3 buttons.
\end{itemize}

When a \textbf{Qlist} is assigned:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  By default the fader will control intensity parameters
\item
  The default action of the top button is Go
\item
  The default action of the mid button is Hold/Back
\item
  The default action of the bottom button is Flash
\end{itemize}

When a \textbf{Scene} is assigned:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  By default the fader will control all parameters
\item
  The default action of the top button is Go
\item
  The default action of the mid button is Hold/Back
\item
  The default action of the bottom button is Flash
\end{itemize}

When a \textbf{Submaster} is assigned:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  By default the fader will act as a submaster
\item
  The default action of the top button is Group Select
\item
  The default action of the mid button is Group Deselect
\item
  The default action of the bottom button is Flash
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{qkey}{%
\subsubsection{Qkey}\label{qkey}}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The \textbf{Qkey} set is a controller with 1 knob or slider (virtual only) and 1 single button.
\end{itemize}

When a \textbf{Qlist} is assigned:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  By default the knob will control intensity parameters
\item
  The default action of the button is Go
\end{itemize}

When a \textbf{Scene} is assigned:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  By default the knob will control all parameters
\item
  The default action of the button is Go/Release
\end{itemize}

When a \textbf{Submaster} is assigned:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  By default the knob will act as a submaster
\item
  The default action of the button is Group Select
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{auxkey}{%
\subsubsection{AuxKey}\label{auxkey}}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The \textbf{Auxkey} set is a controller with 1 slider (virtual only) and 1 single button.
\end{itemize}

When a \textbf{Qlist} is assigned:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  By default the knob will control intensity parameters
\item
  The default action of the button is Go
\end{itemize}

When a \textbf{Scene} is assigned:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  By default the knob will control all parameters
\item
  The default action of the button is Go/Release
\end{itemize}

When a \textbf{Submaster} is assigned:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  By default the knob will act as a submaster
\item
  The default action of the button is Group Select
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{global-slider}{%
\subsubsection{Global Slider}\label{global-slider}}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The \textbf{Global Slider} set is a controller with 1 non-motorized slider and 1 button.
\end{itemize}

When a \textbf{Qlist} is assigned:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  By default the fader will control intensity parameters
\item
  The default action of the button is Go
\end{itemize}

When a \textbf{Scene} is assigned:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  By default the fader will control all parameters
\item
  The default action of the button is Go
\end{itemize}

When a \textbf{Submaster} is assigned:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  By default the fader will act as a submaster
\item
  The default action of the button is Group Select
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Virtual-Controllers-11.2}{%
\subsection{Virtual Controllers}\label{Virtual-Controllers-11.2}}

All the controllers can be added on the layout as Virtual Controllers.

Any controller can be both physical and virtual, and the list of all the Virtual Controller Views available can be found in the Playback Views submenus.

Virtual Controllers may be added as a single part (fader or button), as complete set, or in sets of 5 consecutive controllers.

The Virtual Controllers may be added to the Vibe Layout, moved, assigned and resized as preferred.

Vibe software makes no distinction between physical and virtual controllers, this allows the system hardware to be virtually scaled.

\hypertarget{grand-master-blackout-1}{%
\subsubsection{Grand Master + BlackOut}\label{grand-master-blackout-1}}

Open the virtual Grand Master with {[}SHIFT{]} + {[}B.O.{]}

In this view the Grand Master level can be set, it can be Disabled, and it also can be set to operate on a user-defined range, from 0\%-100\% to 0\%-200\%.

The Proportional Grand Master Level can be used for example in touring productions, where the rehearsal and the lighting programming may be done in a medium-sized venue for a show that will be performed both in smaller and bigger stages. Setting the G.M. at 100\% with a level range up to 200\% will allow the operator to keep enough margin to light up the bigger venues without need to add more fixtures.

The Disable GM button completely inhibits the Grand Master, while it is possibly to exclude form the Grand Master just specific DMX addresses. This is done via the Address View by editing the Address Properties.

A Virtual BlackOut button can be added to the layout by selecting it from the Single Button popup into the Program Views menu.

\hypertarget{ab-crossfader-1}{%
\subsubsection{A/B Crossfader}\label{ab-crossfader-1}}

The Virtual A/B Crossfader has a Locker between the sliders used to set if the sliders' behaviour should be Splitted (\textbf{Separated}) or Merged (\textbf{In/Out}).

\hypertarget{slider-1}{%
\subsubsection{Slider}\label{slider-1}}

Virtual Sliders show the controller's page and number, the name and number of the content loaded, the current and next Cue name and number, the fader level, and the icons of the functions assigned to the buttons and faders.

\hypertarget{qkeys}{%
\subsubsection{Qkeys}\label{qkeys}}

Qkeys can have a virtual button and a virtual fader, or a virtual set of fader and button, independently from the corresponding physical controller's hardware (single button or knob).

Virtual QKeys show the controller's page and number, the name and number of the content loaded, the current and next Cue name and number, the fader level, and the icons of the functions assigned to the buttons and faders.

\hypertarget{auxkey-1}{%
\subsubsection{Auxkey}\label{auxkey-1}}

Auxkeys can have a virtual button and a virtual fader, or a virtual set of fader and button, independently from the corresponding physical controller's hardware (single button).

Virtual AuxKeys show the controller's page and number, the name and number of the content loaded, the current and next Cue name and number, the fader level, and the icons of the functions assigned to the buttons and faders.

Auxkey pages can be switched from the Smart Screen's Programming View with the virtual up/down keys.

\hypertarget{global-slider-1}{%
\subsubsection{Global Slider}\label{global-slider-1}}

Global Sliders can have a virtual button and a virtual fader, or a virtual set of fader and button, independently from the corresponding physical controller's hardware (the physical global sliders are only on the main Vibe console, but the virtual global sliders can be added to any layout).

Virtual Global Sliders show the controller's page and number, the name and number of the content loaded, the current and next Cue name and number, the fader level, and the icons of the functions assigned to the buttons and faders.

\hypertarget{Controller-Keys-11.3}{%
\subsection{Controller Keys}\label{Controller-Keys-11.3}}

\hypertarget{master-keys}{%
\subsubsection{Master Keys}\label{master-keys}}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  The Master Controller is the main controller to which the Master Keys and the syntax shortcuts are referred to.
\item
  One of the controllers is always assigned as the Master Controller.
\item
  Not more than one controller can be assigned as the Master Controller at the same time.
\item
  Any Controller may become the Master Controller at any time.
\item
  To assign a controller as the Master, press \textbf{{[}SELECT{]}} {[}HERE{]} to any of the buttons of the controller.
\item
  The LED color of the second button and the background of the virtual controller that identify the Master Controller is {dark blue}.
\end{itemize}

{The A/B is only linked to the Master Controller if the {[}SELECT{]} is assigned to A/B.}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The Master Controller is linked to the Master Controller Keys.
\end{itemize}

The Master Keys have 4 buttons:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  {[}GO{]} - Initiates a forward timed fade between cues in the selected Qlist using the \textbf{Cue Time}.
\item
  {[}BACK{]} - Initiates a backward timed fade to the previous cue using the \textbf{Default Back Time}.
\item
  {[}HOLD{]} - Pauses the progress of a fade.
\item
  Pressing {[}HOLD{]} again resumes the transition.
\item
  Pressing {[}GO{]} will resume the transition as well.
\item
  Pressing {[}BACK{]} will fade to the previous cue.
\item
  {[}GOTO{]} - Advances to the specified cue using the \textbf{GOTO Time}.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Master shortcuts}

There are some syntax shortcuts used to speed up the programming that are always referred to the Master Controller. The most common ones are:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  {[}STORE{]} {[}STORE{]} - stores a Cue to the first free position of the Qlist loaded on the Master.
\item
  {[}STORE{]} {[}ENTER{]} - stores a Cue to the first free position of the Qlist loaded on the Master.
\item
  {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}STORE{]} - stores a Cue \# to the Qlist loaded on the Master.
\item
  {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}UPDATE{]} - updates the Cue \# of the Qlist loaded on the Master.
\item
  {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}EDIT{]} or {[}TEXT{]} or {[}TIME{]} or any other command - applies the command to the Cue \# of the Qlist loaded on the Master.
\item
  {[}UPDATE{]} - opens the update popup, where the Master Controller can be assigned as Source Filter.
\item
  {[}GRAB{]} - opens the Grab popup, where the Master Controller can be set as Filter.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{GOTO Commands}

GOTO commands can be performed through different syntax depending on the user preference.

GOTO applies a GO command to a Cue of the Qlist that is currently on the Master controller, using the Default GOTO Time specified in the Timing settings:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  {[}GOTO{]} {[}\#{]} {[}ENTER{]}
\item
  {[}GOTO{]} {[}\#{]} {[}GO{]}
\end{itemize}

More GOTO syntax options are available to define a specific Time or a different Qlist.

GOTO a cue on the Master Controller using system GOTO Time:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}GOTO{]}
\end{itemize}

GOTO a cue on the Master Controller using a specified Time:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}TIME{]} - Enter time value {[}GOTO{]}
\end{itemize}

GOTO a cue on the Master Controller using system Cue Time:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}TIME{]} {[}GOTO{]}
\end{itemize}

GOTO a cue on the Master Controller using Goto Cue popup:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Press {[}GOTO{]} - Goto Cue popup will open.
\item
  Select \{Qlist\} if not the current Qlist on the Master Controller.
\item
  Select the \{Cue\}.
\item
  Select behavior.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{Default GOTO time\} - Uses system default GOTO Time.
\item
  \{Cue Time\} - Uses Cue's recorded time.
\item
  \{Custom\} - Set GOTO time using the Custom Time wheel or tap the center of the wheel until it turns red and set the time value from the keypad.
\item
  \{Release Master PB on Top\} - Releases the Master Controller.
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{4}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap Apply or press {[}ENTER{]} or {[}GO{]} to close the popup and execute the GOTO.
\end{enumerate}

{If a Qlist other than the one that is currently on the Master Controller is selected, that Qlist will replace the current one on the Master Controller.}

GOTO a cue NOT on the Master Controller:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}QLIST{]} {[}\#{]} {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}GO{]} (Controller must have a GO button assigned).
\end{itemize}

GOTO Cue Zero (Cue Zero is essentially \{Release PB\}):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}CUE{]} {[}0{]} {[}GOTO{]}
\end{itemize}

GO out of sequence directly to a Cue on the Master Controller (in cue time):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}GO{]}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{operation-keys}{%
\subsubsection{Operation Keys}\label{operation-keys}}

Operational Keys allow to manage the controllers with basic and fundamental operations.

The Operation Keys are physical buttons on the panel and can be added as virtual a view on the layout.

The Operational Keys are: Select, Load, Release, Free, Protect

\begin{itemize}
\item
  {[}\textbf{Select}{]} - Sets the target controller as the Master.
\item
  {[}\textbf{Load}{]} - Preloads a controller with a specified cue \#, but does not advance to that cue.
\item
  {[}\textbf{Release}{]} - Releases the destination controller from affecting the stage (Turns it off).
\item
  {[}\textbf{Free}{]} - Unloads the content from the controller (Makes it empty).
\item
  {[}\textbf{Protect}{]} - Locks the system with a user defined one-time password.
\end{itemize}

{{[}Select{]} {[}Load{]} {[}Release{]} and {[}Free{]} do not work with the big Master Controller buttons, only with any button on the controller that is associated with it as the {[}SELECT{]} controller.}

GOTO and LOAD are used to crossfade to cues out of sequence.

By default:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  GOTO immediately transitions to the specified cue in the system \textbf{GOTO Time}.
\item
  LOAD preloads controllers. The next {[}GO{]} will transition to the preloaded cue in \textbf{Cue Time}.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Select Operations}

Set a target controller as the Master:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}SELECT{]} {[}HERE{]} to any controller button.
\end{itemize}

The Mid and Flash buttons' LEDs and the Virtual controller's background become Blue.

\textbf{Load Operations}

Load a cue on the Master Controller:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}LOAD{]} {[}HERE{]} - To Controller assigned as {[}SELECT{]}
\item
  Press {[}GO{]} - On Controller assigned as {[}SELECT{]}
\end{enumerate}

LOAD a cue NOT on the Master Controller:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  {[}QLIST{]} {[}\#{]} {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}LOAD{]} {[}HERE{]} - Qlist and Cue will be preloaded.
\item
  Press {[}GO{]} - Controller will execute loaded cue.
\end{enumerate}

{If a Qlist other than the one that is currently on the Controller is specified, that Qlist will replace the current one with the specified cue pending.}

\textbf{Release Operations}

Vibe has two types of release functions, Editor Release and Controller Release. The following deals with Controller Release.

Controller Release is done using the system Release Time, as set in system default settings.

Release a controller:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}RELEASE{]} {[}HERE{]} to any controller button.
\end{itemize}

Pressing Release turns the Release button {Red}.

Release more controllers:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}RELEASE{]} {[}RELEASE{]} {[}HERE{]} {[}HERE{]} {[}HERE{]}\ldots{} to any controller button.
\end{itemize}

Pressing Release two times turns the Release button {flashing Red}.

Release All controllers.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}VIBE{]} + {[}RELEASE{]}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Free Operations}

Free unloads a controller and makes it empty, but it does not delete the loaded object.

Free a controller:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}FREE{]} {[}HERE{]} to any controller button.
\end{itemize}

Pressing Free turns the Free button {Red}.

Free more controllers:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}FREE{]} {[}FREE{]} {[}HERE{]} {[}HERE{]} {[}HERE{]}\ldots{} to any controller button.
\end{itemize}

Pressing Free two times turns the Free button {flashing Red}.

Free All controllers.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}VIBE{]} + {[}FREE{]}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Protect Operations}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Protect is used to prevent the console to be operated by unwanted people.
\item
  Protect uses a user defined one-time password to Lock the system, keep the password in mind and use it carefully.
\end{itemize}

To Lock the system:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}PROTECT{]} {[}PASSWORD{]} (any sequence of numbers) {[}ENTER{]}
\end{itemize}

To Unlock the system:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}PROTECT{]} {[}PASSWORD{]} {[}ENTER{]}
\end{itemize}

OR

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap on the screen, Tap {[}UNLOCK{]}, insert the {[}PASSWORD{]}, Tap {[}UNLOCK{]}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{command-keys}{%
\subsubsection{Command Keys}\label{command-keys}}

Command Keys allow to manage the playback of the objects on the controllers, providing ``On the Fly'' access to button functions not directly assigned to controllers.

The Command Keys are physical buttons on the panel and can be added as a virtual view on the layout.

The Command Keys are:

{[}\textbf{Assert}{]} - Takes back control of parameters that have been ``robbed'' by other controllers. Assert acts as a Go command, but recalling the current Cue instead of the next one.

{[}\textbf{Flash}{]} - While pressed turns a controller On and at 100\%.

{[}\textbf{Latch}{]} - {Not implemented yet.}.

{[}\textbf{Solo}{]} - Turns off the intensity parameters on all controllers excluding the target controller.

{[}\textbf{\textbar\textbar\textless{}}{]} - Hold/Back command on a controller. If a controller is running, pauses the controller. If a controller is static, initiates a BACK command to the previous Cue using the Back Time or the Cue Time (as set in system timing settings).

{[}\textbf{\textless-\textgreater{}}{]} - Reverses the sequence direction (mainly used with loops and chasers).

{[}\textbf{\textless\textless\textless{}}{]} - Steps backwards through a Qlist ignoring time 👉 {[}VIBE{]} + {[}BACK{]} will also step backwards through the list.

{[}\textbf{\textgreater\textgreater\textgreater{}}{]} - Steps forwards through a Qlist ignoring time 👉 {[}VIBE{]} + {[}GO{]} will also step forwards through the list.

{[}\textbf{Go}{]} - Go command on a controller. Initiates a GO command to the next cue using the Cue Time.

{[}\textbf{Go/Release}{]} - First press initiates a GO command, second press initiates a RELEASE command.

{[}\textbf{On}{]} - Instantly turns ON a controller.

{[}\textbf{Off}{]} - Instantly turns OFF a controller.

👉 All Command Keys work with the same syntax, that is also common to the Free and Release Operation Keys.

Command a controller:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}COMMAND{]} {[}HERE{]} to any controller button.
\end{itemize}

Pressing the Command turns the Command button {Red}.

Command more controllers:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}COMMAND{]} {[}COMMAND{]} {[}HERE{]} {[}HERE{]} {[}HERE{]}\ldots{} to any controller button.
\end{itemize}

Pressing the Command two times turns the Command button {flashing Red}.

Command All controllers.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}VIBE{]} + {[}COMMAND{]}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{mode-keys}{%
\subsubsection{Mode Keys}\label{mode-keys}}

The Controller Mode keys are located at near the motorized sliders.

The motorized sliders may be assigned to any one of 4 modes.

Controller modes are are designed for ``On the Fly'' usage, allowing a quick modification to various rate and effect parameters.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  {[}\textbf{FADERS}{]} - Normal behavior for the Sliders, as assigned in Controller Definitions
\item
  {[}\textbf{RATE}{]} - The Sliders control the Rate of the fades, loops, effects, and chasers on the controller. Motorized faders will move to an optimized position of 33\% allowing for proportional adjustment of a controller's rate.
\item
  {[}\textbf{SIZE}{]} - The Sliders proportionally adjust the size of effects running on the controller.
\item
  {[}\textbf{CHANNEL}{]} - The Sliders control single DMX Addresses. {Not implemented yet.}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Controller-Configuration-11.4}{%
\subsection{Controller Configuration}\label{Controller-Configuration-11.4}}

Controller buttons and sliders may be customized to allow maximum flexibility.

When Objects are initially assigned to Controllers, the buttons are configured as per the System Settings Defaults for Controller Actions and Controller Settings.

Each Controller may be configured individually by pressing {[}SETTINGS{]} {[}HERE{]} to any button of the controller or by tapping the box of the Playback Status view.

\hypertarget{playback-status}{%
\subsubsection{Playback Status}\label{playback-status}}

Playback Status is a view that shows the current live information of the controller's content and configuration.

The PB Status may be added to the layout as a single box, for a single controller, or as a set of five consecutive controllers, usually positioned in the lower part of the screen.

The view's settings allow to set which target controller is shown, that may be the A/B Crossfader, a Slider, a Qkey, an AuxKey or a Global Slider.

The contents shown on the PB Status are:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Controller Page and Number
\item
  Loaded object Number and Name
\item
  Loaded Object Type (based on the background color coding)
\item
  Fader Level (with Purple background)
\item
  Crossfade Rate Level (with Green background)
\item
  Previous, Current, Next Cue (for Qlists and Chasers)
\item
  Fader and Buttons Function Icons
\end{itemize}

Playback Status are also used for a direct access to the controller's actions and settings and for the content's properties configuration.

\hypertarget{controller-defaults}{%
\subsubsection{Controller Defaults}\label{controller-defaults}}

Controllers' Defaults are divided in Actions and Settings.

To edit the defaults, open System Settings and tap on the Defaults Tab, then:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Tap Controller Actions to open the Default Controller Actions popup
\item
  Tap Controller Settings to open the Default Controller Settings popup
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{controller-actions-1}{%
\subsubsection{Controller Actions}\label{controller-actions-1}}

Assign Buttons and Slider Behavior:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Press {[}SETTINGS{]} {[}HERE{]} to any button of the controller that has an object assigned to it - The \textbf{Controller Actions} popup will appear.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{quote}
Alternatively, tap any of the controller Playback Status on the monitor.
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
Alternatively, press {[}Play-B{]} {[}\#.\#{]} {[}SETTINGS{]} on the hardkeys.
\end{quote}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{1}
\item
  Tap one of the assignable \{Button\} or \{Slider\} - A context sensitive drop down menu based on the object assigned and the specific button selected will appear.
\item
  Choose a Control behavior from the menu.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Assignable Button Behavior:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Blackout} - Disables the Button.
\item
  \textbf{Back} - Fades to the previous Cue in Back Time.
\item
  \textbf{Flash} - Brings the Intensity to 100\%.
\item
  \textbf{Go} - Fades to the next Cue in Cue Time.
\item
  \textbf{Go Release} - Toggles between Go and Release commands in Cue Time and Release Time.
\item
  \textbf{Go Reverse} - Fades to the previous Cue in Cue Time.
\item
  \textbf{Group Deselect} - Deselects the Fixtures currently activated from the Controller.
\item
  \textbf{Group Release} - Releases the Fixtures currently activated from the Controller.
\item
  \textbf{Group Select} - Selects the Fixtures currently activated from the Controller.
\item
  \textbf{Hold} - Pauses the fade.
\item
  \textbf{Hold Back} - Pauses the fade if it is running, makes a Back command if the fade is static.
\item
  \textbf{None} - Disables the Button.
\item
  \textbf{Off} - Makes a Release command in Cut Time.
\item
  \textbf{On} - Makes a Go command in Cut Time.
\item
  \textbf{On Off} - Toggles between Go and Release in Cut Time.
\item
  \textbf{Release} - Releases the controller in Release Time.
\item
  \textbf{Solo} - Turns off the intensity parameters on all the other controllers.
\item
  \textbf{Step Back} - Fades to the previous Cue in Cut Time.
\item
  \textbf{Step Forward} - Fades to the Next Cue in Cut Time.
\item
  \textbf{Teach} - Adjusts the Rate of Effects and Chasers based on the Tap Beat press timing.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Assignable Slider Behavior:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{None} - Disables the Fader.
\item
  \textbf{Intensity Master} - Controls the Dimmer or Virtual Dimmer parameter of the Cue/Scene.
\item
  \textbf{Crossfade} - Acts as a single crossfader.
\item
  \textbf{Crossfade In} - Acts as the In crossfader of a dipless crossfader pair.
\item
  \textbf{Crossfade Out} - Acts as the Out crossfader of a dipless crossfader pair.
\item
  \textbf{All Parameters} - Controls all the parameters of the Cue/Scene.
\item
  \textbf{Non Dimmer} - Controls all the parameters except the Dimmer of the Cue/Scene.
\item
  \textbf{Crossfade Rate / BPM} - Proportionally adjusts the Rate of the Chaser.
\item
  \textbf{FX Rate} - Proportionally adjusts the Rate of the running Effects.
\item
  \textbf{FX Size} - Proportionally adjusts the Size of the running Effects.
\item
  \textbf{FX Offset} - Proportionally adjusts the Offset of the running Effects.
\item
  \textbf{FX Base} - Proportionally adjusts the Base of the running Effects.
\item
  \textbf{Submaster} - Turns the Scene into a Submaster of the fixtures involved. (available only for Scenes and Submaster)
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{controller-settings-1}{%
\subsubsection{Controller Settings}\label{controller-settings-1}}

The left column allows to select one or more controllers to be configured.

If \textbf{NO} controllers are selected, the popup turns to the Default Controller Settings.

\textbf{Flash behavior}

Options to set the behavior of the flash button:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Release On Release
\item
  Pure LTP
\item
  Go On Flash
\item
  Latch
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Slider behavior}

Options to set the behavior of the slider:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Intensity Only
\item
  Go + Fade Parameters
\item
  Go + Jump Parameters
\item
  Release At Bottom
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Properties}

Options to set the properties of the controller:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Exclude from Snap
\item
  Exclude from Override
\item
  Exclude from Free All
\item
  Exclude from Release All
\item
  Prevent from Paging
\item
  Prevent from Grab
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Priority:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Vibe keeps an LTP priority list of which controllers own parameters and the order in which they were asserted. Every time a controller is turned on, its parameters are asserted and the controller moves to the top of the list. Parameters that are still on active controllers but are currently overridden are said to be ``robbed''. When the controller that owns the parameter is released from the stage, the last active controller to own the robbed parameter is now moved back to the top of the list and reasserted the parameter to the stage.
\item
  This feature is very useful for ``On the Fly'' programming where a main Qlist may be temporarily overridden by Qkeys or Controllers and quickly restored when they are released.
\end{itemize}

{The Editor is always the highest propriety and will override any values from controllers until they are released or The Editor is reset}.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Controllers assigned the same Priority number will be LTP among themselves.
\item
  Controllers with lower Priority will be overridden by controllers with higher Priority.
\item
  Controllers with higher Priority cannot be overridden by controllers with lower Priority.
\end{itemize}

Assign Priority:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Tap the top area of a controller display, the Settings popup will open.
\item
  Select the \{Controller Settings\} Tab.
\end{enumerate}

OR

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Press {[}SETTINGS{]} {[}HERE{]} to any controller button, the Settings popup will open.
\item
  Select the \{Controller Settings\} Tab.
\item
  Rotate the virtual wheel to the desired Priority Group number or tap the center of the wheel until it turns red and type a Priority number from 0 to 999.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Move Controllers}

A controller can be moved to any other, even if it's a different type of controller.

The controller will be moved with all its settings and state, including intensity value etc.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  {[}MOVE{]}, Source selection (Occupied controllers), Target selection (Empty controller)
\item
  {[}MOVE{]}, Source selection (Occupied controllers), @, Target selection (Any controller)
\item
  {[}PLAYBACK{]}, {[}\#{]}, {[}MOVE{]}, {[}PLAYBACK{]}, {[}\#{]}, {[}PASTE{]}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{object-settings}{%
\subsubsection{Object Settings}\label{object-settings}}

The Object Settings popups depend on the object type.

The hardkeys method to open every object settings popup is: {[}OBJECT{]} {[}\#{]} {[}SETTINGS{]}

where ``Object'' stands for Qlist, Cue, Scene, Library, Group, Snap, Macro

The softkeys method to open every object settings popup is:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Long Tap on the target object's Softkey (some options will appear) and move the finger on the Settings key.
\end{itemize}

If an object is loaded on a controller, also a direct way to access its settings is available.

The controllers method to open every object settings popup is:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap on the object name into the Playback Status box.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Qlist Settings}

The first column allows to select one or more Qlists to be configured.

If \textbf{NO} Qlists are selected, the popup turns to the Default Qlist Settings.

The second column contains the Qlist Settings for:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Qlist Mode} - Turn a Normal list of Cues into a Chaser and viceversa.
\item
  \textbf{Qlist Direction} - Toggle between Forward and Backward.
\item
  \textbf{Tracking} - Toggle between On and Off.
\item
  \textbf{Qlist Name} - Labels the Qlist, same as with the syntax {[}QLIST{]} {[}\#{]} {[}TEXT{]} or with the long tap on the Qlist softkey and then the Text option.
\end{itemize}

The third column contains the Qlist Preferences:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Back to Top on release} - Rollbacks to the first Cue when the controller is released.
\item
  \textbf{Sequence} - Enables/Disables the sequence of Cues.
\item
  \textbf{Mark Cue at the top of the list} - Adds a prepositioning Cue before the first Cue.
\item
  \textbf{Loop back to first} - A Go command on the last Cue will fade to the first Cue.
\item
  \textbf{Trigger} - Enables/Disables the Triggers of objects such as Macros linked to the Cues.
\item
  \textbf{Look Ahead} - Enables/Disables the Look Ahead option of the Qlist, with priority over the System Settings.
\end{itemize}

The fourth column contains the option for the Loop Go Behavior:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Go Next}
\item
  \textbf{Exit Loop At End}
\item
  \textbf{Fade To End Of Loop}
\item
  \textbf{Finish Loop And Pause}
\item
  \textbf{Restart Loop}
\item
  \textbf{Finish Cue And Pause}
\item
  \textbf{Finish Cue And Exit}
\end{itemize}

Each of these options has a short explanation in the box below the column.

The \textbf{Release Time Wheel} may be used to set a specific Release Time for the Qlist, with priority over the System Settings.

👉 If changes are made to individual controller Qlist Settings, those changes stay with the controller and are not copied or updated to other controllers with the same Qlist.

👉 If it is desirable to update what will be copied to future assignments of the Qlist, toggle \{\textbf{Save to Qlist}\} On. Changes made to the current Qlist Settings will then be passed along the further Qlist assignments on other Controllers, in place of the Default Qlist Settings.

👉 Qlist properties may be restored to their user defaults by tapping the \{\textbf{Restore Default}\} key.

\textbf{Chase Settings}

Chase Settings Tab is enabled only when the selected Qlist is in Chaser Mode.

The first column allows to select one or more Chasers to be configured.

If \textbf{NO} Chasers are selected, the popup turns to the Default Chaser Settings (not implemented yet).

The second column contains the Chaser Settings for:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Chaser Mode} - Toggle the Chaser Time between Rate and BPM.
\item
  \textbf{Chase Pattern} - Defines the Chase behavior affecting the order of the Cues.
\end{itemize}

The third column contains the Chaser Time Preferences:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{BPM} - Sets the BPM of the fades, only enabled when the Chaser Mode is BPM.
\item
  \textbf{Loop Count} - Sets the number of loops that the Chaser will execute. If the Loop Count is 0, the quantity of Loops will be infinite.
\end{itemize}

The fourth column contains the Chaser Properties:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Int Limit} - Limits the Intensity of the Dimmer parameters.
\item
  \textbf{X-fade} - Sets the crossfade between the Cues from 100\% (full fade) to 0\% (step fade)
\end{itemize}

👉 The same options to \{\textbf{Save to Qlist}\} and \{\textbf{Restore Default}\} of the Qlist Settings tab, are also available in the Chase Settings tab.

\textbf{Cue Settings}

Cue Settings popup opens by tapping on the Cue name into the Playback Status box.

The first column allows to select the Qlist containing the Cue to be configured.

The second column allows to select one or more Cues to be configured.

If \textbf{NO} Cues are selected, the popup turns to the Default Cue Settings.

The buttons under the column are used to:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Re-number} the selected Cues.
\item
  \textbf{Link} the selected Cue to another.
\item
  \textbf{Loop} the selected Cue to another, defining the number of loops to be executed.
\end{itemize}

The third column contains the Cue Time Settings.

The behavior lists all the timing possibilities and the Time wheel allows to adjust the selected ones.

The Default buttons bring the time back to the default setting.

The fourth column contains the Cue Properties.

Tracking Options define the tracking behavior of the selected Cue, that can be:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Cue Only}
\item
  \textbf{Skip Cue}
\item
  \textbf{Block Cue}
\item
  \textbf{Default (Track)}
\end{itemize}

Specific buttons are used to:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Force Black} - Enable/Disable a Force Black Cue before the selected Cue.
\item
  \textbf{Color Fade} and \textbf{Position Fade} - Toggle between Physical and Virtual to define is the fade to the selected Cue will involve the physical or the virtual parameters, respectively regarding the Color and the Position.
\end{itemize}

The White fields under the columns may be used to write the Cue's name and the Cue's notes.

👉 If the Cue selection is changed after editing the Cue settings, remember to press the APPLY button to store the changes, otherwise a popup will ask if you prefer to discard them.

👉 Differently from the Qlist and Chase Settings, that can be stored only on the controller, the Cue Settings are directly stored to the Cue and kept in case of further assignments.

\textbf{Scene Settings}

The first column allows to select one or more Scenes to be configured.

The second column contains the Scene Time Settings.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Time In} may be adjusted with the wheel.
\end{itemize}

The White field under the wheel may be used to write the Scene's name.

👉 Differently from the Qlist and Chase Settings, that can be stored only on the controller, the Scene Settings are directly stored to the Scene, like the Cue Settings, and kept in case of further assignments.

\hypertarget{compound-controllers}{%
\subsubsection{Compound Controllers}\label{compound-controllers}}

Controllers may be extended in width and height to make larger compound controllers.

Compound controllers are used to expand the playback possibilities by getting more faders and buttons operating on the same object, so there can be more actions assigned.

There is no limit as to how wide a controller may be, but for practical purposes, a limit of 6 controllers per content is suggested.

For example a single Qlist can be assigned to two Sliders and two Qkeys and set the control of the controllers in the following way:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  1st Slider: Intensity, Go, Back, Flash
\item
  2nd Slider: Crossfade, Step Forward, Step Back, Blackout
\item
  1st Qkey: FX Rate, On
\item
  2nd Qkey: FX Size, Off
\end{itemize}

Other useful examples of compound controllers combinations are:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  creating additional Crossfader pairs,
\item
  adding Rate control to Chasers,
\item
  setting Group Selection, Deselection and Release to Submasters,
\item
  and many more\ldots{}
\end{itemize}

If Playback slider controllers and Qkey buttons are linked to the same object, it could be comfortable to synchronize the paging of Sliders and Qkeys.

This is done by pressing {[}VIBE{]} button together with the Up/Down paging buttons of either Playbacks or Qkeys.

\hypertarget{playback-master}{%
\subsubsection{Playback Master}\label{playback-master}}

A Playback Master is a controller that masters other controllers.

The LED and background color for playback master is blue.

Any key command on the master will be triggered to the controlled Playbacks (GO, BACK, etc.).

Any fader behavior and movement on the master will be triggered to the controlled Playbacks (Intensity, Rate, etc.).

Playback Masters are stored in snaps.

\textbf{Set Up a PB Master}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Press {[}PLAYBACK{]}
\item
  Tap {[}MASTER{]} on the toolbar
\item
  Press any of keys of the playbacks to be mastered
\item
  Press {[}STORE{]}
\item
  Press any key of the controller that becomes the Playback Master.
\end{enumerate}

{ Not to be confused with the Master Controller, that can be only one at a time, the Playback Masters can be as many as needed.}

\hypertarget{Live-Controller-11.5}{%
\subsection{Live Controller}\label{Live-Controller-11.5}}

The Live Controller View is used to check and follow the playback of the Qlist, Chase or Scene loaded on the target controller.

As many Live Controller Views as needed may be added to the layout, and each of them can be focused on a specific controller.

The \textbf{Header} of the view reports the controller that is currently shown and the name of the object loaded on that controller.

The \textbf{List button} enables/disables the automatic scrolling of the Cues.

The \textbf{Arrow button} shows or hides the empty columns (fields with no data).

The \textbf{Chain button} opens the Controller Selection popup.

The target controller that needs to be shown on the view can be selected from the list on the popup, divided per Controller Type, Page Number and Controller Number.

The \textbf{Master} key, when enabled, makes the Live Controller View follow the Master Controller.

The \textbf{Tabs} on the bottom of the Live Controller view switch between:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Cue Sheet} - shows all the Cue's numbers, names, times and notes.
\end{itemize}

👉 Cue Sheet may be used to directly access the Cue's Timing popup by tapping on the desired time cell, either to rename the Cues by tammping on the Cue Name cell and digit the text on the keyboard.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Playback} - shows all the Controller's settings, actions and state.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Cue Exam} - shows a table of the values contained in the current Cue, listed by Fixture.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Preview} - shows a table of the values contained in the next Cue, listed by Fixture.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Teach-Rate-11.6}{%
\subsection{Teach Rate}\label{Teach-Rate-11.6}}

Rate and Teach are the features designed to directly control the fading and looping speed of chasers and effects, and are very useful for live lighting playback.

\textbf{Rate} operates as a multiplier to linearly adjust the stored timing.

Rate can be assigned as a fader Action to any controller, splitted between Crossfade Rate (used with Chasers) and FX Rate (used with Effects).

Moreover the Rate button on the Controller Mode Keys sets all the sliders to control the rate.

\textbf{Teach} operates as a ``tap beat'' button to adjust the rate by giving timed inputs, according to the sound rhythm.

Teach can be assigned as a button Action to any controller and it affects both Chasers and Effects.

Additionally the Vibe panel offers two buttons dedicated to the Rate and Teach, used to open the \textbf{Rate Picker} on the Smart Screen and manage many playback rates at a time.

This screen view is very helpful in fine tuning chases and effects to synchronize with music or to improvise performances ``on the fly''.

The Rate Screen operates outside of the editor and is non destructive. Changes made in the Rate Screen are not stored in cues but may be stored and recalled in Snaps.

Press the {[}RATE{]} button to open the Rate Picker on the Smart Screen.

\textbf{Smart Screen - Rate Picker}

\textbf{Rate Screen}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Press {[}RATE{]}
\item
  All loaded controllers will appear in the ``Selected Controllers'' section of the smart screen. All loaded controllers will be shown and all controllers with Chasers or Effects running will be selected.
\item
  Pressing any physical or virtual {[}Key{]} of a controller, or tapping on the controller's box in the Rate screen, will deselect all other controllers.
\item
  Additional controllers may be toggled on or off as desired, they will appear in the Selected Controllers display.
\item
  \{Select All\} will select all loaded controllers on the rate control.
\item
  \{Deselect All\} will deselect all selected controllers from the rate control
\item
  \{Show Active\} will remove all controllers in the ``Selected Controllers'' section that are not currently under the control of the rate wheels.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Encoder wheels}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Wheel 1 - \{Controller Rate\} speeds up or slows down the cross fade rate of the controller. This may be used for cue transitions, chases, or effects.
  Range: 0\% (Stopped) ⟶ 3000\% (effectively cut time)
\item
  Wheel 2 - \{X Fade \%\} adjusts the ``fade vs jump'' percent of a chaser.
  Range: 0\% (full jump) ⟶ 100\% (full cross fade)
\item
  Wheel 3 - \{Intensity Limit\} proportionally limits the intensity output of a controller.
  Range 0\% (no intensity output) ⟶ 100\% \{full intensity\}
\item
  Wheel 4 - \{Library Editor Rate\} adjusts the speed that Libraries are faded into the editor. {Not implemented yet.}
\end{enumerate}

Four virtual sliders on the Rate Picker allow the same control of the encoder wheels.

\textbf{Extra wheels}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Wheel 1 - \{FX Rate\} adjusts the rate of effects that are running on the selected controllers.
\item
  Wheel 2 - \{FX Size\} adjusts the size of effects that are running on the selected controllers.
\item
  Wheel 3 - \{FX Offset\} adjusts the offset (Spread) of effects that are running on the selected controllers.
\item
  Wheel 3 - \{FX Base\} adjusts the base values (start point) of parameters that are in running effects on the selected controllers.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Reset keys}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \{Reset FX Rate\} - Resets controlled FX rates back to their stored values.
\item
  \{Reset FX Size\} - Resets controlled size of effects back to their stored values.
\item
  \{Reset FX Offset\} - Resets controlled offset of effects back to their stored values.
\item
  \{Reset FX Base\} - Resets controlled effects base values back to their stored values.
\item
  \{Reset Ctrl Rate\} - Resets all controller rates back to their default of 100\%.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Freeze keys}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \{Freeze All\} - Pauses all running chases and effects until the \{Freeze All\} key is toggled again.
\item
  \{Freeze Chase\} - Pauses all running chases until the \{Freeze Chase\} key is toggled again.
\item
  \{Freeze Effect\} - Pauses all running effects until the \{Freeze Effect\} key is toggled again.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{quote}
Freeze only affects chases and effects at the playback level, they are not released from the cue.
\end{quote}

To indentify the Freezed Controllers, an indicator will appear on the controller's Status Box in place of the Rate value:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  FA for Freezed All
\item
  FC for Freezed Chase
\item
  FE for Freezed Effect
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Transport Bar}

When actively in the Rate Screen, physical controller keys are mapped to the rate interface and may not be used to activate Qlists, cues, or scenes. As an alternative, the transport bar may be used. To use the transport bar:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Tap \{Enable Controller PB\} to activate the Transport Bar. (The controller keys are now active with the assigned functions).
\item
  Select the controllers to control with the bar by toggling any of the controller's physical control keys
\item
  Use the transport Bar buttons to:
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{\textless\textless\} Step Back
\item
  \{\textbar\textless\} Back
\item
  \{\textbar\textbar\} Hold (Pause)
\item
  \{\textgreater\} Go
\item
  \{\textgreater\textgreater\} Step Forward
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{3}
\tightlist
\item
  \{Release\} may be tapped at any time to deactivate the selected controllers using release time.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Teach Rate}

Teach may be used with the button on the Rate screen or with the {[}TEACH{]} button on the panel.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Set the number of taps to be averaged:
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{TAPx2\} for 2 taps - {[}TEACH{]} button's LED becomes Red
\item
  \{TAPx4\} for 4 taps - {[}TEACH{]} button's LED becomes Green
\item
  \{TAPx8\} for 8 taps - {[}TEACH{]} button's LED becomes Blue
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{1}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap \{Teach Rate\} or press {[}TEACH{]} the number of times defined.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{Teach Rate\} will show the number of taps received.
\item
  {[}TEACH{]} will blink until the total number of taps is reached.
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{2}
\tightlist
\item
  The selected controllers' rate will change accordingly to the average time between the pressures of Teach.
\end{enumerate}

{[}SHIFT{]} + {[}TEACH{]} allows to switch between 2, 4 and 8 taps with no need to open the Rate Screen.

\hypertarget{snap}{%
\section{Snap}\label{snap}}

👉 Take a \textbf{Snap} of the Vibe controllers' live state, trigger it manually or with a cue to restore the playback configuration.

This chapter deals with storing and triggering Snapshots of the current playback state.

\textbf{Chapter index:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Snaps-12.1}{12.1 Snaps}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Trigger-Snaps-12.2}{12.2 Trigger Snaps}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Attach-Snaps-12.3}{12.3 Attach Snaps}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Snap-Time-12.4}{12.4 Snap Time}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Store-Snaps-12.1}{%
\subsection{Snaps}\label{Store-Snaps-12.1}}

Snaps are Snapshots of the Playback configuration, they capture and record the current state of all Controllers including:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Current Page
\item
  Current Master Controller
\item
  Loaded Qlist
\item
  Active Cue
\item
  Slider position
\item
  Priority list (stack) at the time of the snapshot
\item
  Controller priority number
\item
  Controller slider and button assignments
\item
  Linked controllers
\item
  Rate overrides
\item
  Effect Size overrides
\item
  Effect Offset overrides
\item
  Effect Base overrides
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Store a Snapshot:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Make sure that all controllers on all pages are setup, loaded, and activated exactly as you want them to be restored.
\item
  Press {[}STORE{]} {[}HERE{]} to any available \{Snap\} softkey.
\end{enumerate}

OR Press {[}SNAP{]} {[}\#{]} {[}STORE{]}.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{2}
\tightlist
\item
  Press {[}TEXT{]} to open the Text popup and label the Snap - this is only valid directly after storing the Snap, otherwise {[}SNAP{]} {[}\#{]} {[}TEXT{]} may be used at any time.
\end{enumerate}

Snaps may be viewed on the Exam view:

\hypertarget{Trigger-Snaps-12.2}{%
\subsection{Trigger Snaps}\label{Trigger-Snaps-12.2}}

Snaps may be directly triggered using the keypad or Snap Softkeys.

\textbf{Trigger a Snap using the keypad:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}SNAP{]} {[}\#{]} {[}ENTER{]}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Trigger a Snap using Snap softkeys:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap the required \{SNAP\} softkey.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Trigger a Snap from a Qkey:}

{Not implemented yet.}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  {Assign the Snap to the Qkey by pressing {[}SNAP{]} {[}\#{]} {[}HERE{]} to any Qkey button.}
\item
  {Press the Qkey button to trigger.}
\end{itemize}

Snaps on Vibe are absolute: all controllers are replaced with new loaded objects and controllers without new loaded objects are cleared.

{Future releases will have an option for ``Page Holdover'' where active objects will not be replaced until the controller is released.}

\textbf{Additional Snap Settings:}

These additional options can be enabled or disabled in the System Settings on Defaults tab.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Load Pages - Restore the controllers to the page they were on when the Snap was stored.
\item
  Load Master PB - Restore the assignment of the Master Controller to the controller it was assigned to when the Snap was stored.
\item
  Snap Time - Snaps will be loaded in the Time stored, or in the default Snap Time if there is not a specific time stored.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Controller Settings Properties that affect Snaps:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Exclude from Snap - Controller will not be included when Snaps are stored.
\item
  Exclude from Override - Controller will ignore the Snap activation and will not be overridden.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Attach-Snaps-12.3}{%
\subsection{Attach Snaps}\label{Attach-Snaps-12.3}}

Snaps may be attached and triggered from Cues.
Snaps are triggered when:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  The Qlist is advanced to the cue with the Snap trigger.
\item
  {[}GOTO{]} commands are used.
\item
  {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}GO{]} is used.
\end{itemize}

{Back commands will not trigger Snaps.}

\textbf{Attach a Snap to a Cue:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}SNAP{]} {[}\#{]} {[}STORE{]}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Release a specific Snap from a Cue:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}SNAP{]} {[}\#{]} {[}RELEASE{]}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Release all Macros from a Cue:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}SNAP{]} {[}RELEASE{]}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Disable triggers:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap the controller display header for the Qlist that contains Macro or Snap triggers
\end{enumerate}

OR {[}SETTINGS{]} {[}HERE{]} to any controller button and select the Qlist Properties tab.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The Qlist Properties popup will appear.
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{1}
\tightlist
\item
  Deselect the \{Trigger\} button.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Triggering will be disabled for the Qlist assigned to that specific controller.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Snap-Time-12.4}{%
\subsection{Snap Time}\label{Snap-Time-12.4}}

Snaps may be assigned a time to fade in their transitions.

When a Snap is triggered the current playback status will fade to the status stored into the Snap in the assigned Snap time.

\textbf{Set a Snap Time:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Press {[}SNAP{]} {[}\#{]} {[}SETTINGS{]} - The Snap Settings popup will appear.
\item
  Toggle the \{Snap Time\} key On.
\item
  Adjust the Snap Time with the Virtual Time Wheel.
\item
  Close the popup by using {[}ENTER{]} or tapping Apply.
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{macro}{%
\section{Macro}\label{macro}}

👉 Teach a \textbf{Macro} to include any sequence of Vibe commands, trigger it manually or with a cue to expand the program features.

This chapter deals with creating and triggering custom command sequences called Macros.

\textbf{Chapter index:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Teach-Macros-13.1}{13.1 Teach Macros}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Trigger-Macros-13.2}{13.2 Trigger Macros}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Attach-Macros-13.3}{13.3 Attach Macros}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Macro-Time-13.4}{13.4 Macro Time}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Teach-Macros-13.1}{%
\subsection{Teach Macros}\label{Teach-Macros-13.1}}

Macros can be created using the \textbf{Teach Macro} recorder.
In future releases this will expand to include manually editable macros, where the macro time is automatically recorded with macros and the user may decide whether to enable time or ignore it.

\textbf{Store a Macro:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Press {[}TEACH{]} - Vibe will enter Teach Macro record mode and the {[}TEACH{]} key will flash red.
\item
  Press editor keys or controller buttons as required.
\item
  Press {[}TEACH{]} again to terminate the recording - the {[}TEACH{]} key will stop flashing and the key presses are now stored in the Macro clipboard.
\item
  Press {[}STORE{]} {[}HERE{]} to a Macro Softkey.
\end{enumerate}

OR Press {[}MACRO{]} {[}\#{]} {[}STORE{]} - The Macro is now stored.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{4}
\tightlist
\item
  Press {[}TEXT{]} to open the Text popup and label the Macro - this is only valid directly after storing the Macro, otherwise {[}MACRO{]} {[}\#{]} {[}TEXT{]} may be used at any time.
\end{enumerate}

Macros may be viewed on the Exam view:

\hypertarget{Trigger-Macros-13.2}{%
\subsection{Trigger Macros}\label{Trigger-Macros-13.2}}

Macros may be directly triggered using the keypad or the Macro Softkeys.

\textbf{Trigger a Macro using the keypad:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}MACRO{]} {[}\#{]} {[}ENTER{]}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Trigger a Macro using Macro Softkeys:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap the required \{Macro\} Softkey.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Trigger a Macro from a Qkey:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Assign the Macro to the Qkey by pressing {[}MACRO{]} {[}\#{]} {[}HERE{]} to any Qkey button.
\item
  Press the Qkey button to trigger.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Attach-Macros-13.3}{%
\subsection{Attach Macros}\label{Attach-Macros-13.3}}

Macros may be attached and triggered from Cues.
Macros are triggered when:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  The Qlist is advanced to the cue with the trigger.
\item
  {[}GOTO{]} commands are used.
\item
  {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}GO{]} is used.
\end{itemize}

{Back commands will not trigger Macros.}

\textbf{Attach a Macro to a Cue:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}MACRO{]} {[}\#{]} {[}STORE{]}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Release a Macro from a Cue:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}MACRO{]} {[}\#{]} {[}RELEASE{]}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Release all Macros from a Cue:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}MACRO{]} {[}RELEASE{]}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Disable triggers:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap the controller display header for the Qlist that contains Macro or Snap triggers
\end{enumerate}

OR {[}SETTINGS{]} {[}HERE{]} to any controller button and select the Qlist Properties tab.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The Qlist Properties popup will appear.
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{1}
\tightlist
\item
  Deselect the \{Trigger\} button.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Triggering will be disabled for the Qlist assigned to that specific controller.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Macro-Time-13.4}{%
\subsection{Macro Time}\label{Macro-Time-13.4}}

\textbf{Set a Macro Time:}

{Not implemented yet.}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  {Use steps 1 ⟶ 3 to store the macro to the Macro clipboard.}
\item
  {Press {[}MACRO{]} {[}\#{]} {[}TIME{]} {[}STORE{]} - The Macro is now stored including it's timing.}
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{timeline}{%
\section{Timeline}\label{timeline}}

👉 Record a Vibe \textbf{Timeline} to automatize the show playback, based on an audio track or an external timecode signal.
