This chapter deals with playback automation by creating Timelines and receiving external Time Code protocols and MIDI signals to trigger the controllers.

\textbf{Chapter index:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Timelines-14.1}{14.1 Timelines}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Time-Code-14.2}{14.2 Time Code}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{MIDI-14.3}{14.3 MIDI}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Timelines-14.1}{%
\subsection{Timelines}\label{Timelines-14.1}}

Vibe uses a philosophy of Timeline Tracks.

There is no limit to the number of Timeline Tracks that may be recorded in a show.

Timelines can be added to a Timeline View and played simultaneously following an internal or external Time Code.

\textbf{Vibe can read the following Time Code sources}:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Virtual Local - Internally generated Time Code.
\item
  Physical Local - SMPTE or MIDI Time Code received at the SMPTE audio input or via the DIN 5 pin MIDI connector on the back of the console.
\item
  USB MIDI class compliant Time Code.
\item
  MIDI over Ethernet via Compulite VC protocol.
\item
  Input sources from remote Multi-User consoles and devices
\end{itemize}

Vibe can record data from multiple controllers simultaneously.

Additional timeline subtracks will be created on a per controller basis.

Vibe also supports fader automation on timeline tracks.

Vibe automatically matches frame rates to the incoming source.

Supported frame rates are:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  24 FPS
\item
  25 FPS
\item
  29.97 FPS drop-frame
\item
  30 FPS
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{view}{%
\subsubsection{View}\label{view}}

If no layout contains a Timeline view, the first step to create a Timeline is to add a Timeline View to the layout.

Unlimited Timeline views may be created and each Timeline View may have its own \textbf{Time Code}.

As long as more Timeline views are set on different clock sources, they can load different Timelines and moved independently.

If Timeline views are based on the same clock source, they will be synchronized and will load the same tracks and options.

The Timeline view shows all the active Timelines and allows to enable and disable them, or play in ``solo'' mode.

Use the Timeline view's menu to load the Timelines on the view, as well as to create, edit or delete Timelines.

\hypertarget{record}{%
\subsubsection{Record}\label{record}}

After a Timeline View has been created, timeline tracks may be recorded.
Timeline events may be recorded in ``real time'' and then edited afterwards.

\textbf{Create a timeline track:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Open the Timeline View menu.
\item
  Tap + and the ``Create New Timeline'' popup will open.
\item
  Select a timeline number and name.
\item
  Choose Start from \{Blank\} or \{Existing\} Timeline and Apply.
\item
  Tap the \{Select Clock\} drop down in the top left corner and select the timecode source.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Virtual Local - Use internal timecode clock.
\item
  Physical Local - Use the SMPTE or MTC timecode inputs on the console.
\item
  Additional options will populate the list depending on the interfaced timecode device.
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{5}
\tightlist
\item
  Enable the red \{Record\} button or the \{+ Record\} button.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{Record\} overwrites the existing events.
\item
  \{+ Record\} adds new events to the existing ones.
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{6}
\item
  If using internal TC, press Play.
\item
  If using external TC start the timecode source.
\item
  The timeline will start to move and all controller presses and motorized fader moves will now be recorded on sub-tracks, one for each controller's events.
\item
  When all recording is done, either stop the external timecode source or press the Stop button if the clock source is internal.
\item
  👉 {Disable the Record button to prevent accidental overwrite of events.}
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Trigger a Timeline}

Timelines can be triggered and zoomed via the Timeline view's transport bar.

Timeline commands can be also triggered via the Editor Toolbar in Program and Playback pages.

\textbf{Grid View Filters}

Switch from the Graphical to the Grid view and viceversa with the toggle button on the control bar.

In the grid view it is possible to see all events from all timelines or to filter events by timeline and see only its own events.

\hypertarget{commands}{%
\subsubsection{Commands}\label{commands}}

These commands will affect the selected timelines in the sequence. Some sequences will affect the selected timelines regardless of their current view and some will affect them on the ``Active Timeline View''.

\textbf{Commands for the Timeline}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Go to idle state
\item
  Press Timeline \# (+\#, ⟶\#, etc.)
\item
  The Editor Toolbar shows the available commands:
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{3}
\item
  Sequence may also end with:

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Store -- will create the timelines if they are not already created
  \item
    Delete -- will delete the timelines if they exist
  \item
    Text -- will open text box to give a name to the selected timelines
  \item
    Copy -- will add the timelines to the clipboard
  \item
    Paste -- will paste the copied timelines from the clipboard
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  If the source is external MIDI or SMPTE timecode, press {[}Play{]} on the timecode source. The events will synchronize to the source timecode.
\item
  If the source is internal, tap \{Play\} on the timecode view's control bar. The internal timecode will start and events will execute.
\item
  The control bar may be used to stop and rewind events when the source is internal.
\item
  Record may be enabled and disabled ``on the fly'' to overwrite sections of a timeline track.
\item
  forwards and backward using the Set Delta pop-up in the Actions drop down.
\end{itemize}

To trigger Timeline commands via the panel and Editor Toolbar there is an ``Active Timeline View'' indicator:

If there are more than 1 Timeline Views on the layout, only 1 of them can be the ``Active'' one.

Tapping the white header of the view will select the current view as the ``Active'' and will remove the Active sign from the other views.

{Each Timeline View has an active clock. All Timeline Views that have the same clock will be automatically synced.}

\textbf{Commands for the Active Timeline}

These commands will affect the Active Timeline View.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Go to idle state
\item
  Press \emph{Timeline} from the panel
\item
  You see on the Editor Toolbar the available commands:
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{gestures}{%
\subsubsection{Gestures}\label{gestures}}

Use the following \textbf{gestures} on the timeline view to manage the events:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Tap on a single event -- toggles the event selection
\item
  Tap on a playback box -- toggles the playback selection
\item
  Tap on a marker -- toggles the marker selection
\item
  Rectangle draw using touch and drag -- toggles all the events inside the rectangle
\item
  Double-tap an empty area -- clear the selection
\item
  Double tap on the time bar -- sets the current time to the selected time
\item
  Long click an event and drag -- moves the position of all the selected events
\item
  Double finger vertical scroll on the view -- will scroll the Timelines
\item
  Double finger horizontal scroll on the time bar -- will scroll the time bar
\end{itemize}

{Selection can have only one type of objects at a time. Selecting a timeline object (i.e.~Playback, Event, Marker\ldots) will clear all other objects selections.}

\hypertarget{markers}{%
\subsubsection{Markers}\label{markers}}

To aid in timeline navigation, Markers may be inserted.

To insert a marker:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Press the + at the desired location when the timeline is running. A marker will be inserted.
\item
  Press ⟵ to move backwards through the markers.
\item
  Press ⟶ to move forwards through the markers.
\item
  Press 🗑️ to delete a marker
\end{itemize}

In the Timeline view, markers can be moved by assigning a direct time, and they can be assigned a delta.

The Timeline Grid View shows the list of Markers on a dedicated tab, where they can be named.
Markers' names will appear also in the graphical view, on the bottom of the marker.

\hypertarget{move-selection}{%
\subsubsection{Move Selection}\label{move-selection}}

The Move Selection submenu offers options to set move the selected events.

It is possible to change selected event times via the physical wheels.

There are 2 new buttons to assign the delta time for the selected events to the physical wheel and then move it.

There are 2 physical wheels that are available for use:

The left one is for moving the selected events.

The right one is to change the time resolution for the current time movement.

\hypertarget{actions}{%
\subsubsection{Actions}\label{actions}}

The Actions submenu offers options to manage the selected events.

All the events can be moved on the timeline with drag\&drop commands in the graphical view, or by assigning a different time in the grid bar.

The Actions menu allows to enable or disable the selected events, to delete them from the timeline, or to hide the disabled events from the view.

In the Actions menu there is the option to \textbf{Reset Slider Value} from a playback and set it to a fixed value between zero and full.

To clear the slider value from a timeline:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Open a timelines
\item
  Select the target playback
\item
  On the menu, open the Actions
\item
  Press Reset Slider
\item
  Assign the slider value on the virtual slider popup
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{timestamp}{%
\subsubsection{TimeStamp}\label{timestamp}}

It is possible to view the timeline and events data in different time formats.

The default time format is milliseconds, but it is also possible to use FPS format.

The time format selection can be set on the Timestamp View sub-menu.

\hypertarget{audio-track}{%
\subsubsection{Audio Track}\label{audio-track}}

Timelines can load Audio Tracks in common file formats such as .mp3 and .wav.

To add an audio track to a timeline:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  open the Audio section on the menu
\item
  tap ADD
\item
  select the target timeline on the selection popup and tap Apply
\item
  select the audio file on the load popup and tap Apply
\end{itemize}

The Audio Track will be loaded on the top line of the Timeline, showing the file name and the audio spectrum.

\hypertarget{Time-Code-14.2}{%
\subsection{Time Code}\label{Time-Code-14.2}}

Time Code is used to synchronize Vibe events with the internal timeline or external SMPTE Linear Time Code (LTC) or MIDI Time Code (MTC).

\hypertarget{connections}{%
\subsubsection{Connections}\label{connections}}

Vibe has Time Code connectors for the following:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Audio Combi Jack connector for SMPTE input with:

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Female unbalanced line level input on 1/4'' phone jack
  \item
    Female 3 pin XLR balanced line level input.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Female 5 Pin DIN connector for MIDI input.
\item
  Female 5 Pin DIN connector for MIDI output.
\item
  USB 2 and USB 3 ports for USB class compliant MIDI over USB devices.
\item
  RJ45 ports for MIDI over Ethernet (Compulite devices)
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{ltc}{%
\subsubsection{LTC}\label{ltc}}

To trigger a timeline with an \textbf{LTC} or \textbf{Linear Time Code} source, the SMPTE input protocol needs to be enabled.

Open the I/O Settings popup from the Vibe menu or with the \{I/O Settings\} button.

The SMPTE Tab contains the toggle button to enable or disable the LTC input.

{Currently Vibe is only capable of receiving LTC inputs, but will be able to send SMPTE output in future releases.}

\textbf{Recommended quality and level for SMPTE:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  SMPTE is very sensitive to distortion and also requires adequate gain. A clean audio signal with a gain ranging between 0db to +5db is recommended.
\item
  As the console SMPTE input is high impedance, if long runs are to be used it is recommended to send a 150 - 600 ohm balancing line level feed to a transformer close to the console and convert to high impedance.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{mtc}{%
\subsubsection{MTC}\label{mtc}}

To trigger a timeline with an \textbf{MTC} or \textbf{Midi Time Code} source, the MTC input protocol needs to be enabled.

Open the I/O Settings popup from the Vibe menu or with the \{I/O Settings\} button.

The MIDI Tab contains the toggle button to enable or disable the MTC input.

{Currently Vibe is only capable of receiving MTC inputs, but will be able to send MTC output in future releases.}

\textbf{MIDI Time Code} is similar to the SMPTE Time Code and is used to synchronize audio, video, and lighting equipment, but unlike the SMPTE it does not use an audio signal.

MTC is available in the following frame rates:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  24 frame/s (standard rate for film work)
\item
  25 frame/s (standard rate for PAL video)
\item
  29.97 frame/s (drop-frame timecode for NTSC video)
\item
  30 frame/s (non-drop timecode for NTSC video)
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{MIDI-14.3}{%
\subsection{MIDI}\label{MIDI-14.3}}

MIDI (Musical Instrument Digital Interface) is a protocol that was originally developed by the music industry to allow multiple MIDI sound devices to be triggered from just one source, usually a master keyboard or synthesizer.

MIDI is a control protocol and does not transmit audio.

Over the years the MIDI protocol has evolved to become a common way to synchronize sound, video and lighting equipment.

MIDI is based on a 7-bit architecture and therefore has a theoretical limit of 127 possible notes for a keyboard. (Most pianos use only 88 notes and synthesizers even less)

\textbf{MIDI implementation:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  16 independent MIDI channels - The channels systems listen to.
\item
  127 Note ONs - Pressing down on a keyboard.
\item
  127 Note OFFs - Lifting off a keyboard.
\item
  Note On Velocity of 1 ⟶ 127 - How hard the key is struck.
\item
  After Touch pressure of 0 ⟶ 127 - How much pressure is applied after the key reaches the bottom.
\item
  127 controllers each with a range of 0 ⟶ 127 - Encoder wheels or sliders.
\item
  Patch changes from 0 ⟶ 127 - Change from one instrument to another.
\item
  Pitch bend wheel that has a 14-bit resolution.
\item
  A section for MIDI time code and other manufacturer's ``System Exclusive'' information.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{midi-settings}{%
\subsubsection{MIDI Settings}\label{midi-settings}}

To receive and generate Midi signals each protocol needs to be enabled.

Open the I/O Settings popup from the Vibe menu or with the \{I/O Settings\} button.

In MIDI Tab it is possible to enable and disable the Midi Notes, the Midi Time Code and the Midi Show Control devices.

\textbf{Configure MIDI:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Connect all appropriate MIDI devices and power them on.
\item
  Toggle a MIDI Connection source:
\end{enumerate}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Local MIDI} is generated and received from the 5 pin DIN connectors on the back of the console.
\item
  \textbf{MIDI over USB} is transmitted and received over standard USB connectors. The device must be MIDI over USB class compliant to work with Windows without a driver. If the device has a Windows driver available it is recommended to install the driver for optimal results.
\item
  \textbf{MIDI over Ethernet} is supported by Windows class compliant MIDI over Ethernet and Compulite VC MIDI over Ethernet devices.
\item
  \textbf{Refresh MIDI Connection} looks for new connections if devices have been added later.
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{2}
\tightlist
\item
  Press Apply to stay in I/O settings, press {[}ENTER{]} or tap the Apply icon to close the popup.
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{midi-notes}{%
\subsubsection{MIDI Notes}\label{midi-notes}}

In lighting MIDI notes and controllers from external sources are matched to the console's controllers, buttons and wheels to synchronize the playback.

\begin{quote}
A GO Button on console A is configured to send Note \#46, and a GO button on console B is configured to receive note \#46. Pressing GO on console A console will press the GO on console B. In EDM shows DJ's frequently control lighting via MIDI touchpad controllers.
\end{quote}

To configure \textbf{MIDI Notes} open the I/O Settings popup from the Vibe menu or with the \{I/O Settings\} button.

In Midi Tab enable the Midi Notes In / Out and the Active Midi Channels on the columns.

In Notes/Commands Tab it is possible to assign specific commands to the Midi notes.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  The numbers 0 ⟶ 127 on the MIDI grid represent 10 3/4 octaves of notes from C-0 ⟶ G-10.
\item
  Tabs at the bottom filter the type of MIDI message to be received.
\item
  Channel \# may also be selected to filter just messages being received on the selected channel.
\end{itemize}

The MIDI grid is used to select any of the 127 values available for the MIDI message types selected with the tabs.

\textbf{Assign MIDI Notes to Controllers:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  In Midi Tab enable Notes In or Notes Out.
\item
  By default, all 16 available channels will be selected. Deselect as needed.
\item
  In Notes/Commands Tab select the tab for the type of MIDI message to be received.
\end{enumerate}

Possible messages types are:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Note} (Note ON = press down on controller key, Note OFF = release of controller key).
\item
  \textbf{After Touch}
\item
  \textbf{Continuous Controller}
\item
  \textbf{Path Change}
\item
  \textbf{Channel Pressure}
\item
  \textbf{Pitch Bend}
\end{itemize}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{3}
\item
  Tap \{Teach \} - The Teach key will turn purple.
\item
  Tap the desired note box in the MIDI grid - The box will turn purple.
\item
  Press any one of the controller keys - The location and button function of the controller will appear in the box.
\item
  Tap the \{Teach\} key again to toggle it off.
\item
  Tap Apply or press {[}ENTER{]} to close the popup.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Assign MIDI Continuous Controller to Vibe Controller:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Select the channel that the MIDI message will be received on.
\item
  Select the tab for the type of MIDI message to be received. In this case \textbf{Continuous Controller}.
\item
  Tap \{Teach \} - The Teach key will turn purple.
\item
  Tap in the desired continuous controller \# in the MIDI grid - The box will turn purple.
\item
  Move any of the Vibe slider faders - The location and controller type will appear in the box.
\item
  Tap the \{Teach\} key again to toggle it off.
\item
  Tap Apply or press {[}ENTER{]} to close the popup.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Assign Macros to a MIDI note:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Select the channel that the MIDI message will be received on.
\item
  Select the tab for the type of MIDI message to be received.
\item
  Tap \{Teach \} - The Teach key will turn purple.
\item
  Tap the desired note box in the MIDI grid - The box will turn purple.
\item
  Press {[}MACRO{]} - The Macro selector popup will appear.
\item
  Select or type a previously recorded \{Macro \#\} - the Macro \# will appear in the the box.
\item
  Tap the \{Teach\} key again to toggle it off.
\item
  Tap Apply or press {[}ENTER{]} to close the popup.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Additional functions:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Tap \{Clear\} followed by a MIDI grid box to clear its assignment.
\item
  Tap \{Clear All\} to clear all MIDI assignments.
\item
  Tap \{Copy\} followed by the source MIDI assignment and then the destination MIDI assignment to copy from one note to another.
\item
  Tap \{Move\} followed by the source MIDI assignment and then the destination MIDI assignment to move the assignment from one note to another.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{msc}{%
\subsubsection{MSC}\label{msc}}

\textbf{MIDI Show Control} is a significant Real Time System Exclusive extension of the international Musical Instrument Digital Interface (MIDI) standard.

MSC enables all types of entertainment equipment to easily communicate with each other through the process of show control.

The MIDI Show Control protocol is an industry standard ratified by the MIDI Manufacturers Association in 1991 which allows all types of entertainment control devices to talk with each other and with computers to perform show control functions in live and canned entertainment applications.

Just like musical MIDI, MSC does not transmit the actual show media, it simply transmits digital information about a multimedia performance. * Courtesy Wikipedia

Midi Show Control was developed as a simple standard method of triggering basic commands on dissimilar devices.

Basic MSC commands are:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Go
\item
  Stop
\item
  Fire (Generally used for macros)
\end{itemize}

Also supported on receive are:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  All fixtures
\item
  General lights
\item
  Moving lights
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Receive MSC:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Tap \{MTC In\} in the I/O Settings popup, Midi Tab.
\item
  Match the \{Device \#\} to number of the peer that will be sending the control commands.
\item
  If the Peer \# is above 111, select a Group \# that matches and all devices set to that Group \# will receive MSC packets.
\item
  If the Peer \# is 127 (Broadcast Mode) packets will be received on all devices regardless of their Device or Group numbers.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Transmit MSC:}

{Not implemented yet.}

Recommended reading: \url{http://jac.michaeldrolet.net/SCS_10_Help/scs_options_mid_msc.htm}

\hypertarget{topo}{%
\section{Topo}\label{topo}}

👉 Create a Vibe \textbf{Topo}, not only a 2D stage plan but also an impressively intuitive view for touch selections and triggers.

This chapter deals with creating a Topographical Map to select the fixtures and trigger the objects.

\textbf{Chapter index:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Topo-View-15.1}{15.1 Topo View}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Topo-Menu-15.2}{15.2 Topo Menu}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Add-Elements-15.3}{15.3 Add Elements}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Selection-15.4}{15.4 Selection}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Tools-15.5}{15.5 Tools}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Live-Mode-15.6}{15.6 Live Mode}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Topo-View-15.1}{%
\subsection{Topo View}\label{Topo-View-15.1}}

Topo is the short name for ``Topographical Display'', it is a view used to make fast and intuitive the process of fixture selection and the triggering of stored objects.

Practical samples of some Topo applications can be:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  show the light plan to other users (also as a patch-map for technicians),
\item
  make graphical fixture selections (and forget about fixtures' IDs),
\item
  leave a smart interactive control screen to ``not-expert'' users,
\item
  check the fixtures' live status on the stage and easily program offline.
\end{itemize}

When starting a new show any Topo view stored in the layout will be empty until the system is patched and fixtures are assigned to a Topo.

Virtually unlimited Topos may be created and displayed in a Topo view. Fixtures and objects may be shared by different Topos.

Topo view's toolbar collects some visualization options to fastly center the stage and fit it to the screen, as well as an horizontal slider to zoom manually at the desired distance.

The \{Show Address\} / \{Show Name\} button toggles between the visualization of the patched addresses and the fixtures names on the icons.

The Topo view has 2 operating modes, switchable with the toggle button on the bottom right:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{\{LIVE\}}: Topo toolbar and menu get a {Green} frame. Enables fixture selection and object triggering, locks the Topo editing.
\item
  \textbf{\{EDIT\}}: Topo toolbar and menu get a {Red} frame. Enables all the Topo editing options, disables fixture selection and object triggering.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{topo-toolbar}{%
\subsubsection{Topo Toolbar}\label{topo-toolbar}}

The Topo Toolbar is on the bottom of the Topo View and is used for Topo navigation and for the most common Topo Actions.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Horizontal Slider - allows to Zoom In nd Zoom Out the Topo, showing the multiplier
\item
  Center Stage - centers the Topo to the axes origin
\item
  Fit Stage to Screen - adapts the Zoom to visualize all the stage
\item
  Fit Selection to Screen - adapts the Zoom to visualize all the selected object on the Topo
\item
  Fit Stage to Screen - adapts the Zoom to visualize all the existing object on the Topo
\item
  Reset Zoom - sets the Zoom multiplier to x1.0
\end{itemize}

👉 The last Topo Zoom settings used are automatically kept when changing the selected Topo or switching Vibe pages.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Object Settings} - opens the object settings popup to customize the Fixture Icons and to define the wheels that should be visualized for each device's icon.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Show Fixture Address / Name} - switches the visualization on the fixture icons' headers between the Fixture Names and the DMX Addresses.
\end{itemize}

👉 When the Stage Patch information needs to be shared with the Lighting Technicians, apart from exporting the Patch Data with the list of fixtures and addresses through the Show Report function in the Vibe Show Menu, a screenshot of the Topo showing the Fixture IDs and Addresses may be taken, to easily share a map of the lighting rig referencing the addresses to the fixtures' positioning.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{From Editor} - selects the current fixture selection from the Editor and adds it to the Topo.
\end{itemize}

👉 A single press on \{From Editor\} adds the fixture selection directly, while a long press gesture allows to drag\&drop the selection on the Topo to the desired position.

\hypertarget{Topo-Menu-15.2}{%
\subsection{Topo Menu}\label{Topo-Menu-15.2}}

The Topo Menu is a hierarchical menu that provides the tools for creating and editing Topos.

The menu is divided in 3 categories: Topo, Actions, Actions History.

\textbf{TOPO} - Provides the Topo selection, with options to:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  create a new Topo
\item
  edit the existing Topos
\item
  duplicate the selected Topo
\item
  delete the selected Topo
\end{itemize}

\textbf{ACTIONS} - Collects the submenus containing all the operations that may be done on the Topo:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Add Elements - Accesses the available objects
\item
  Selection -- Accesses the selection options
\item
  Tools - Accesses the smart adjustment options
\end{itemize}

\textbf{ACTIONS HISTORY} - Records and writes down all the actions that have been applied to the Topo, featuring the commands to:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  UNDO - rolls back the last operation on the list on each press
\item
  REDO - recovers the undone operation on the list on each press
\end{itemize}

👉 When the Topo view is in \textbf{Edit} Mode all the Topo submenus are enabled. When the Topo view is in \textbf{Live Mode} only the Topo menu and the Selection submenu are enabled, while the Add Elements and Tools submenus are grayed out and disabled.

\hypertarget{topo-actions}{%
\subsubsection{Topo Actions}\label{topo-actions}}

\textbf{To create a new Topo}

Navigate to a Topo view

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap the \{+\} key in the Topo Menu. - The ``Create New Topo'' popup will appear.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{1}
\item
  Select the desired Topo number, and give the Topo a unique name.
\item
  Enter the size of the Stage surface. The stage dimensions may be in be in Imperial or Metric. This default is set in the System Settings popup.
\item
  Select the stage point of view. The default is Top view.
\item
  Tap Apply or press {[}ENTER{]} - This closes the popup, enters Topo \{Edit\} mode, and the grid will be displayed.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{\{Add Elements\}}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{Sets\} Opens the filter of all available sets: Fixture, Channels, Spots, Matrix, Servers, and users created sets.
\item
  \{Groups\} Opens the filter of all available groups.
\item
  \{Libraries\} Opens the filter of all available libraries (Intensity, Position, Color, \ldots).
\item
  \{Macros\} Opens the filter of all available macros.
\item
  \{Snaps\} Opens the filter of all available snaps.
\item
  \{Filters\} Opens the filter of all available filters.
\item
  \{Pixel Mapper\} Adds a User definable matrix to be used for Pixel Mapping.
\item
  \{From Editor\} Allows to Drag\&Drop on the Topo the elements that are selected in Vibe's editor.
\item
  \{Text Label\} Opens a popup to create a customizable text label.
\item
  \{Line\} Adds a customizable line.
\item
  \{Background\} Adds/Removes a customizable and adjustable background image.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{\{Selection\}}

👉 All types of selection must start outside of the fixture icons.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Rectangle: Selection by creating a rectangular shape, dragging from left to right or right to left to surround the target objects.
\item
  Lasso: Selection by creating a free-form path, dragging through the target objects.
\item
  Circle with X: De-selection of all selected icons all. The same operation can be done with a double tap on an empty area of the Topo.
\item
  Delete selected (only in Edit Mode).
\item
  Snap to Grid: Fixtures and object will snap to the nearest grid node.
\item
  Sync to Editor: Fixtures can be selected using the embedded keypad (only in Edit Mode).
\end{itemize}

\textbf{\{Tools\}}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \{Align\}: Aligns the selected elements horizontally to the left, center or right, and vertically to the top, middle or bottom. The alignment can also be set on a circular or a square shape and between two points it is possible to adjust the element location on the shape, the radius and the ending point.
\item
  \{Align to Point\}: A base point must be specified on the Topo surface as a starting reference. The options are the same as Align except there is no point to point.
\item
  \{Spread\} (Fan): Spreads the selected elements horizontally from the left, center or right, and vertically from the top, middle or bottom. When Spread is selected, a virtual modifier wheel will appear.
\item
  \{Transform\} - Modifies size and rotation of the objects to scale and rotate and to rotate of 45 degrees clockwise or counterclockwise. The last icon is to resets the transform options.
\item
  \{Arrange by Calibration\} - When tapped, selected fixture icons get their positions from the XYZ calibration.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Add-Elements-15.3}{%
\subsection{Add Elements}\label{Add-Elements-15.3}}

\hypertarget{fixtures}{%
\subsubsection{Fixtures}\label{fixtures}}

There are two ways to add fixtures to a Topo, from the keypad or from the Topo menu.

\textbf{Using keypad selection}:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Press zoom option \{FIT STAGE TO SCREEN\} on the toolbar at the bottom of the Topo View to see the whole stage area.
\item
  Select the fixtures using the keypad or a group.
\item
  Tap \{FROM EDITOR\} on the toolbar at the bottom of the Topo View.
\item
  Selected fixtures will now appear in the top right section of the Topo.
\item
  The fixtures will be selected (outlined in red), and can be dragged freely into place.
\item
  Double tap in a clear space on the TOPO to deselect.
\end{enumerate}

{If the \{From Editor\} key is selected with a long press (held for a few seconds) the fixtures may be directly dragged from the \{From Editor\} area. Only a maximum of 5 fixture icons will be shown but the whole selection will actually be there. Drag the fixtures to a location on the Topo view and drop. The fixture icons will now appear on the Topo surface. \textbf{The reference point for dropping the fixtures is the top left corner of the dragged fixture icons.}}

\textbf{Using \{ADD ELEMENTS\} from the Topo menu:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Create the Topo
\item
  From the Topo menu tap \{ADD ELEMENTS\}
\item
  From the drop down, tap \{SETS\}
\item
  From the next drop down, select a fixture set - A list of the fixture in the set will drop down.
\item
  From the fixture list, select the fixtures to drag onto the Topo.
\item
  Press, hold and drag the fixtures onto the Topo surface.
\item
  Double tap in a blank area of the Topo to deselect the fixtures.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Add Sub Devices to the Topo View}

Topo view supports the fixture layers, so sub-devices of a fixture (for example 70, such as fixture 70.1 -\textgreater{} 70.7), can be added as independent objects.

\hypertarget{softkeys}{%
\subsubsection{Softkeys}\label{softkeys}}

There is an option to add softkeys of any type to the Topo with dragging and dropping items from the menu.

Using the same methods for adding fixtures to the Topo, drag the objects into the grid.

Softkey objects that can be currently added to the Topo include:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Groups
\item
  Libraries
\item
  Macros
\item
  Snaps
\item
  Filters
\end{itemize}

Softkey object icons can be arranged on the Topo in the same way the fixture icons are.

\hypertarget{pixel-maps}{%
\subsubsection{Pixel Maps}\label{pixel-maps}}

The pixel mapper creates a matrix of fixtures.

The aspect ratio of the pixel map is controlled by the number of columns and rows that are created.
Matrix fixtures may be selected by blocks using the rectangle or the lasso selection tools.

\textbf{Create a new pixel mapper:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  From the Topo Actions menu, tap \{Add Elements\}
\item
  From the \{Add Elements\} drop down, tap \{Pixel Mapper\} - The Create New Pixel Mapper popup will appear.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{2}
\item
  Fill in the number of columns and rows and set the start position and direction.
\item
  Press {[}ENTER{]} or tap Apply to close the popup and create the blank pixel map.
\item
  👉 {Currently, the pixel map will appear in the top right corner and in some cases, a zoom out will be needed to find the pixel map and drag it back to the desired location.}.
\item
  Using the same methods for adding fixtures to the Topo, drag the fixtures into the pixel map matrix.
\item
  Release the fixtures and they will populate the pixel map.
  👉 {The number of fixtures must not exceed the number of pixel mapper cells.}.
\item
  The populated matrix in \{Edit\} Mode will display the fixture numbers in each cell. In \{Live\} Mode, the fixture's color mix will be shown. CMY, RGB, and HSV color spaces are supported.
\end{enumerate}

👉 Multiple pixel mappers may be added to the same Topo along with other objects.

👉 The current Pixel Mapper is for the selection of fixtures and displaying of live output but future versions will add the ability to map content to individual pixel maps.

\hypertarget{labels}{%
\subsubsection{Labels}\label{labels}}

Text labels of any color can be added don the Topo, for example to put notes, descriptions, or important information.

\textbf{Create a new text label:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  From the Topo Actions menu, tap \{Add Elements\}
\item
  From the drop down, tap \{Text Label\} - The Create Label pop-up will appear.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{2}
\item
  Close the pop-up and the test label will appear on the Topo surface.
\item
  Drag the label to the desired location.
\item
  Transpose tools may be used to size and rotate the label.
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{background}{%
\subsubsection{Background}\label{background}}

Backgrounds images, such as studio layouts, renderings and rig elements, may be added to the Topo.

\textbf{Add a new background:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Prepare a USB stick with a JPEG, BMP, or PNG file content.
\item
  Insert the USB stick into one of the Vibe's USB ports.
\item
  Tap \{Add Elements\} on the Topo menu.
\item
  Tap \{Add Background\} - A browser popup will appear.
\item
  Select the USB stick and click on the desired content - Once imported, the content may be found in the default local directory on the hard-drive under D:\Vibe\Compulite\WorkDir\show\Images\Topo\\
\item
  Tap \{Load\}, {[}ENTER{]} or tap Apply to close the popup and place the content as a background to the Topo.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Remove a background:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Tap \{Add Elements\} on the Topo menu.
\item
  Tap \{Remove Background\}.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Edit a background:}

Topo background images can be edited in size and position, through the tools under the Add/Remove Background buttons.

\hypertarget{Selection-15.4}{%
\subsection{Selection}\label{Selection-15.4}}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Fixture Selection may be switched at any time between the \textbf{Square} and the \textbf{Lazo} mode.
\item
  The \textbf{Deselect} key toggles off the current object selection. The same result may be achieved through a double tap on any empty area of the Topo.
\item
  The trash bin is to \textbf{Delete} the currently selected object from the Topo.
\item
  \textbf{Snap to Grid} option forces the objects to be positioned along the Topo Grid, while in Edit Mode. If the option is not enabled the objects are free to be positioned anywhere.
\item
  \textbf{Sync to Editor} option synchronizes the Topo selection with the Editor selection.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Add-Elements-15.5}{%
\subsection{Tools}\label{Add-Elements-15.5}}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Align} sub-menu contains all the align options that may be applied to the icons.

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Selected icons may be aligned to the left, central or right icon
  \item
    Selected icons may be aligned to the top, central or bottom icon
  \item
    Selected icons may be aligned in a Circle shape, in a Square shape, or in a Line between 2 points. Both Circle and Square alignment options may be adjusted through the virtual sliders that appear when they are applied.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Align to Point} sub-menu contains basically the same options of the Align, but the alignment instead of being based on the first selected fixture may be based on any point of the Topo by tapping on it.
\item
  \textbf{Spread} sub-menu contains all the spread options that may be applied to the icons.

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Selected icons may be spread to the sides, starting from the left, central or right icon
  \item
    Selected icons may be spread up and down, starting from the left, central or right icon
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Transform} sub-menu contains all the transform options that may be applied to the icons.

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Selected icons may be sized bigger or smaller
  \item
    Selected icons may be rotated in both directions
  \item
    Selected icons may be rotated in steps of +/-45 degrees or their rotation may be reset to a 0 degrees rotation angle
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Arrange by Calibration} key automatically positions the selected icons on the Topo, following the Fixtures' coordinates and the Stage Size that have been inserted (or imported) in the Calibration Data popup.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Live-Mode-15.6}{%
\subsection{Live Mode}\label{Live-Mode-15.6}}

When the Topos are ready, they become a very useful tool to easily manage the fixture selection and the objects triggering.

Switching the Topo View to Live Mode, the frame will change from Red to Green to show that it is active.

While Topo Views in Edit Mode show the selected icons' frames in Red, Topo Views in Live Mode show the selected icons' frames in yellow.

A few user samples of Topo Views utilities are as follows.

Topos may be used to make a top visualization of the Lighting Rig and show the fixture addresses and patch information based on the fixture positions.

Topos may be used for having a graphical Live view of the stage lighting, where the Global Icons of the fixtures show in real time their intensity level, position, color, prism and gobo wheels' steps.

Topos may be used to create an intuitive ready-to-use interface that allows any not-experiences people to make a fixture selection and trigger Vibe objects such as Libraries, Snaps or Macros.

Topos may be set with a Front view and a Background, showing the stage appearance and positioning the Global Icons over the image, to simplify the lighting rig checking process.

Topos may be set with a Top view and a Background, showing the stage appearance and positioning the Fixture Icons over the image, to simplify the fixture selection process.

Topos may be used to simplify the management of the more complicated fixtures (such as the ones made of many sub-fixtures) and the control of Media Servers, by adding softkeys of the needed Groups and Libraries that may be easily activated.

\hypertarget{exam}{%
\section{Exam}\label{exam}}

👉 \textbf{Exam} the Vibe show file to analise all the stored data, objects and values, navigating a user friendly tree structure.

This chapter deals with viewing and checking the content of stored object and show data.

\textbf{Chapter index:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Exam-View-16.1}{16.1 Exam View}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Show-Exam-16.2}{16.2 Show Exam}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Patch-Exam-16.3}{16.3 Patch Exam}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Exam-View-16.1}{%
\subsection{Exam View}\label{Exam-View-16.1}}

The \textbf{Exam} view contains all the data stored in the show, divided by object type.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  The root menu is divided into two main sub menus, \{Show\} and \{Patch\}.
\item
  The Exam feature allows to check the content of anything that has been created or stored, for example the Fixtures into a Group, the Parameters into a Library, the Controllers into a Snap.
\item
  Exam view can also be opened with the {[}EXAM{]} key. If an object is selected, the view will jump directly to that object's exam view.
\end{itemize}

\begin{quote}
{[}GROUP{]} {[}2{]} {[}EXAM{]} - visualize the data of Group 2.
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
{[}LIBRARY{]} {[}2{]} (Position) {[}1{]} (Library number) {[}EXAM{]} - visualize the data of Position Library 1.
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
{[}FIXTURE{]} {[}3{]} {[}EXAM{]} - visualize the data of Fixture 2.
\end{quote}

\hypertarget{Show-Exam-16.2}{%
\subsection{Show Exam}\label{Show-Exam-16.2}}

\textbf{Qlist} - List of all show Qlists with Cues Count.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  {[}QLIST{]} {[}\#{]} {[}EXAM{]} - Displays a Cue Data sheet and a tab with all the libraries referenced in the Qlist.
\item
  {[}CUE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}EXAM{]} - Displays a track sheet for the selected Cue, Set tabs, and a tab with all the libraries referenced in the Cue.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Library} - List of all stored Libraries types with Count.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  {[}Library Type{]} {[}\#{]} {[}EXAM{]} - Displays all libraries for the selected library type.
\item
  {[}LIBRARY{]} {[}\#{]} {[}EXAM{]} - Displays fixtures and parameters values for the stored library, Set tabs, and a tab with all the Cues with reference to the Library.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Group} - List of all stored Groups with Fixtures Count.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  {[}GROUP{]} {[}\#{]} {[}EXAM{]} - Displays all the fixtures in the group in their selection order.
\item
  {[}FIXTURE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}EXAM{]} - Displays the selected fixture's information organized in tabs by Patch, Sets, Cues, Libraries.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Snap} - List of all stored Snaps.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}SNAP{]} {[}\#{]} {[}EXAM{]} - Graphically displays the Controllers recorded in the Snap and their status and configuration.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Macro} - List of all stored Macros.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}MACRO{]} {[}\#{]} {[}EXAM{]} - Displays all macro steps, time (if enabled), macro state, and macro commands.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Timelines} - List of all stored Timelines.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {Not implemented yet.}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Look Ahead Mask}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Displays which fixtures are enabled for the Look Ahead feature, with Set tabs.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Midi Notes} - List of the patched MIDI Notes.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {Displays patched Midi notes (currently unimplemented, use \{I/0 Settings\} as an alternative.) }
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Filters}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  {[}FILTERS{]} {[}EXAM{]} - Displays all macro steps, time (if enabled), macro state, and macro commands.
\item
  {[}FILTERS{]} {[}\#{]} {[}EXAM{]} - {Not implemented yet.}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Scene} - List of all stored Scenes.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  {[}SCENE{]} {[}EXAM{]} - Displays a list of the Scenes and Submasters.
\item
  {[}SCENE{]} {[}\#{]} {[}EXAM{]} - Displays a track sheet for the selected Scene, Set tabs, and a tab with all the libraries referenced in the Scene.
\item
  {[}SCENE{]} (Submaster) {[}\#{]} {[}EXAM{]} - Displays all the fixtures in the Submaster in their selection order.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Home Scene}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}HOME{]} {[}EXAM{]} - Displays all fixtures and parameters stored in the Home Scene, with Set tabs.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Highlight Scene}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}H.LIGHT{]} {[}EXAM{]} - Displays all fixtures and parameters stored in the Home Scene, with Set tabs.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Lowlight Scene}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}L.LIGHT{]} {[}EXAM{]} - Displays all fixtures and parameters stored in the Home Scene, with Set tabs.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Patch-Exam-16.3}{%
\subsection{Patch Exam}\label{Patch-Exam-16.3}}

\textbf{Devices} - List of all imported Devices and their properties.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Individual Device Exam - Displays graphically the DMX parameter allocation for the current device and mode.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Sets} - List of all Sets with Fixtures Count.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Individual Set Exam - Displays the fixtures in the Set with Fixture ID \#, Set ID \#, Name, Device.
\item
  Individual Fixture Exam - Displays the selected fixture's information organized in tabs by Patch, Sets, Cues, Libraries.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{DMX Out} - List of all DMX universes that have fixtures patched, with Fixtures Count.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Individual Universe Exam - Displays graphically a table of patched fixtures and their DMX properties.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{DMX In} - Displays DMX inputs used on each DMX input universe available.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Individual Universe Exam - Displays a table of DMX input assignments.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Park} - Displays the Count of parked Addresses and Fixtures.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Park Address - Displays a list of all parked addresses.
\item
  Park Fixture - Displays a list of all parked fixtures and parameters.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Curve} List of all the Curves.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Curves Exam - Displays a list of all read only and user created Functions and which of them are included in the favorites list.
\item
  Individual Curve Exam - {Not implemented yet.}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{network}{%
\section{Network}\label{network}}

👉 Configure the Vibe system's \textbf{Network}, manage input and output protocols, setup the connected external devices.

This chapter deals with configuring DMX over Ethernet and other frequently used protocols such as RDM, OSC and CITP.

\textbf{Chapter index:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Console-Network-17.1}{17.1 Console Network}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Compulite-Devices-17.2}{17.2 Compulite Devices}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{DMX-17.3}{17.3 DMX}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{RDM-17.4}{17.4 RDM}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{OSC-17.5}{17.5 OSC}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{CITP-17.6}{17.6 CITP}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{Console-Network-17.1}{%
\subsection{Console Network}\label{Console-Network-17.1}}

\textbf{Vibe has different user accessible Ethernet networks:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Data Network - Accessible via the two EtherCON connectors on the rear panel.
\end{itemize}

◾ Used for Art-Net, sACN, Compulite VC protocol nodes and Master/Slave backup.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  General Networks - Accessible via the two RJ45 connectors on the rear panel.
\end{itemize}

◾ Used for Network storage of show files and remote diagnostics.

Each network device must have its own unique static IP address in the form of four triplets xxx.xxx.xxx.xxx, to identify it on the network. In Vibe, this can be set in the Network Settings popup.

It is not necessary to have three digits in each segment of the IP but there must be at least one digit in each segment. Vibe ships with a default Subnet Mask of 255.0.0.0 which means that it is only important that the first triplets of the IP (the start address) must match on all devices in the network. Devices on the same network must \textbf{not} have identical IP addresses.

\textbf{Common Start IP addresses:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  2.x.x.x - Default for Art-Net networks.
\item
  10.x.x.x - Default for most ETC networks and alternate for Art-Net network.
\item
  192.x.x.x -Default for most MA consoles.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Subnet Mask} - Identifies the network itself and in conjunction with the IP address can be used to properly route network devices.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  255.0.0.0. - Compulite Default. Only the first triplet must match.
\item
  255.255.0.0 - Common in configurations with different systems on the same network. The first and second triplets must match.
\item
  255.255.255.0 - Common in configurations with many different systems on the same network. The first, second and third triplets must match. Not suggested for networks having more Compulite consoles connected.
\end{itemize}

Compulite uses the third triplet as a console identifier in some of the products.

The Vibe can be used in networks using a DHCP server but cannot act as a DHCP server.

\textbf{Switches}:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Vibe has two internal switches, single direct device to device connections are possible without a cross cable.
\item
  When more than one device is connected to the Vibe a good quality gigabit switch should be used.
\end{itemize}

{Many systems have gone down due to bad Ethernet cabling and cheap switches. It is recommended that EtherCON connectors are used where possible in conjunction with professional grade switches designed for lighting and audio applications.}.

\hypertarget{network-settings}{%
\subsubsection{Network Settings}\label{network-settings}}

\textbf{Vibe Consoles} have 4 different networks:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  the Main Network is referred to the Data ports, that are in parallel under the same network board.
\item
  the General Networks are referred to the General\_1 and General\_2 ports, that are independently addressable.
\item
  the Internal Network has no external ports, it is used for internal connection between the Vibe components. The User should avoid editing this network's settings as it may compromise the console functioning.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Vibe PC} has only one network configuration tab:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  the Main Network, referred to the system's network port used as Data output.
\end{itemize}

Open the \{NETWORK SETTINGS\} from Vibe Menu \textgreater{} Settings or tap the Network Settings button - The Network popup will open.

\hypertarget{main-network-1}{%
\subsubsection{Main Network}\label{main-network-1}}

The \textbf{Main Network} Tab shows the configuration of the network used for data transmission.

\textbf{Local:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Option to set the local information of the console such as IP and name.
\item
  Unlock the boxes to edit the fields.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Console State:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  There are 2 possible individual states:
\item
  Stand Alone (Default) -- Console is not in a session but networking for outputs, remotes and external connections are active.
\item
  Local (Offline) -- Same as Stand Alone but disables the DMX over Ethernet.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{User ID:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  {Not implemented yet.}.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Start Session:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The console becomes a Master, meaning it's hosting a session where Backup consoles can be connected.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Online IP Devices:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Displays all Valid IP based devices active on the network.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Delete Selected:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Remove the selected devices from the list.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Clear IP Devices:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Clear the whole IP device list.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Connect As \ldots{} / Disconnect:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Related to starting or ending a session with a master console.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{user-networks-1}{%
\subsubsection{User Networks}\label{user-networks-1}}

On the Vibe consoles, the \textbf{User Networks} Tab shows the configuration of the networks used for additional operations.

General Network IP addresses, both regarding General Network 1 and General Network 2, can be configured in the User Networks tab:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  select the Network Adapter,
\item
  fill the IP Address and DNS fields as desired,
\item
  apply the network settings with the dedicated key.
\end{itemize}

General Networks may be used for specific operations that require special network configurations, that may be very different, or even incompatible, with the Data network settings.

Some examples of General Network purposes are:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  connection to remote external servers for files backup and management,
\item
  connection to web services such as TeamViewer to get remote support,
\item
  communication with third party devices to exchange OSC commands.
\end{itemize}

👉 General Networks are not intended to be used for Data output or to establish multiuser connection between Vibe systems and accessories. Please use the Main Network for these common lighting control operations.

\hypertarget{Compulite-Devices-17.2}{%
\subsection{Compulite Devices}\label{Compulite-Devices-17.2}}

Compulite manufactures some additional devices that can be connected to the Vibe system and configured directly via the Vibe software.

These devices include:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Compulite Vibe Wings
\item
  Compulite ePort Nodes.
\end{itemize}

The \textbf{External Devices View} shows all the ePort nodes and the Wings connected to the network, permitting remote management and configuration of the settings.

Vibe actively scans the network to find other compatible Compulite devices. Once recognized by the Vibe system, all the devices found in the network are automatically listed in the view's tabs.

By selecting a Compulite device, its current options will appear in the External Devices view, according to the amount of ports available (ePorts) or controllers types on the panel (Wings).

The Vibe may be used to configure and update Compulite Device's settings as well as to perform firmware updates.

This view also permits to manage other brands devices that are compliant with the RDM protocol.

\hypertarget{vibe-wings}{%
\subsubsection{Vibe Wings}\label{vibe-wings}}

Any Vibe Wing, as well as most of the Vector Wings, can be connected and configured directly from Vibe, through the External Devices view.

Supported Vibe and Vector Wings that are automatically found by the Vibe while scanning the network are listed in the Wings tab of the External Devices view.

The Wings tab inside the External Devices view allows to rename the wings and to enable or disable parts of the panel, such as the Grand Master, the Editor, the Controllers.

Unavailable parts, such as the Editor on a Playback Wing, will be grayed out.

Vibe Wings have the same controller types of the console, including Sliders, Qkeys and Auxkeys.

The Wings' Controllers may be configured from the External Devices view by assigning the starting number for each controller type, as well as ordering the numbering direction from right to left or from left to right.

👉 When a Wing is used with a VibePC, it may be good to have its controllers starting from 1, but when it is used as an expansion, its controllers may be starting from 11 or from 16 (depending on the console model) to obtain additional physical controllers that are not on the console panel.

The Wing's IP address is not assignable, as it is automatically set by the console once the wing is connected and linked, with a logic that adds +10 to the last number of the console's IP or of the last Wing connected to the system.

External Wings that are compatible with the Vibe software, such as Vibe PC Wing and Vector PC Wing, will keep the settings configured in the External Devices view once they are reconnected to the system, unless they have been connected to other Compulite systems in between.

\textbf{Configure the Wing}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Make sure that the Vibe IP and the Wing IP are in the same range.
\item
  Write a text in the Name field to rename the Wing.
\item
  Write the First Slider, Qkey and Auxkey number in the relative field.
\item
  Set the Controllers' numbering direction.
\item
  Set the panel part to be Enabled or Disabled (if available).
\item
  Tap \{Update Wing\} to apply the changes or \{Reset Changes\} to discard.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Update Firmware}

Before updating the device's Firmware, make sure the update file is the correct one for the Wing's version. Compulite support may be contacted to check that.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Select the Wing from the list.
\item
  Tap \{Update Firmware\}. The file popup will open.
\item
  Select the update file from the USB stick and press Apply.
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{eport-nodes}{%
\subsubsection{ePort Nodes}\label{eport-nodes}}

Compulite manufactures the \textbf{ePort} serie of DMX over Ethernet nodes.

Any ePort can be connected and configured directly from Vibe, through the External Devices view.

Supported Vibe and Vector Wings that are automatically found by the Vibe while scanning the network are listed in the Wings tab of the External Devices view.

\textbf{Configure the ePort}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Make sure that the Vibe IP and the ePort IP are in the same range.
\item
  Write a text in the Name field to rename the ePort.
\item
  Select the DMX-ON-Ethernet protocol in the protocol list.
\item
  Tap \{Update Eport\} to apply all the changes.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Patch Universes to the Ports}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Tap on the Universe cells in the Port's table to enter the edit mode.
\item
  Assign a numeric value and press Enter (or tap outside the cell).
\item
  The Edited cells get a yellow background.
\item
  Tap on the direction cells to switch the port from Output to Input.
\item
  Tap on the RDM cells to switch the RDM protocol On / Off.
\item
  Tap \{Update Eport\} to apply the changes or \{Reset Changes\} to discard.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Update Firmware}

Before updating the device's Firmware, make sure the update file is the correct one for the ePort's version. Compulite support may be contacted to check that.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Select the ePort from the list.
\item
  Tap \{Update ePort Firmware\}. The file popup will open.
\item
  Select the update file from the USB stick and press Apply.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{C-Net Manager software}

Compulite ePort nodes may also be configured through the dedicated C-Net Manager software, downloadable from the Compulite website www.compulite.com

C-net Manager, same as the Vibe software, automatically scans the network and lists down all the ePort nodes that are found connected, allowing the configuration of the communication protocol and ports according to the ePort type.

C-net Manager may also be used to remotely ensure whether the ePort's outputs are active or inactive, by checking the Green or Red status of each Port's LED.

\textbf{Supported Protocols}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  ePort-2

  ◾ Compulite VC

  ◾ sACN

  ◾ Art-Net 3
\item
  ePort 41 (Prior to Sept 2016)

  ◾ Compulite VC

  ◾ Art-Net v2
\item
  ePort-41 (Current)

  ◾ Compulite VC

  ◾ sACN

  ◾ Art-Net 3
\item
  ePort-8

  ◾ Compulite VC

  ◾ sACN

  ◾ Art-Net 3
\end{itemize}

For more product information, see ePort Documents on www.compulite.com

\hypertarget{DMX-17.3}{%
\subsection{DMX}\label{DMX-17.3}}

Vibe supports transmission of 256 universes on \textbf{DMX over Ethernet} using the industry standards of Art-Net versions 1 ⟶ 3, ad sACN (ANSI E1.31-2016 standard). and the Compulite VCs protocol.

For DMX output management, physical or over network protocols, please refer to the \textbf{Patch Chapter}.

\hypertarget{RDM-17.4}{%
\subsection{RDM}\label{RDM-17.4}}

RDM is fully supported on the 8 physical DMX output ports and Compulite VC protocol.

Vibe uses the External Devices view, RDM Devices tab, to scan the network for RDM compatible devices, get their information and perform settings updates on the target devices.

\textbf{View RDM information:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Open the External Devices View.
\item
  Tap the RDM Devices tab.
\item
  Tap \{Get Devices\} on the view's menu - The system will scan and display RDM compliant devices.
\item
  If new devices are added, press \{Re Scan\}.
\end{enumerate}

👉 RDM protocol on a DMX wiring works only when every device on the line is RDM compliant. This includes any fixture, any splitter and any DMX node that may be wired in between the line.

\hypertarget{OSC-17.5}{%
\subsection{OSC}\label{OSC-17.5}}

OSC (Open Sound Control) is a protocol for networking with sound synthesizers, computers, and other multimedia devices for purposes such as musical performance or show control.

OSC enables a text string based exchange of commands and triggers between different devices via a network connection, that is used to establish the synchronization between different systems.

Connections with OSC can use TCP or UDP protocol for direct communication over the network.

\textbf{OSC Input}

Vibe supports the OSC input protocol using TCP, UDP, or both.

In the I/O Settings popup, on the OSC tab, there is the list of supported OSC strings as well as OSC ports and protocols.

Custom OSC strings can also be created by the user.

\textbf{OSC Output}

Vibe supports the OSC output protocol using TCP, UDP, or both.

The OSC view shows the existing commands, allowing the user to create and edit custom commands and to send immediate commands and use keyboard arrows to re-send commands from the cache.

OSC commands can be attached to Cues with the syntax:
{[}QLIST{]}, {[}\#{]}, {[}CUE{]}, {[}\#{]}, {[}ATTACH\_OSC{]} (toolbar).

\hypertarget{CITP-17.6}{%
\subsection{CITP}\label{CITP-17.6}}

The CITP (Controller Interface Transport Protocol) is a network protocol used between lighting control consoles, visualizers and media servers.

The protocol allows to transport information, browse media and thumbnails, stream media among different devices.

\textbf{CITP Settings} popup gathers all the CITP capabilities of the Vibe together.

To open the CITP Popup:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Tap Vibe menu button
\item
  In Settings, tap CITP Settings
\end{enumerate}

or

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap {[}CITP Settings{]} button.
\end{enumerate}

In the popup there are 4 tabs:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  General
\item
  Media Servers
\item
  Visualizers
\item
  Consoles
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{general}{%
\subsubsection{General}\label{general}}

This tab shows the general information about the console and gives the option to enable/disable CITP communication from/to the console.

\hypertarget{media-servers}{%
\subsubsection{Media Servers}\label{media-servers}}

This tab shows a list of the detected Media Servers on the network.

In order to connect to a media server and start a session, select a media server from the list and press \emph{Connect}.

In order to link a media server to a specific fixture, select a connected media server from the list and tap \emph{Configure}. A popup will appear where you can choose which fixtures you want to link.

In order to get all the available data from a media server, select a connected and linked media server from the list, and tap \emph{Get Data}. All available data such as thumbnails, effects etc. will be synced from the media server to the fixtures on your show.

\hypertarget{visualizers}{%
\subsubsection{Visualizers}\label{visualizers}}

This tab shows a list of the detected Visualizers on the network that are using CITP.

To connect to a visualizer and start a session, select it from the list and tap Connect.

Once connected, to get the patch from the visualizer, select it from the list and tap Get Show.

👉 This action will clear the current show data, get the devices, fixtures, numbers, patch, XYZ information from the visualizer and automatically create them on your show.

\hypertarget{consoles}{%
\subsubsection{Consoles}\label{consoles}}

This tab shows a list of the detected Consoles on the network that are using CITP and their state.

\hypertarget{multiuser}{%
\section{MultiUser}\label{multiuser}}

👉 \textbf{Connect} to a Vibe session to program and control the show with more consoles and operators working together in real time.

This chapter deals with connecting more consoles to the same network and configuring the users.

\textbf{Chapter index:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Masterux2fBackup-18.1}{18.1 Master/Backup}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Multi-User-18.2}{18.2 Users}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{masterbackup}{%
\subsection{Master/Backup}\label{masterbackup}}

Master/Backup is the basic way to connect two Vibe systems and make them work simultaneously on the same show file.

In order to establish a Master/Backup connection, the two Vibe consoles must be in the same network and must run the same Vibe software version.

👉 { Any Vibe console can work in a MultiUser Session, while the VibePC needs to install a \textbf{Network Licence} to enable the networking. Not implemented yet.}.

\textbf{Connection Process}

On the Vibe console that has to become the Master, open the Network Settings to start the \textbf{Session}.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Verify the Network Adapter and IP address used, refresh them if changes are applied.
\item
  Tap on the \textbf{Start Session} key. The Console State changes from \textbf{Stand Alone} to \textbf{Master}.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  On the Backup console select the Master console from the \textbf{Online IP Devices} box.
\item
  Tap on the \textbf{Connect as Backup} key.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The Backup console gets a \textbf{Connect Request} popup.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Tap Approve and wait for the Show Sync Progress bar to finish on both consoles.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The Backup console gets a \textbf{Layout Sync Request} popup.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Tap Apply to synch the layout too, or Cancel to keep the layouts separate.
\item
  A popup on both systems informs when the connection is ready.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  In the Status Box, a green line shows that the connection is established, and the consoles' command lines show if the console is the Master or the Backup.
\end{itemize}

When the Master/Backup connection is ready, both the Vibe systems run the same show and use the same outputs, the ones of the Master.

Any operation that is done either on the Master or on the Backup is synchronized in real time on the other system.

👉 Master and Backup consoles share also the Editor, so the programming operations should be done on one system at a time otherwise they will be merged.

\textbf{Disconnection Process}

To manually end the whole network session, on the Master's network settings tap on \textbf{End Session} key.

To manually end the synchronized connection:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Open the Network settings on either the Master or the Backup console.
\item
  Tap on the \textbf{Disconnect} key.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Wait for the Disconnect Progress bar to finish.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The \textbf{Partner Disconnected} advice appears on both consoles.
\end{itemize}

The \textbf{Partner Disconnected} advice appears also in case of connection failure (for example if the cable is disconnected or if one console fails or loses power).

If the connection with the Master fails, the Backup will automatically take control of the outputs, ensuring that the show won't lose any data.

The Command Line informs that the connection with the partner is lost.

If a lost connection is restored, a popup appear on both systems to inform the user that the other system is online again and ask which option to choose.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Stand Alone - the consoles will use the show independently from each other, leaving the session.
\item
  Offline - the console will keep the show but disables the outputs, without leaving the session.
\item
  Master - the console will be the Master, performing a full sync operation to make the other console the Backup.
\item
  Backup - the console will be the Backup, performing a full sync operation to make the other console the Master.
\end{itemize}

{ In a MultiUser Session there may be different type of Clients connected. The Client type affects the system's rights of use and limits the operations that can be done from it. The Client type may be \textbf{Backup} (Primary Client), \textbf{Normal} (Secondary Client), \textbf{Low} (Basic Client). Not implemented yet.}.

\hypertarget{users-1}{%
\subsection{Users}\label{users-1}}

{Not implemented yet.}.

{ Users popup allows to configure and assign user rights to the Vibe systems on the same network that are connected to the Master console's session.}.

{ Each User Client has specific authorizations and limitations for managing the Master console's show patch, program and playback properties.}.

{ The User right may be assigned with Default options or Customized.}.

\hypertarget{offline-tool}{%
\section{Offline Tool}\label{offline-tool}}

👉 Use the \textbf{Offline Tool} software for the Vibe software and hardware maintenance and updates.

This chapter describes the Offline Tool integrated software that is used for system update, restore and maintenance.

\textbf{Chapter index:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Introduction-19.1}{19.1 Introduction}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Maintenance-19.2}{19.2 Maintenance}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Activation-19.3}{19.3 Activation}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Database-19.4}{19.4 Database}
\item
  \protect\hyperlink{Hardware-19.5}{19.5 Hardware}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{introduction-1}{%
\subsection{Introduction}\label{introduction-1}}

The Offline Tool is a Vibe inbuilt software used for the system maintenance.

Offline Tool can be launched either from the Vibe Menu or from the OS programs.

If Vibe software is running, it is not possible to open the Offline Tool at the same time.

The Offline Tool may be opened from the Vibe Menu automatically pausing the Vibe software, otherwise, if it's launched from the OS, a popup advice asks to close the Vibe first.

The main uses of the Offline Tool are:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Software Updates and Patches
\item
  Device Packages Updates
\item
  Licences Validations
\item
  Restore of Databases (Console, Show, Layout)
\item
  Console Panel Test
\item
  External Ports test
\item
  Firmware Updates
\end{itemize}

To close the Offline Tool, tap on the Exit key on the bottom right of the screen. A popup will ask if only the user prefers to just \textbf{Exit} the software or to \textbf{Open Vibe} software and resume programming.

\hypertarget{maintenance}{%
\subsection{Maintenance}\label{maintenance}}

The Offline Tool's \textbf{Maintenance} column collects the functions that keep the Vibe software up to date.

\hypertarget{sw-update}{%
\subsubsection{SW Update}\label{sw-update}}

The bottom left corner writes the last installer software version.

Whenever a new software release comes out, download and install it to keep the Vibe up to date.

The first installation of Vibe PC on a computer requires to use the VibePC.exe file and run some additional commands, from then on it the update process may be done in the same way of the console update process, through the Offline Tool.

On the Vibe consoles this is not necessary as they come already with the software pre-installed.

To update the Vibe software:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Download the latest installer (.vsw) from Compulite website,
\item
  Save the installer file into a USB stick,
\item
  Connect the USB stick to the Vibe console,
\item
  Open Offline Tool and tap \textbf{SW Update} key,
\item
  Launch the update file and wait the process to be finalized,
\item
  Enjoy the latest features!
\end{itemize}

👉 In the Release Notes document that is published for each new software release, uploaded on Compulite website, are collected and explained the version's highlights and bug fixes.

\hypertarget{sw-patch-file}{%
\subsubsection{SW Patch File}\label{sw-patch-file}}

Some minor changes and fixes may not need a full software version to be released.

These changes may be performed by installing smaller patch files that are provided by Compulite Support.

If Compulite software support sends a patch file to fix a user-specific issue, it may be launched through the Offline Tool by tapping the Patch File key.

To install a Vibe patch file:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Download the file sent by Compulite support,
\item
  Save the patch file into a USB stick,
\item
  Connect the USB stick to the Vibe console,
\item
  Open Offline Tool and tap \textbf{SW Patch File} key,
\item
  Launch the patch file and wait the process to be finalized.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{full-device-package}{%
\subsubsection{Full Device Package}\label{full-device-package}}

The lighting fixtures market is always expanding, with many brands continuously releasing new products and fixtures.

Compulite Fixture Support provides the device building for the new fixtures coming on the market and every user request fo any missing device is then added to the Vibe Device Database.

Compulite regularly releases a Full Device Package update.

The Device Package file (.vdp) can be downloaded from Compulite website and installed on the Vibe system (consoles and PCs) to keep the device database up to date.

To update the Vibe Device database:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Download the latest device package (.vdp) from Compulite website,
\item
  Save the file into a USB stick,
\item
  Connect the USB stick to the Vibe console,
\item
  Open Offline Tool and tap \textbf{Full Device Package} key,
\item
  Launch the update file and wait the process to be finalized,
\item
  Enjoy the latest fixtures!
\end{itemize}

👉 The Device Database update doesn't affect the devices that have been already used in a stored show file. When the show is loaded, if the system finds on the Console Device DB new updated devices, it asks the user if updating them in the Show Device DB or not.

👉 The User-created devices won't be deleted or overwritten by a Device Pack update, as they have an ID that is different from the manufacturer's devices.

\hypertarget{create-error-log}{%
\subsubsection{Create Error Log}\label{create-error-log}}

In case of troubles, after contacting Compulite Support the user may be asked to generate an Error Log.

This report helps the software debuggers and developers to analize and fix the issue.

If Compulite Support asks the user to create an Error Log, this may be done with the offline Tool by tapping on the \textbf{Create Error Log} key.

A popup will tell the user the system directory where the generated error file is saved.

\hypertarget{activation}{%
\subsection{Activation}\label{activation}}

The Offline Tool's \textbf{Activation} column contains the console, software and database activation licenses installed on the system.

\hypertarget{validate-console-license}{%
\subsubsection{Validate Console License}\label{validate-console-license}}

This function is used to activate the Vibe system.

\hypertarget{get-sql-key}{%
\subsubsection{Get SQL Key}\label{get-sql-key}}

This function is used to activate the Vibe Database.

It requires an Administrator Password to be performed.

\hypertarget{set-computer-name}{%
\subsubsection{Set Computer Name}\label{set-computer-name}}

This function asks to insert the console's Serial Number to identify the system.

Vibe Serial Number is a 5 digits number that may be found on the back of the console.

\hypertarget{database}{%
\subsection{Database}\label{database}}

The Offline Tool's \textbf{Console}, \textbf{Show} and \textbf{Layout} columns collect the functions that allow to save the current show and layout, load new ones, or initialize the databases.

Most of these operations may be done directly on the Vibe software. The Offline Tool's functions are kind of a backup system that allow to store the show and layout files in case of issues that don't permit to open the Vibe software and perform these operations.

{ Initialize DB functions removes the current show and/or layout and brings the system back to the default starting point: New Show and New Layout. In order not to lose the currently running show and layout, it is highly recommended to save those files before initializing the database. }.

👉 Initialize DB functions doesn't affect the files stored in the system memory, but only the currently active files.

\hypertarget{console-db}{%
\subsubsection{Console DB}\label{console-db}}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Initialize ALL DB} - Set the whole System back to Factory Default (or to the latest software update. This includes initialization of Console, Show, Layout and Device Databases.
\item
  \textbf{Initialize Console DB} - Set the Console installation information, directories and paths back to Factory Default.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{show-db}{%
\subsubsection{Show DB}\label{show-db}}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{New Show} - Load a new show on the Vibe.
\item
  \textbf{Save Show} - Save the current show file in the show folder.
\item
  \textbf{Initialize Show DB} - Set the Show database back to Factory Default.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{layout-db}{%
\subsubsection{Layout DB}\label{layout-db}}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{New Layout} - Load a new empty layout on the Vibe.
\item
