  \textbf{Default Layout} - Load the default layout on the Vibe.
\item
  \textbf{Save Layout} - Save the current layout file in the layout folder.
\item
  \textbf{Initialize Layout DB} - Set the Layout database back to Factory Default.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{hardware-2}{%
\subsection{Hardware}\label{hardware-2}}

The Offline Tool's \textbf{Hardware} column collects the functions that help for the Vibe hardware maintenance, such as testing the physical controllers and buttons on the panel and the signals transmission in the input and output ports.

\hypertarget{upgrade-dmx-count}{%
\subsubsection{Upgrade DMX Count}\label{upgrade-dmx-count}}

{ Vibe Licenses may be used to expand the DMX universes supported by the Vibe consoles. Not implemented yet.}.

\hypertarget{panel-test}{%
\subsubsection{Panel Test}\label{panel-test}}

The Panel test is used to test the console components and its views are according to the console type used.

The Panel Test view is divided in 4 tabs:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Editor Keys
\item
  Fader Keys
\item
  Upper Panel Keys
\item
  More
\end{itemize}

The \textbf{Editor Keys}, \textbf{Fader Keys}, \textbf{Upper Panel Keys} tabs work all in the same way, but they refer to the different components of the panel, showing the virtual equivalents of all the physical buttons and controllers.

\textbf{Editor Keys}

\textbf{Fader Keys}

\textbf{Upper Panel Keys}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Press any physical button, including the pressable encoders, knobs and wheels, and the virtual equivalent on the Panel Test display will light up.
\end{itemize}

👉 Keep either the physical or the virtual button pressed to start a loop of the RGB backlight colors, to check if all the Leds are working properly.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Move any encoder and wheel, and the virtual equivalent on the Panel Test display will show a number according to the rotation speed and a + or - sign indicating the rotation direction.
\item
  Move the trackball, and the virtual equivalent on the Panel Test display will show its positioning.
\item
  Move any slider controller, including the Grand Master and the A/B Xfader, and the virtual equivalent on the Panel Test display will show its current level.
\end{itemize}

The \textbf{More} tab shows some more features to test the panel.

\textbf{More}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Fader Test - Bring all faders at Ful or Zero level, or set custom top and bottom limits, set the fade timing, and start an automatic fader movement test.
\item
  Fader Move Detection - Enable the fader detection and the box will show the last moved fader in real time.
\item
  Desklight - Use the virtual wheel to test the desklight's dimming.
\item
  Panel Backlight - Use the virtual sliders to test the panel's Min and Max backlight levels.
\item
  LED Functions - Set all the panel's LEDs to ON or OFF. This will highlight all the panel's backlights in white.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{midismpte-test}{%
\subsubsection{MIDI/SMPTE Test}\label{midismpte-test}}

Tap on the \textbf{MIDI / SMPTE Test} key to access the function.

If a SMPTE input is received, the boxes on the left side of the screen will show the real timing transmitted by the timecode signal and its frame rate.

If MIDI packets are send or received, the boxes on the right side of the screen will show in real time the messages transmitted with the MIDI protocol.

\hypertarget{firmware-update}{%
\subsubsection{Firmware Update}\label{firmware-update}}

This function is used to update the firmware of the Vibe hardware components.

Tap on Firmware Update to check the Editor and Sliders Panel version and the VDMX version.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Update Editor} installs the new firmware to the panel's Editor boards, including: buttons, encoders, wheels and trackball.
\item
  \textbf{Update Sliders} installs the new firmware to the panel's Controllers boards, including: Grand Master, A/B Xfader, Sliders, Qkeys, Auxkeys, Global Sliders.
\item
  \textbf{Update VDMX} installs the new firmware to the DMX processing board.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{fixture-builder}{%
\section{Fixture Builder}\label{fixture-builder}}

👉 Use the \textbf{Fixture Builder} software for the Vibe's fixture devices importing, building and editing.

This chapter describes the Fixture Builder integrated software that is used for managing the device database and for the creation and editing of the existent fixture devices.

{\textbf{CHAPTER UNDER DEVELOPMENT}}

\textbf{Chapter index:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item

\hypertarget{introduction-2}{%
\subsection{Introduction}\label{introduction-2}}

The Fixture Builder is a Vibe inbuilt software used to manage the Device Database.

Fixture Devices may be created, imported and exported through the Fixture Builder.

Fixture Builder can be launched either from the Vibe Menu or from the OS programs.

It's highly recommended to keep Vibe software closed while the Offline Tool is running.
If launched from Vibe, this operation will be automatically done.

If launching the Fixture Builder from the Vibe, a popup will ask if it has to be opened in Console or in Show mode:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Console Mode - All the devices contained into the console's device database will be listed.
\item
  Show Mode - Only the devices imported into the current Vibe show will be listed.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{device-import}{%
\subsection{Device Import}\label{device-import}}

Tap \{Device Import\} to open the device import popup.

Select the Device to import from the USB stick and press Apply.

When the import is completed, the ``Device Imported Successfully'' popup will be displayed.

\hypertarget{gdtf-import}{%
\subsection{GDTF Import}\label{gdtf-import}}

Tap \{GDTF Import\} to open the GDTF file import popup.

Select the Device to import from the USB stick and press Apply.

When the import is completed, the ``Device Imported Successfully'' popup will be displayed.

GDTF file format is open source, therefore anyone can create files of this type. Because the GDTF files may have errors affecting the conversion process, a popup will suggest to check the generated devices, parameters and steps to ensure the have been correctly matched or to fix any eventual error.
